# Decisions

One line per decision: what, why, what would change it ("revisit if"), and the rough
monthly figure when cost is a dimension. Newest block last. Compliance angle inline.

## 2026-10-29 — plan rev 5 approved (docs/PLAN-2026-10-mature-setup.md)

- System of record = GitHub (Issues, PRs, Actions) for own repos for now; Forgejo deferred to Appendix A — zero infrastructure, same platform as work, the tracker's `Backend` protocol is the seam; GitHub cannot enforce one-label-per-scope, so the CLI enforces it and `label-lint` reports; revisit if: the Appendix A trigger fires (≥1 month of tracker use AND a need for server-enforced labels, own retention/OIDC issuer, or a non-GitHub lab). 0 CHF/month.
- The kit ships as committed repo files (`AGENTS.md`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.github/hooks/guard.json`, `.claude/skills`, `.claude/agents`, `tools/guard`, `tools/tracker`, `.githooks`, workflows, issue forms) installed by `install.sh --into <repo>` from `kit/` + root `agents/` + `skills/`, with the source commit in `.claude/kit-version`; not a Claude plugin, not user scope — the only form both harnesses read, and the policy version is a commit; revisit if: ≥3 repos drift (then the plugin for Claude, `.github-private` at work).
- `guard` is harness-neutral: stdin JSON in both shapes, normalized tool classes, `permissionDecision` at top level and under `hookSpecificOutput`, exit 2 = deny, ≤2 s, no network in PreToolUse — Copilot hook timeouts fail open and its cloud agent maps `ask` to `deny` (docs 2026-10-29); revisit if: either harness changes the contract (fixtures will fail first).
- `AGENTS.md` is the canonical working agreement; `CLAUDE.md` = `@AGENTS.md`; fallback `.github/copilot-instructions.md` = "Read AGENTS.md first" only if the work org has `chat.useAgentsMdFile` off; revisit if: never.
- Agents stay in Claude format under `.claude/agents` (VS Code reads it); Copilot tool identifiers are added in the same frontmatter only if the step-1.6 probe shows Claude names are ignored; revisit if: a harness stops reading the other's format.
- Skills live in `.claude/skills` in task repos (read by Claude Code and Copilot); `.github/skills` is not duplicated; revisit if: a work policy mandates `.github/skills` (then a symlink or the installer's `--skills-dir`).
- Every guard hook ships with allow/ask/deny + crash fixtures from BOTH harnesses run in CI; no LLM judge in PreToolUse (a judge once blocked valid `git -D`/worktree commands); revisit if: never.
- Hook fixtures are captured from real sessions and redacted before commit; no transcript, path outside the repo, or token survives redaction; revisit if: never.
- ask-on-delete.sh stays at user scope until the kit's delete-guard has run a month; revisit if: duplicate prompts become noise.
- Monthly prompt-fatigue review: any hook prompting weekly with zero "no" answers is widened or demoted; revisit if: never.
- Model routing fixed by task class and pinned in agent frontmatter with aliases (Fable plans; Opus code and review; Haiku/Sonnet lookups and workflow verifiers; fleet for take-home-sized mechanical packets; Gemini plans only); at work the picker's model names are substituted per repo — the 91 %-from-generic-subagents run came from inherited models; revisit if: the ledger shows top-tier share above 80 % on mechanical work two months running.
- Reviewer and planner agents have no Edit/Write (asserted by a test); residual gap: Bash heredocs; revisit if: never.
- Main thread edits small planned changes (≤3 files); larger work goes to opus-pinned subagents or fleet; no never-edits hook; revisit if: the spawn log exposes a stable marker and telemetry shows main-thread edits drive cost.
- Fan-out guard = spawn log + PreToolUse on the spawn tool answering `ask` at the 6th spawn and every +10, never deny; revisit if: Workflow agents bypass it.
- Deploy/apply boundary = `ask` on terraform apply/destroy, kubectl apply/rollout/delete, helm install/upgrade, docker push, gh workflow run, gh release, az deployment, aws cloudformation, hcloud server; fleet's verifier is the semantic backstop; revisit if: never.
- Client boundary = `KZ_BOUNDARY=client` in the repo's committed `.claude/guard.json`; the kit carries no client data and nothing from home (memory, telemetry, tokens) is installed at work; revisit if: never.
- Guard-the-guard: `ask` on edits of the kit's own settings, hooks and guard code; revisit if: never.
- Protected branches: GitHub ruleset on `main` with required check `ci` now and 1 approval once the bot identity exists; client-side hook path-scoped and knob-gated; revisit if: Q5 answer differs.
- Bot identity for agent sessions decided at step 2.8: GitHub App (recommended: short-lived tokens, `app[bot]` author) or machine user; until then PRs are authored by Kacper and approvals are 0; revisit if: Q6 is declined.
- Scratch-.md hook starts at `ask`, flips to `deny` after a month without approvals; hook only; revisit if: approvals occur.
- Tracker = GitHub Issues + two label families (`type/*`, `status/*`) driven by a stdlib `tracker` CLI with a `Backend` protocol (GitHub first via `gh`; Forgejo with Appendix A; Jira/ADO on the first non-GitHub work repo) and PreToolUse denies on raw `gh`/API writes; not an MCP server; ten verbs, dogfooded here two weeks before any further verb; exclusivity = add then remove siblings, verified by `label-lint` that never edits labels; revisit if: verb count > 25, the model keeps guessing flags despite `tracker help`, or a client mandates MCP. 0 CHF/month.
- Human-only transitions (ready, unblock, done, drop) enforced by surface absence + no-TTY refusal + PreToolUse deny, and by principal once the bot identity exists; revisit if: Q6 is declined.
- Closing keywords rejected in commits and PR bodies, `Refs #N` only — auto-close would bypass the closing protocol; revisit if: never.
- Issue body frozen once `status/ready`; amendments are comments; revisit if: re-ready becomes a weekly chore.
- No effort figure in the closing record — ALCOA accuracy; revisit if: a client contract requires it (then measured claim→review wall-clock, labelled).
- Commit trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude …` allowed, neither required nor banned; closing-keyword ban and `--no-verify` deny are the enforced parts; revisit if: a client policy forbids AI attribution (Q7).
- Independent review: at home `claude -p` on Opus with a fixed prompt and a receipt per PR (≈$3–6/month); at work Copilot code review steered by the same prompt in `.github/instructions/review.instructions.md`; the human approval is the control in both; `ask` only, never a merge gate; revisit if: monthly cost passes $20 or the work org disables code review.
- Egress screen in front of every `claude -p`, `agy` and future `judge` call; held items listed, exit 3, never dropped; revisit if: never.
- Typed-judgment tier deferred (break-even ≈16 items / 30 k tokens per batch vs 0 issues today); when built: stdlib `judge` over `claude -p --model haiku`, never an MCP server; revisit if: ≥40 open tracked items in one repo, ≥60 memory files in one project, or two ≥30 k-token triage batches in 30 days.
- Local llama-server only as a `judge` backend during fleet batches; revisit if: an item class is classified on-machine-only.
- No other-vendor code-level second opinion; revisit if: Opus review and author disagree twice in a quarter on a non-client diff.
- Memory: a private bare repo on GitHub via GIT_DIR/GIT_WORK_TREE with transcripts excluded BEFORE any index regeneration; deprecate instead of delete; index on demand under 18 KB; push off by default with a slug allowlist; moves to the forge with Appendix A; revisit if: Claude Code ships native memory versioning.
- Cost telemetry = statusline + SessionEnd ledger (Claude Code only, always exit 0, API-equivalent USD); no ccusage, no OTel; Copilot usage is the org's dashboard; revisit if: the parser breaks twice or a client asks for cross-machine usage evidence.
- Queue/groom/audit skills deferred to numeric triggers; default tick is serial; revisit if: a repo regularly has 3+ independent ready issues per week.
- The private app repo stays on GitHub and adopts the tracker through the GitHub adapter (step 3.8); revisit if: it ever migrates to the forge (own task-intake).
- Work pilot = one work repo, kit only, `KZ_BOUNDARY=client`, findings recorded in that repo's DECISIONS.md; revisit if: the org forbids repo-level hooks (then `AGENTS.md` + instructions only, recorded as the reduced control set).
- Appendix A (Forgejo on VPS or `forge-vm`, pierun as Proxmox host with `runner-vm`, R2 dumps + restore drills, no UPS purchase, forge-hosted memory and registry) deferred as one block with the trigger above; the pierun reinstall keeps its own plan in `arch-cluster`; the mini PC stays deferred (trigger: pierun availability <99 %/quarter, RAM <16 GB, or CI beyond one runner VM). ≈CHF 4–5/month when it lands.
- icm-architect not adopted (state-in-folders contradicts tracker-as-record and the scratch-.md hook); walk test and any-harness packaging borrowed; revisit if: a validation-evidence pack needs a quote-checked knowledge bundle (then trial it there once).

## 2026-10-29 — step 1.2: kit skeleton, guard engine, fixtures

- No separate `kit/` tree: the repo root IS the kit and `kit.manifest` lists what `install.sh --into` copies (init-only vs managed vs mapped dirs), so the files dogfooded here and the files shipped are the same bytes; deviation from plan §8 recorded; revisit if: a shipped file must differ from the dogfooded one (then a template layer for that file only).
- `guard` never emits `allow`: it stays silent and lets the harness's own permission flow run — an explicit `allow` would bypass that flow in Claude Code; only `ask` and `deny` are ever written; revisit if: a harness requires an explicit allow to proceed.
- Deny = JSON on stdout + reason on stderr + exit 2, which both harnesses read as deny; `ask` = JSON + exit 0; guard failure = exit 1 with nothing on stdout, which Claude Code treats as non-blocking and Copilot's preToolUse as deny (documented asymmetry, crash fixtures assert the exit code); revisit if: either harness changes its exit-code contract.
- One Copilot path: `.claude/settings.json` carries `--wrapper claude` and `.github/hooks/guard.json` carries `--wrapper github`; a Copilot-shaped event arriving through the claude wrapper is a no-op, so VS Code (which reads both files) prompts once; revisit if: VS Code stops reading `.github/hooks`.
- Claude Code hook shapes were captured from a real probe session on 2026-10-29 (18 event/tool combinations, redacted, path-normalized, under `tests/fixtures/_shapes/claude`); Copilot shapes are synthesized from the GitHub/VS Code docs and marked so in `_fixture.source` until step 1.3 captures them on the work machine; revisit if: a Copilot capture contradicts a synthesized shape (then the fixture is replaced and the normalizer fixed).
- Subagent marker exists: tool calls made inside a Claude Code subagent carry `agent_id` and `agent_type` in the hook input (SubagentStart/Stop carry them too) — the "main thread never edits" convention can become a hook once telemetry shows it is worth it; revisit if: the step-3.6 ledger shows main-thread edits drive cost.
- `guard.py` is a single stdlib file (Python ≥ 3.9, no third-party imports) so vendoring is one copy and the hook command is one line; split when it passes ~1500 lines; CI runs 3.9 and 3.13; revisit if: a work machine ships an older Python (then a shim).
- Hook budget 5 s in both wrappers, no network in PreToolUse, telemetry writes best-effort — Copilot timeouts fail open, so a slow guard is no guard; revisit if: a rule needs a network call (then it moves to CI).
- A nested `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions` probe is not used for fixture capture (blocked by the auto-mode classifier as an unsafe agent, and rightly so); captures come from a normal-permission `claude -p` run in a scratch directory, where PreToolUse fires before the permission check; revisit if: never.
- In this repo `.claude/agents` and `.claude/skills` are symlinks to the root `agents/` and `skills/` (one copy, dogfooded by both harnesses); task repos get real copies through the manifest's `map` lines; revisit if: a harness refuses to follow the symlinks.

## 2026-10-29 — step 1.4: boundary rules in the guard

- Secret-bearing files (`.env*` except `*.example|sample|template|dist`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12/pfx/jks`, `*.tfstate*`, `id_*` private keys, `~/.aws/credentials`, `~/.kube/config`, `~/.config/gh/hosts.yml`, `~/.docker/config.json`, `.netrc`, `.pypirc`, `.git-credentials`): Write/Edit → deny, Read → ask, in both harnesses; shell spellings covered (redirects, `tee`, `cp/mv` targets, `sed -i`, `git add` → deny; `cat/jq/grep/source/…` → ask); revisit if: a legitimate workflow writes one of these weekly (then an allowlist knob, never a blanket off).
- `*.tfvars` Write/Edit → ask, not deny (deviation from plan step 1.4): tfvars are daily Terraform work and usually hold sizes and names, not secrets; the prompt asks to confirm no secret values; reads are not judged; revisit if: a tfvars with a real secret is ever committed (then deny + a `.tfvars.example` rule).
- Here-document bodies are data, not commands: `secret-shell`, `git-guard`, `deploy-ask` and `egress-guard` strip them before judging (the delete-guard keeps scanning them, per the owner rule) — found by dogfood: the guard went live in the session that wrote it and denied a commit whose message quoted `echo X=1 > .env`; revisit if: a heredoc is used to smuggle a write past the guard (then the body is scanned for redirect targets only).
- git-guard: deny force pushes (`-f`, `--force`, `--force-with-lease[=…]`, `--force-if-includes`, `+refspec`, `--all`, `--mirror`), pushes whose destination is a protected branch (explicit refspec, `HEAD:main`, or a bare `git push` while the checked-out branch is protected, resolved with `git rev-parse` in the command's cwd, 3 s budget), and `--no-verify` (`commit -n`); protected set from `.claude/guard.json` (`main` by default, `KZ_PROTECTED_BRANCHES` overrides); `gh pr merge` is the way onto `main`; revisit if: a repo needs a release branch (then add it to the set).
- deploy-ask: ask on terraform/tofu apply|destroy|import|taint|state, kubectl apply|create|delete|rollout|drain|scale|patch|exec, helm install|upgrade|uninstall|rollback, docker push, gh workflow run, gh release, az deployment / az … create|delete, CloudFormation deploy|*-stack, aws s3 rm|rb|sync --delete, hcloud server lifecycle, pvesh/qm, pulumi up|destroy, cdk deploy|destroy, ansible-playbook, flux uninstall|suspend; `--dry-run`, `--check`, `--diff`, plan/validate/template/lint/diff never prompt; revisit if: a prompt fires weekly with zero "no" answers (prompt-fatigue review).
- egress-guard is the minimal client-boundary control until the egress screen (step 3.2): with `boundary: client` in `.claude/guard.json` or `KZ_BOUNDARY=client`, other-vendor LLM CLIs (`agy`, `gemini`, `codex`, `llm`, `openai`, `ollama`, `aider`) and curl/wget to LLM API hosts → ask; everything else unchanged; revisit if: step 3.2 lands (then this rule delegates to the screen).
- The Claude-Code-only `permissions` block in `.claude/settings.json` duplicates a subset of the rules (deny writes to `.env*`/pem/key/tfstate, force push, `--no-verify`; ask on tfvars writes and secret reads) as a belt in case the hook is slow or disabled; Copilot ignores the key; revisit if: the two drift (the hook is the source of truth).
- Project hooks are NOT snapshotted at session start: a hook added to `.claude/settings.json` fired in the same Claude Code session that wrote it (observed 2026-10-29); the rev 4 "hook snapshotting" probe is thereby answered; revisit if: a later Claude Code version changes it.
- Fixture corpus is generated: `tools/guard/tests/make_fixtures.py` holds the case tables and writes `tests/fixtures/<rule>/<harness>/<decision>/`; a test fails when the files on disk differ from the generator, so cases are edited in one place; revisit if: never.
- `claude -p --model fable` works (model id `claude-fable-5-1` in `modelUsage`, verified 2026-10-29), so agent frontmatter can pin `fable` by alias; revisit if: the alias disappears.

## 2026-10-29 — step 1.5: scratch-markdown guard

- A NEW `.md` written inside a git tree outside the documented homes (`docs/`, `.claude/`, `.github/`, `skills/`, `agents/`, `.agents/`, `evidence/`, the root `README/DECISIONS/AGENTS/CLAUDE/CHANGELOG/CONTRIBUTING/SECURITY/LICENSE.md`, any `README.md`, `SKILL.md`, `AGENTS.md`) → `ask`, in both harnesses; existing files, non-markdown, edits and paths outside a repo are never judged; `scratch_md_mode` in `.claude/guard.json` (`ask` → `deny` after a month without approvals, `off` for a repo that wants none; `KZ_SCRATCH_MD` overrides); revisit if: a repo keeps approving the prompt (then extend `scratch_md_allow` there, not the default).

## 2026-10-29 — step 1.6: model routing in agent frontmatter

- Every agent pins a tier: the six engineers, `git-surgeon`, `infra-reviewer`, `gxp-compliance-reviewer` → `opus` (code and review, per the AGENTS.md routing table); `platform-planner`, `rag-infra-advisor` → `fable` (design); `code-reviewer` stays `opus`; nobody on `sonnet`/`haiku` because no agent here is a plain lookup. Cost: on the Claude subscription this is quota, not money (€0 marginal); on API pricing Fable is the most expensive tier, so the pin keeps it to the two design agents. `tools/tests/test_agents.py` enforces the pin and that reviewer/planner/advisor agents list no write tools (validation evidence for "advisory agents cannot change the artifact they judge"); revisit if: a lookup-only agent appears (then `haiku`), or VS Code refuses an alias (Kacper's picker probe at work).

## 2026-10-29 — steps 2.2 + 2.3: protected-branch and fan-out rules

- `protected-branch` is path-scoped: the checkout that owns the *file* decides (walk up to its `.git`, then `git symbolic-ref`), not the session's cwd, so editing `~/Documents/TechVault/x.md` from a repo on `main` passes and editing a file of a repo on `main` from anywhere is denied. `git commit` is denied when the repo it runs in (cwd or `git -C <dir>`) is on a protected branch; `merge`/`cherry-pick`/`rebase` are not covered on purpose (`git merge --ff-only` is how `main` is synced; the push guard catches the rest). Files under `.git/` are never judged. The protected set is `protected_branches` in `.claude/guard.json` (default `main`, same set as git-guard and the GitHub ruleset of step 2.8). The private app repo keeps its inline hook until step 3.8 (its settings run live in Kacper's other sessions; swapping is done together with the tracker adapter there). Revisit if: a repo needs per-path exemptions (then a `protected_allow` list, not a looser default).
- `fan-out` never denies: it appends `{ts, event, harness, session, agent, model, agent_id, cwd}` to `<telemetry_dir>/spawns.jsonl` on every spawn (`PreToolUse` on `Agent`/`Task`/`task`, and `SubagentStart` in Claude Code) and returns `ask` at the 6th `PreToolUse` spawn of a session and every 10th after (6, 16, 26, …), with a note when `127.0.0.1:8012` answers (llama-server, 39 GB). Counting uses the harness `session_id`, so a long session is one budget; the log is scanned on each spawn (fine below ~10⁴ lines; rotate monthly if it grows). The Copilot `.github/hooks/guard.json` gets no `SubagentStart` entry: VS Code does not list that event, so the `task` tool's `PreToolUse` is the only Copilot signal (counts spawns, logs no model). Cost angle: the prompt is the hook-level version of the "<=5 agents, brief first" guardrail in `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`; it costs nothing when nobody fans out. Revisit if: a legitimate workflow needs >5 agents routinely (then raise `FANOUT_FIRST` in `.claude/guard.json`, which is not a knob yet on purpose).

## 2026-10-29 — step 2.1: guard-the-guard

- `guard-config` returns `ask` on every Edit/Write of hook configuration: `.claude/settings*.json`, `.claude/guard.json`, `.github/hooks/**`, `tools/guard/**` in any repo, and `~/.claude/settings*.json`, `~/.claude/hooks/**` for Claude Code. Classification is by path components, so it needs no git tree and works for nested repos. Known gap, on purpose for now: shell writers (`sed -i`, redirects, `cp` onto these paths) are not scanned, because `secret-shell` already shows how and the Edit/Write path is what an agent reaches for first; add the scan when a real evasion shows up in the captures. Compliance angle: the hook set is the automated control, so changes to it get the same human gate as a deploy (audit trail = the prompt plus the commit plus this file). Revisit if: the prompt becomes noise in this repo, which develops the guard (then narrow `tools/guard/**` to `guard.py`, never drop the config paths).

## 2026-10-29 — step 2.4: tracker data model on GitHub Issues

- Label families `type/*` (5) and `status/*` (7) and the transition table live as data in `tools/tracker/tracker.py`; every triple outside the table raises (`tools/tracker/tests/test_model.py`). Exclusivity is the CLI's job (add the new status, then remove the siblings) and `label-lint` only reports: one comment per issue and finding set (a fingerprint in a hidden marker keeps it idempotent), one tracking issue kept current, labels never touched, exit 0 so a label mistake never turns an issue-event run red. Events extended beyond the plan (`closed`, `reopened` added) so "closed without done" is caught at once, not nightly. Issue forms `task.yml` and `bug.yml` carry the definition-of-ready fields and blank issues are off. The "body frozen after ready" record is a sha of the body with line endings and the state of acceptance boxes normalised away, recorded by `owner ready` and re-checked by `owner done`, so the owner can tick boxes without breaking the freeze. Cost: GitHub Issues are free; the nightly `label-lint` run is about a minute of Actions time per day, inside the free tier (€0/month). Compliance angle: the issue is the change record, the transition table is the approval matrix, and every move leaves a comment with who, when and on which branch. Revisit if: a repo needs more status values (then a per-repo extension of the table, not a free-text status).

## 2026-10-29 — step 2.5: `tracker` CLI, first cut

- Stdlib CLI with ten verbs plus `owner labels-sync`; `Backend` protocol with `FakeBackend` (tests) and `GitHubBackend` through `gh`, so its auth is reused and nothing new is stored. The `urllib + GITHUB_TOKEN` fallback named in the plan is deferred: there is no consumer without `gh` (Actions runners ship it), and a second transport doubles the surface. Owner verbs refuse when there is no TTY on both ends or `CLAUDECODE` is set (probe 2026-10-29: inside Claude's Bash both are false/set); the actor is `human` only at a terminal. One in-progress per actor is read from the claim marker (`by`). Markers are HTML comments on the first line of a comment: invisible in the UI, parseable by the CLI, and they survive edits of the human text below. `owner done` treats `skipped` checks as green, treats `*[bot]` logins and `KZ_TRACKER_BOTS` as non-human mergers, and notes when the PR touched `DECISIONS.md`. The GitHub contract test runs only with `KZ_TRACKER_TEST_REPO=<owner>/<scratch-repo>` set; creating that scratch repo from this session was refused by the harness's own classifier, so the first run is Kacper's. `ForgejoBackend` arrives behind the same contract test with Appendix A. Revisit if: a second consumer of the tracker appears (then the verb set grows from real use, not from the plan).

## 2026-10-29 — step 2.6: tracker-guard

- Denies the shell paths around the tracker: `gh issue create|edit|comment|close|reopen|delete|transfer|pin|unpin|lock|unlock|develop`, `gh label create|edit|delete|clone`, `gh api` with a mutating method or field flag on `…/issues` or `…/labels`, `curl|wget|http|https|xh` with a mutating method or body to those endpoints, and `tracker owner …` from an agent (the CLI refuses it too; the guard fires before the process runs, so no partial output). Reads stay silent (`gh issue view|list|status`, `gh label list`, GET `gh api`). Temporary allow: `gh issue create --label agent-loop|handoff` until step 3.8 moves the private app repo's loop onto the tracker. The reason names the replacement verb. Revisit if: `gh` grows a new mutating subcommand (the deny list is explicit on purpose, not a prefix match).

## 2026-10-29 — step 2.7: wiring the tracker into the skills and the session start

- `pr-prep` gains the tracker link (acceptance criteria copied into the PR checklist, `Refs #N` in the body, `tracker review N --pr <url>` after `gh pr create`, the /verify summary as `tracker note`) and `task-intake` gains step 7 (decompose the approved plan into `tracker new` issues, left in backlog for the owner). Both are conditional on `tools/tracker` being present, so the skills still work in a repo without the kit. The session-start digest is one `gh issue list` call filtered locally (handoffs, blocked, in-progress, ready; at most ten lines, four titles per line), three-second alarm, silent on any failure: a session must start even offline. Wired as `SessionStart` in `.claude/settings.json` and `.github/hooks/guard.json` (Copilot lists that event). The memory digest named in the plan joins it at step 3.4. Revisit if: the digest ever delays a session start noticeably (then cache the last answer in the telemetry dir).

## 2026-10-29 — step 2.8: dogfood, first half (ruleset); issues wait for the owner

- GitHub ruleset `main: PR + green ci, no force, no delete` (id 26124070) is active on the default branch: changes reach `main` only through a pull request, the `ci` check must be green on the head, no force pushes, no deletion; zero required approvals for now and no bypass actors (the owner uses PRs too). Approvals go 0 → 1 the day a bot identity exists (Q6, still open: the default proposal is a GitHub App installed on this repo, so a bot-authored PR shows the human approval as a separate principal; a classic PAT is the fallback, not the choice). Hook-level git-guard and the server-side ruleset now say the same thing, which is the point: the hook catches it early, the ruleset catches every path, including a push from another machine. The dogfood issues (remaining plan steps as `tracker new`) wait for `tracker owner labels-sync` in this repo, an owner verb by design. Cost: €0 (rulesets are free on public repos). Compliance angle: the ruleset is the technical control behind "one topic, one PR, evidence attached"; its JSON lives in the GitHub API and an export belongs in the evidence set (step 3.7). Revisit if: the owner needs to hot-fix `main` under time pressure (then a bypass actor for the owner, logged by GitHub, rather than disabling the rule).

## 2026-10-29 — step 3.1: commit-msg gate

- One stdlib script, three entry points: the `commit-msg` hook (`.githooks/commit-msg`, activated by `core.hooksPath` which the installer sets in git targets and this clone sets by hand), the CI job `commit-msg` on the PR range, and `--message` for tests. Rules: `type(scope): subject` with eleven types, subject ≤72, lower-case start, no trailing period, a blocklist of past/gerund first words as the "imperative" check (a grammar check is not worth a dependency), no closing keyword anywhere (`Fixes #N` closes behind the tracker; `Refs #N` is the form), trailers only in the last block. Merge/Revert/fixup!/squash! pass. `--no-verify` is already denied by git-guard, which is the "commit-gate" the plan named. `core.hooksPath` hides any hooks in `.git/hooks` of that clone; recorded here because it surprises. Cost: €0. Compliance angle: the commit message is the first line of the change record; the gate makes its shape a tested rule, and CI re-checking means a hook skipped on a laptop still fails the PR. Revisit if: a repo needs a ticket key in the subject (then a per-repo regex, not a global one).

## 2026-10-29 — step 3.2: egress screen

- `tools/egress/egress_screen.py` holds, never drops: secret patterns (the fleet gate's set plus age keys, JWTs, Azure account keys, connection strings and a conservative `key = value` assignment pattern), gitleaks when installed (`gitleaks stdin --exit-code 9`; absent or failing gitleaks falls back to the own patterns, never to "send"), client markers as regexes from `.claude/guard.json` (`client_markers`, empty by default), a 200 KB size cap, and the client boundary (`boundary: client` or `KZ_BOUNDARY=client` holds every target not in `egress_allowed_targets`, default claude + copilot). `--pass` is all-or-nothing: a batch with one held item emits nothing. Found while dogfooding: the screen held this very PR's diff twice (test literals that looked like a private key and an API key), so the tests now build such strings at runtime; a screen that cannot be argued with is the point. Wired into `gemini-review` step 1 (user-scope skill, absolute path) and into `fresh-review`. Cost: €0. Revisit if: false positives from the assignment pattern exceed one a week (then tighten the value charset, keep the rule).

## 2026-10-29 — step 3.3: fresh-context review with a receipt

- `bin/fresh-review` → `tools/review/fresh_review.py`: `git diff <base>...HEAD` through the egress screen, then the version-controlled prompt (`tools/review/prompt.md`) plus the diff to `claude -p --model opus --output-format json --tools "" --json-schema …` (probed 2026-10-29: `structured_output` carries the verdict, `modelUsage` the model and cost; the CLI has no `--max-turns` any more, `--tools ""` plus the schema keeps it to one answer). Receipt `.git/fresh-review/<head-sha>.json` with sha, base, model(s), cost, prompt and diff hashes, verdict and findings; exit 0 APPROVE/COMMENT, 1 BLOCKER, 3 held, 2 error; advisory, never a hook, never on merge. At work the same prompt is `.github/instructions/review.instructions.md` (a test keeps the two identical) and Copilot code review plus the human approval are the control; no API key in a client's CI. Cost: an Opus review of a typical PR diff is cents to about a dollar; the receipt records the exact figure, and the monthly sum is read from the receipts (plan estimate ≈$3–6/month holds for a PR a day). Compliance angle: the receipt is the review evidence (who reviewed = which model, what exactly = diff hash, with which instructions = prompt hash). Revisit if: receipts are skipped twice in a month (then the PR-creation ask-gate from the deferred list).
- First dogfood of `fresh-review` on its own PR (#6): two rounds, $0.38 and $1.04, COMMENT both times, seven and six findings; every MAJOR was real (a skipped CI job counted as green, the installer overwriting `core.hooksPath` and hiding live `.git/hooks`, the egress screen failing open on a broken or wrong-typed `guard.json` and on a downgraded `KZ_BOUNDARY`, receipts overwritable, `git commit -v` scissors) and is fixed in the same PR. `.github/instructions/review.instructions.md` keeps `applyTo: "**"` with an in-text scope ("apply when asked to review; ignore while writing code") because Copilot's per-agent scoping fields are not verifiable from here; Kacper checks at work whether code review picks it up and whether chat ignores it (Q-B list). Revisit if: the work probe shows chat obeying the reviewer persona (then scope with the mechanism VS Code offers that day).


## 2026-10-29 — step 3.4: memory under git

- `tools/memory/memstore.py`: one bare repository `~/.claude/memory-store.git` whose work tree is `~/.claude/projects`, with `info/exclude` letting only `*/memory/**` in (transcripts, tool results and scratch never enter it). Chosen over per-project repos (the memory dir is not a repo and Claude Code owns the directory layout) and over copying files elsewhere (a copy drifts). Every memory write through Write/Edit becomes one commit via the PostToolUse hook (`memstore.py hook`), message `memory(<slug>): add|update|delete <name>`; a deletion made with a shell command is picked up by the next commit for that project (the hook stages the whole `<slug>/memory` directory, so such a commit may carry `+N more`). Push is off: `push` needs a remote `origin` and `KZ_MEMORY_PUSH=<slug,...>|*`, and refuses when unpushed commits touch a project outside the allowlist (the first push counts every commit). Cost: $0 (private GitHub repo, local git). Compliance angle: memory is a record the agent writes about the work; git gives it history, authorship and diffs, and the allowlist keeps client-project memory off GitHub. The kit ships `memstore.py`, `ledger.py` and `prices.json` as managed files only so the shared `.claude/settings.json` template resolves in every install; both hooks exit 0 on a Copilot payload (the guard's `timestamp`/camelCase tell) and on any path outside `~/.claude/projects`, so at work they do nothing (see `docs/USING-AT-WORK.md`). Revisit if: memory moves into a Claude Code built-in sync (then the hook becomes a no-op) or a project needs memory shared across machines (then a per-slug remote).
- Wiring: this repo's `.claude/settings.json` runs the hook and a SessionStart digest (`memstore.py digest`, ≤ 8 lines, ≤ 600 bytes) for its own sessions; the user-scope lines for `~/.claude/settings.json` are the owner's to add (guard-the-guard rule, step 2.1). Remote creation (a private GitHub repo) is the owner's too.

## 2026-10-29 — step 3.5: memory schema, validator, index

- Schema = the existing frontmatter plus optional `metadata.status` (`active` default | `deprecated` | `superseded` + `superseded_by`), `stale_after: YYYY-MM-DD` (required for `project` and `reference`, the two types that age), `verified: true` + `verified_on`. No `trap` field: a memory that would mislead gets `status: deprecated` with the reason in its body, which costs no new vocabulary. The validator (`memstore.py validate`, also run by the hook with exit 2 = feedback to the agent) warns and never blocks: the file is committed first, so a half-written memory is a visible commit, not a lost one. Lenient on purpose: ~90 existing files lack `stale_after`; `triage` lists them and the index and digest keep working. Compliance angle: `stale_after` plus `verified_on` make "when was this last true" answerable, which the plan's ALCOA+ line asks of every record the agent relies on. Revisit if: warnings get ignored for a month (then `stale_after` becomes a deny for new files of those types).
- `memstore.py index --write` regenerates `MEMORY.md` from frontmatter (active files only, sorted by type then name, ≤ 18 KB with an explicit "N more not indexed" line) instead of the hand-kept list; the cap exists because Claude Code loads the index whole and truncates it silently past ~25 KB. `deprecate <file> [--by slug]` edits the `metadata:` block in place and commits. Revisit if: the index needs grouping by type (then headings, same cap).

## 2026-10-29 — step 3.6: session ledger

- `tools/telemetry/ledger.py hook` on SessionEnd reads the session transcript and its subagent transcripts (`<project>/<session>/subagents/*.jsonl`), counts one usage per `requestId` (streamed duplicates otherwise double the numbers), joins the spawn log from step 2.3, prices with `tools/telemetry/prices.json` (USD per million tokens, dated, `null` for tiers whose list price is not in the file: the row then shows tokens and `n/a` rather than a guess) and appends one line to `~/.claude/telemetry/sessions-YYYY-MM.jsonl`. Always exit 0; problems become `parse_errors` in the line. `ledger.py cost --days 7` prints per project and model: sessions, tokens, output, cost, spawn count, sessions whose subagent share exceeds 70 % (the fan-out smell), and hook prompts per rule from the new `decisions.jsonl` (guard 0.4.1 logs every ask/deny as rule names + tool, no command text) so prompt fatigue is measured before thresholds move. Claude Code only; at work the org's Copilot dashboard is the ledger. Cost: $0, local files. Compliance angle: none required (own telemetry), but the same shape is what a client would ask for as "which model touched this change": the fresh-review receipt already records that per PR. Revisit if: Anthropic's own usage export covers subagents and projects (then the hook keeps only the spawn join).
- Hook commands in `.claude/settings.json` are `python3 "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}"/tools/...` from now on. Found 2026-10-29 while dogfooding: Claude Code runs hook commands in the Bash tool's persisted working directory, so after a `cd` into a subfolder the relative `tools/guard/guard.py` no longer resolved, python3 exited 2 and the harness read that as a deny for every hooked tool; the session had to be recovered through a worktree switch. Claude Code sets `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` for hooks; Copilot does not, and there the fallback `.` is the workspace root (`.github/hooks/guard.json` stays relative for the same reason). Hooks are snapshotted at session start, so a running session keeps the old form until restarted. Revisit if: Copilot starts running hooks from a different directory (then the same variable form with its own name).

## 2026-10-30 — step 3.7: test-guard, image-tag scanner, evidence export

- Test-guard = the harness kit's `check-test-tampering.sh` copied into `tools/testguard/` (source: the private harness kit, commit 4dd6956e, harness v1), not referenced: the kit is private and task repos get the file through `kit.manifest`. The copy adds pytest conventions (deleted or renamed `test_*.py` / `*_test.py`, added `pytest.mark.skip` / `xfail`, `pytest.skip(`, `unittest.skip`; `skipif` / `skipIf` stay allowed because a conditional skip is visible by design) and a check for a `def test_*` that disappears without coming back under the same name (a move between files passes, a rename needs the label; names make the finding nameable). `--worktree` compares the merge-base with the working tree and never calls gh, so the PostToolUse wrapper (`testguard.py`, runs only when the written path or the command names a test file) sees an uncommitted deletion within the second and no hook touches the network. The escape hatch is the kit's `test-removal-ok` PR label; it does not exist in this repo yet (`gh label create` is an owner action), so until the owner creates it every removal fails CI, which is the right default. Upstreaming the pytest rules into the harness kit is the owner's call. Found while dogfooding: the guard flagged its own test file (marker strings as test data), so those are built at runtime like the egress tests; the wrapper was first named `test_guard.py`, which pytest collects and the guard scans, hence `testguard.py`; and Claude Code ran the new project PostToolUse hook in the running session without a restart (the 3.6 note about hooks being snapshotted at session start is not universal). Cost: €0. Compliance angle: "the suite was not weakened" becomes a CI check with a human-visible exception (the label on the PR is the approval record). Revisit if: test renames become routine (then per-file counts), or a repo's trunk is not `main` (then `BASE_REF` in that workflow's env).
- PostToolUse feedback contract, verified in both docs 2026-10-29: Claude Code honours exit 0 + JSON `decision: block` with `reason` (exit 2 shows stderr, but Copilot documents no exit-2 semantics for postToolUse); Copilot honours exit 0 + top-level `additionalContext`, camelCase whatever the input format. Both new hooks emit the shape matching the harness tell (`timestamp` / camelCase = Copilot) and exit 0 always: the tool already ran, and a guard that cannot run must stay silent rather than nag. Revisit if: either harness documents a blocking PostToolUse (fixtures first).
- `latest-tag` is an own stdlib scanner, not hadolint or semgrep: three file kinds by name (Dockerfiles, compose files, GitHub workflows), `FROM` and `image:` / `uses: docker://` lines; `:latest` and an untagged reference both count because an untagged reference is what the registry resolves to `latest`; digests, build-stage names and `scratch` pass; `${VAR}` references are left to the variable's owner (gxp-check's grep covers pinning). The hook judges only the file just written; `scan` walks `git ls-files` for CI and reads a named file whatever its name, so a k8s manifest can be checked on demand without widening the hook. Not diff-aware on purpose: a floating tag is wrong wherever it is, and this repo and the kit's target repos start clean; a brownfield repo passes paths until it is. Cost: €0. Compliance angle: the tag is the identity of what was validated (Traceable, Enduring); a floating tag makes the validation evidence point at nothing. Revisit if: a brownfield repo needs `--base` like the test guard, or the kinds grow (Helm values, k8s manifests: then by content, not name).
- CI job `guards` (full-depth checkout) runs both and feeds the `ci` aggregate, so the ruleset's required check covers it. Labels come from the event payload (`PR_LABELS`, fallback `none`) and the title through `env`, not inline: no token in the job (a gh lookup would need `pull-requests: read`, and an unlabeled PR must fail, not warn), no script injection. On a push to `main` the guard has nothing on top of the base and exits 0; the scan still runs. Revisit if: the job ever exceeds a minute (then cache nothing, split the scan).
- Ledger writes one line per session id: `append_entry` takes `flock` on the month file itself (same inode, no rename: with a rename the second hook could append to an unlinked file), drops an earlier line for the same session and appends the new one, so the project and user hooks of one SessionEnd leave one line and a resumed session carries cumulative figures (the last end wins); `load_sessions` keeps one row per session across month files for the no-`fcntl` case. The trigger was the user-scope `ledger.py hook` line from step 3.6, still the owner's to add. Cost: €0. Revisit if: a session id is ever reused across machines (then key by session + host).
- Evidence export lives next to the tracker (`tools/tracker/evidence_export.py`): it reads the tracker's markers and reuses `GitHubBackend` for `gh api`, read-only (tracker-guard allows GET). `capture` keeps a check's merged output verbatim and records sha256, size, exit code and the entry-point tool's `--version` (for `uv run …` that is uv's version: known gap), exiting with the check's own code. `export` takes git refs: a PR is in the release when its merge commit is in `rev-list FROM..TO` (exact for merge, squash and rebase merges); the window from the refs' commit dates only bounds the API paging (by hand with a stop on `updated_at`, `--paginate` would fetch every closed PR) and the "issues closed in the window" set; issues referenced by `Refs #N` are included whatever their state; rulesets are exported whole (asked for in 2.8); captures are re-hashed with `intact`. First real run, committed as `evidence/2026-10-29-mature-setup-steps-1-3.6/change-records.json`: 7 PRs over 36 commits, every merge SHA with a green `ci`, 0 tracker issues (PRs #1–#7 predate the tracker), 0 reviews (approvals start with Q6); `SUMMARY.md` and captured outputs arrive with the first release that runs `validation-evidence` end to end. Cost: €0 (API reads inside gh's auth). Compliance angle: this is the closed change record the plan asked for — who promoted (ready marker), who executed (claim), what merged (merge SHA), what checked it (check runs), under which rule (ruleset) — and the hashes let a reviewer verify that captured outputs were not edited afterwards. Revisit if: a repo has over a thousand closed PRs updated after the range start (then the merged-date search endpoint), or the pack needs signing (then an attestation over the JSON).
- First `fresh-review` on this PR (#11): COMMENT, 8 findings, $1.37 (the $0.5–1 estimate was for a smaller diff; this one is about 2k lines with tests). Both MAJORs were real and are fixed in the PR: CI read PR labels from the original event payload, so applying `test-removal-ok` would not have turned `guards` green without a push (`pull_request` now also runs on `labeled` / `unlabeled`); and a Copilot CLI `toolArgs` sent as a JSON string silenced both hooks (parsed now; `guard.py`'s `Event` has the same gap, outside this issue's scope limit, to take with step 3.8). MINORs fixed: untracked test files invisible to the hook (a diff from nothing per untracked test file), `FROM nginx AS nginx` passing as a stage, the scalar `container: ubuntu` form, a missing named path scanning green, the in-place ledger rewrite (now a temp file and `os.replace` under `.ledger.lock`, foreign bytes kept), and single-page fetches of comments, reviews and check runs in the export. Revisit if: a review round ever exceeds $2 (then split the PR).

## 2026-11-01 — rename to agent-change-control, private names out

- The repo is `agent-change-control` (was `claude-platform-setup`): the kit is harness-neutral and a Claude-branded name read as a Claude tool, which matters most when a work colleague clones it for the Copilot pilot. GitHub redirects the old URL; the installer records whatever `origin` says in `kit-version`, so clones update their remote once; the local folder and the Claude Code project folder (memory, transcripts) move together, by the owner. The user-scope installer merges a legacy `claude-platform-setup.manifest` into the new one on install and uninstalls both when both exist (`tools/tests/test_install.py` covers the three paths); the evidence schema id is now `agent-change-control/change-records/1` (the pack under `evidence/` keeps its old id as a dated record). Cost: €0. Compliance angle: the `kit-version` line in every task repo is the traceability link back to this repo; it changes once, now, before any pilot records it. Revisit if: the kit is split out of this repo (then the kit keeps the name and this repo becomes the dogfood site).
- Private repository names stay out of this public repo: files, issue bodies and PR bodies say "the private app repo", "the private harness kit" or "a private bare repo" instead of a name. Found 2026-11-01 (owner): an open issue, 15 doc lines and one code comment named one private repo; eight more lines named three others. Merged history and closed issue or PR bodies keep what they had; editing those is the owner's call, and history is never rewritten. Compliance angle: this repo is the sanitised public mirror; a private name is a pointer a reader cannot follow and reveals what else exists. Revisit if: a private repo goes public (its name may return).
- The `test-removal-ok` label exists since 2026-10-30 (owner-created, colour `#b60205`, described as the test-guard escape hatch); the step 3.7 line above predates it, so `guards` now has its human-applied exception path. Revisit if: never.
- `fresh-review` on this PR: COMMENT, 4 findings, $0.64. MAJOR fixed: the installer's legacy-manifest paths had no test; `tools/tests/test_install.py` now runs `install.sh` against a temporary `CLAUDE_DIR` for legacy-only uninstall, reinstall over a legacy install (one manifest, legacy entries kept) and both manifests present at uninstall (both removed), which also answers the MINOR about a stale legacy file. MINOR fixed: the label line above; the home forge hostname left the plan text. Not changed: `arch-cluster` is a public repo; `pierun` is a machine nickname, not an address. Round 2 after the fixes: COMMENT, 1 MINOR, $1.05: manifest entries were trusted on uninstall and now travel into the merged manifest, so an absolute or `..` entry could reach outside `CLAUDE_DIR`; both paths skip such entries with a warning, with a test. No third round. Revisit if: a review round exceeds $2 (then split the PR).

## 2026-11-01 — Azure DevOps port, steps A1–A8 (docs/PLAN-2026-11-ado-port.md), verified offline

- Azure DevOps (ADO) support is additive: `tools/tracker/ado.py` (`AdoBackend`) behind the existing `Backend` seam, chosen by `backend_for()` (env `KZ_TRACKER_BACKEND`, else the origin host, else GitHub) in `tracker`, `label_lint` and `session_start`; the GitHub path and its tests are untouched. The trigger from the mature-setup plan ("the first work repo that is not on GitHub") fired on 2026-11-01. Verified offline only, by the owner's instruction ("implement it, now ... without me creating scratch ado"): the contract suite runs against an in-memory fake of the ADO REST API (`tools/tracker/tests/fake_ado.py`, which answers 400 to any call the backend was not written for) in both status modes with no xfail; the live run is the same test gated by `KZ_TRACKER_TEST_ADO=org/project/repo` and waits for a scratch organisation (#16, Q-ADO-4). Compliance angle: the fake encodes the documented request and response shapes, so a divergence of the real service shows up as a failing live contract run, not as silent drift. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: a third forge appears (the factory grows one line) or the live run contradicts the fake (the fake changes, never the test).
- ADO transport = stdlib `urllib`, REST api-version 7.1, bearer token from `SYSTEM_ACCESSTOKEN` (pipelines; mapped explicitly) else `az account get-access-token --resource 499b84ac-1321-427f-aa17-267ca6975798`; no PAT code path, no token cache, `az` never reads or writes work items or PRs (its extension cannot read comments or PR labels and starts too slowly for the 2–3 s hook budgets). Compliance angle: every write is attributable to an Entra identity and nothing long-lived sits on disk. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: the SessionStart digest never appears on ADO (then a 0600 cache deleted on expiry) or an Azure DevOps Server client has no Entra (then a PAT from the vault through env, guarded, and its own plan).
- Status model on ADO: tags mode by default (`type/*` and `status/*` in `System.Tags`, read-modify-write under a JSON Patch `test /rev`, the GitHub level of enforcement, `label-lint` unchanged); process mode behind `KZ_TRACKER_ADO_MODE=process` maps `status/*` to `System.State` (Backlog, Ready, In Progress, Blocked, Review, Done, Removed for dropped) and `type/*` to the picklist `Custom.ChangeType` on a custom work item type, where `remove_labels` of a status is a no-op and `labels-sync` verifies the states exist instead of creating anything. The work item type comes from `KZ_TRACKER_ADO_TYPE` (Task in tags mode, Change in process mode); reads and writes refuse items outside the tracker's area path (`KZ_TRACKER_AREA`, default the project) and type, because ADO ids are organisation-wide. Compliance angle: process mode is the only form where "one status at a time" and "who may transition" are server rules (Services only, `docs/ADO-ADMIN.md`); tags mode names that as a gap. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: a client already has a change work item type (map onto it) or refuses process customisation (tags mode stays).
- Work items are created with the Description in Markdown mode (`/multilineFieldsFormat/System.Description`, irreversible, Services only); `get_issue` refuses an HTML item and names the fix; protocol markers stay `<!-- tracker:{json} -->` first-line comments posted as Markdown comments (7.1-preview.4) and read back all pages, oldest first. Assumptions that only a live run settles, all listed on #16: HTML comments survive in Markdown comments; the Markdown Description round-trips exactly (CRLF, trailing whitespace, after a web edit) so `body_hash` holds; the field-format value is spelled `Markdown`; `connectionData.authenticatedUser.id` is the same lowercase GUID as comment `createdBy.id` and PR `closedBy.id`; `closedBy` is the completing human under auto-complete; comments page through `continuationToken` with `order=asc`, builds through the `x-ms-continuationtoken` header; iteration changes compare against the merge base and page via `nextSkip`; the Tags API lists a tag only once an item carries it. Fallback if markers do not survive: a visible fenced marker accepted by both backends (Q-ADO-8). Compliance angle: the frozen Markdown body is the original change record; a mismatch would surface as `done` refusing "body changed since ready". Cost: 0 USD.
- Closing keywords on ADO: a PR description that says `Fixes #N`, `Resolves #N`, `Closes #N` or ANY valid state name before `#N` transitions the work item on merge while the repo setting "Commit mention work item resolution" (default ON) is on (Microsoft Learn, auto-complete-work-items-pull-requests, 2026-02-28). So: the shared PR-body regex now also rejects the ADO default state names, the kit's own status names and `AB#N` (GitHub PR bodies such as "Review #5" are refused too: accepted side effect, the author rephrases); `done` refuses when the PR's `completionOptions.transitionWorkItems` or `bypassPolicy` is true; `commit_msg.py` rejects the keywords before `AB#N`; the repo setting OFF is an admin item in `docs/ADO-ADMIN.md`, documented and attested, not exportable (assumption). Compliance angle: the tracker stays the only state writer; the forge never closes a record on its own. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: a REST read of the setting is confirmed (then the evidence pack exports it).
- Canonical ADO identity = `IdentityRef.id` (lowercase GUID) for whoami, marker `by`, comment author and PR `closedBy`; `KZ_TRACKER_BOTS` lists bot GUIDs in lowercase (exact match, as on GitHub); no `[bot]` suffix exists on ADO, so Build Service and service-principal ids must be listed or a pipeline merge counts as human. Compliance angle: attributable (ALCOA+); automation never reads as a human merger. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: auditors need names in markers (then display names in the evidence pack, additively).
- Guard 0.5.0: tracker-guard denies `az boards work-item create|update|delete|relation add|remove` (reads stay allowed), every `az devops invoke` (any area; non-wit reads through it are blocked too, by design), `az rest` and curl/wget/httpie writes to `*/_apis/wit/*` on `dev.azure.com` and `*.visualstudio.com` (bodies on `wiql` and `workitemsbatch` count as writes), `bypassRules` or `--bypass-rules` anywhere, and `az repos pr create|update` with `--bypass-policy`, `--transition-work-items` or `--status completed` (an explicit `false` passes so pr-prep can send the safe value); deploy-ask no longer asks on `az repos pr create` and now asks on `az pipelines run`; captures and the egress screen recognise the ADO PAT (84 characters, `AZDO` at positions 76–80; the alphabet is an assumption until one revoked PAT confirms it) and captures also mask JWTs (Entra bearer tokens); `~/.azure` joins the secret-bearing directories (`AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` elsewhere is not covered); `latest_tag.py` scans `azure-pipelines*.yml` for container images (hosted `vmImage` labels and templates under other names are not scanned). Known gaps, unchanged in kind from the GitHub rules: heredoc bodies are not inspected, `az repos pr set-vote` and `--auto-complete` are branch-policy matters. 104 fixtures added, none changed; one hook run takes about 25 ms. Compliance angle: the tracker-as-only-writer control holds on both forges and is tested code. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: `az` grows a flag that moves work items (then the list) or a capture shows a token the patterns missed.
- Azure Pipelines: `ci/azure-pipelines.yml` (init, the target owns it) mirrors the GitHub `ci` workflow (test matrix 3.9/3.13, commit-msg on `HEAD^1..HEAD^2` of the merge checkout, guards, an aggregate gate that fails on anything but success) and `ci/azure-pipelines-label-lint.yml` (managed, nightly with `always: true`); PR runs come from a build-validation branch policy because YAML `pr:` is ignored on Azure Repos, so the policy configuration is evidence (A6); `tools/testguard/ado_pr_context.py` reads the PR title and labels over REST with the job token and fails closed, exporting `PR_LABELS_SET=1` so an empty label list is authoritative (GitHub keeps passing `none`); `check-test-tampering.sh` accepts a `refs/heads/` base; every `main` run ends with a retention lease (`daysValid` 36500, `protectPipeline` false: the owner may raise it) through a 600-permission header file so the token never appears in argv; the only Marketplace task is `UsePythonVersion@0`, whose major version floats because no full version was verified (named gap: tasks pin to `@X.Y.Z` at best, never a SHA; hosted images update weekly). Assumptions for the first live run, on #20: `System.PullRequest.targetBranchName` exists (fallback `TargetBranch` with the prefix stripped), the labels endpoint accepts api-version 7.1, the Build Service token may read PR labels and create leases, `dependencies.test.result` is one combined result across matrix legs, the merge checkout has two parents, the `%AZP25` escaping stops a crafted title from injecting a second logging command. Compliance angle: the same gates as GitHub on the real merge commit (`trigger: main`), and run logs survive deletion of the run by the lease. Cost: 0 USD on the free tier (one hosted parallel job, 1,800 min/month after linking an Azure subscription; or a self-hosted agent), 40 USD per extra hosted job. Revisit if: two pipeline registrations per repo prove a burden (then one file with `Build.Reason` conditions).
- Installer forge switch: `kit.manifest` lines are `mode src [dst] [@github|@ado]` (unmarked = both); `install.sh --forge github|ado`, default detected from the target's origin (`dev.azure.com`, `visualstudio.com` → ado); ADO targets receive `azure-pipelines.yml`, `azure-pipelines-label-lint.yml` and `docs/ADO-ADMIN.md`, never the GitHub workflows or issue forms; files of the other forge already in a target are left in place with a note, never deleted; `.claude/kit-version` records `forge: <x>`; an unknown `--into` argument is refused (before, any third argument was read as `--force`); `tools/tracker/ado.py` ships on both forges. Compliance angle: no inactive file can be read as an active control, and the forge is part of the version record. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: never.
- Evidence pack schema `agent-change-control/change-records/2` on both forges (`evidence_export.py` 0.2.0): `/1` plus `forge`, `api_version`, `tools.transport`, per-PR `completion_options`, per-issue `revisions` (field names only, no values), top-level `policies`, `branch_acl` (null for now, with `branch_acl_note` naming the candidate security token), `retention`; a section a forge lacks is null, so both forges carry the same key set; the committed pack under `evidence/` keeps its `/1` as a dated record. On ADO the reader (`AdoReader`, GET only; the WIQL query is the one POST, asserted by a test) takes completed PRs on the default branch (detail call per PR, since list items may truncate the description that holds `Refs #N`), votes from PR threads (`CodeReviewVoteResult`: 10 and 5 → APPROVED, -10 → CHANGES_REQUESTED, -5 and 0 → COMMENTED, with the raw `vote` and a `source` kept because the mapping alone loses detail), checks from builds on `lastMergeCommit` (with `raw_result` and `blocking`, since partiallySucceeded must never pass) plus policy evaluations, work items by `Refs #N` or `ClosedDate` in the window under the area path and type, `wit/workItems/{id}/updates` as revisions, `_apis/git/policy/configurations?repositoryId&refName` (the documented repo-scoped endpoint, not the project-wide one), `build/retention`; timestamps are parsed before comparison because ADO returns fractional seconds and the git bounds do not (a test shows string comparison puts both boundary items on the wrong side). Assumptions for the first live run, on #22: the vote-thread property names and identity index, `$top`/`$skip` paging of the PR list, `ClosedDate`/`ClosedBy` set on Completed-category states (Removed-category items appear only through a PR reference), WIQL date-only precision (widened by a day, cut on the client), the branch ACL token form. Compliance angle: the record names its source and API version; raw values travel next to every normalised one. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: a consumer depends on `/1` (then a `/1` writer behind a flag) or the live run shows a shape the fake did not (the fake changes).
- `docs/ADO-ADMIN.md` is the one-time administrator checklist (process spec for the `Change` type, Change Owners group and the restricted transitions, the denies for agent identities, area node, Build Service rights, repo settings, branch policies, retention, auditing stream, optional GHAzDO and Copilot review), each item with the REST GET that proves it or marked "attest with a dated DECISIONS line" where no read exists (commit-mention resolution, GHAzDO enablement); shipped to ADO targets only (`init docs/ADO-ADMIN.md @ado`). Assumptions it carries, on #23: the REST action type behind "Restrict the transition to state", whether a group condition combines with a state condition, the chained area-node and hex-encoded branch security tokens, the policy settings key names, the permission the Build Service needs for retention leases. Compliance angle: the checklist is what an auditor compares the exported configuration against. Cost: 0 USD. Revisit if: the first live pilot (#25) corrects an item (then the line and the GET).
- Repairs made while dogfooding: this checkout's `core.hooksPath` still pointed at the pre-rename folder, so the local commit-msg hook had not run since 2026-11-01 (every commit on the branch was checked with `tools/commit_msg.py --range` and CI re-checks the PR range); reset to `.githooks`. The session-start digest armed `SIGALRM` and cleared it only on the success path, so a backend error left a pending alarm that fired inside whatever ran next in the process (the 3.9 suite saw it as a killed `git push` in a testguard test); fixed with a `finally` that clears the alarm and restores the handler, asserted by a test. The user-scope hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json` (memory store, digest, ledger) point at the same stale folder and are the owner's to fix. Revisit if: the installer should re-point a stale `core.hooksPath` that names a vanished directory (then a line in `install.sh`).
- Found on the way, not fixed here: the one-in-progress-per-actor guard in `claim` reads claim markers from `list_issues()` results, which carry no comments on GitHub or ADO, so it fires only on `FakeBackend` (#26, a GitHub-path bug); `label-lint` exits 1 when a human-created HTML work item sits in scope (it calls `get_issue`); remotes of the form `dev.azure.com/org/_git/repo` (no project) and `vs-ssh.visualstudio.com` are not parsed. Fixed here: `test_evidence_export.py` imported `conftest` by name and got whichever `tests/conftest.py` pytest loaded last, so the tracker and guard suites could not run together.
- Copilot code review exists for Azure Repos PRs (public preview on Services since Sprint 279, 2026-09-04; claim refuted during planning): it reads `.github/instructions/*.instructions.md` from the target branch (the kit's file already carries `applyTo`), always votes Comment, never counts toward reviewer policies, and is billed per review in GitHub AI credits on a linked Azure subscription; it is advisory only (branch policy "Automatically request Copilot code review" where the org allows, with a credit cap), and `fresh-review` receipts (home) plus the human approval (both forges) remain the review evidence. Cost: per review, price unverified. Revisit if: it can vote or count toward required reviewers.
- Independent review before the PR: `bin/fresh-review` in three slices, because the 434 KB branch diff is over the egress screen's 200 KB cap (`main..3b65cd3`, `3b65cd3..f890b4e`, `f890b4e..a8a0b60`; the first two from detached worktrees, receipts under `.git/fresh-review/` and `.git/worktrees/review-s*/fresh-review/`), Opus, three COMMENT verdicts, 15 findings, 3.57 USD, plus two rounds on the fixes, 0.38 USD. Fixed on this branch (the commits `fix(tracker): bound the ADO evidence reader to its scope and window`, `test(tracker): skip the alarm test where SIGALRM is missing`, `docs: fix the status lines, the bots wording and two comments` and `fix(tracker): format the PR window in UTC and cover the area-path scope`; subjects rather than SHAs, because main is rebased): the evidence export read any work item a PR's `Refs #N` named, ids being organisation-wide (now id-only with a `skipped` reason for an item outside the tracker's type and area path, and for a missing id; `in_scope` unit-tested on a child node, a sibling that shares the prefix, the parent and another type); the completed-PR listing read the repository's whole history (now `searchCriteria.queryTimeRangeType=closed` with the window a day wider on each side, both bounds converted to UTC before the literal `Z`; Microsoft Learn, Get Pull Requests 7.1, 2025-03-18; the test's git bounds carry `+01:00` and the asserted stamps are UTC); the alarm test needs SIGALRM (skipped on Windows); status lines and the `KZ_TRACKER_BOTS` wording. Committed on this branch after the owner's explicit go-ahead on 2026-11-01, because the Claude Code session had refused to commit changes to security tooling on its own (`fix(guard): read az global options before the command words`, `feat(egress): hold long Basic credentials in the screen`; not fresh-reviewed, covered by the fixture corpus and the egress test): `az` global options before the command (`az -o json devops invoke …`) walked past the tracker-guard's command parser (skipped with their values; two deny and one allow fixture); a Basic credential (how a PAT travels, base64) was not held by the egress screen. Accepted and recorded, not changed: `bypassRules=true` is denied wherever it appears outside a heredoc, a `grep` for it included (the deny names the rule; a request-argument parser is not worth it); `label-lint` in process mode reads every item ever created each night, so lint time grows with the project (raise `timeoutInMinutes` or add a changed-since filter when a project feels it); `pr_checks` stops after 1000 newer builds and returns an empty list, which `owner done` refuses ("no checks found"), never passes; a board drag to Removed is the human `dropped` and leaves no marker by design; `latest_tag` reads `image:` lines inside block scalars too (documented). Slice artefacts: the `PR_LABELS_SET` handling, `ado_pr_context.py` and the retention-lease line each sit in a slice the other reviewer did not see (covered by `test_azure_repos_inputs_base_ref_with_prefix_and_an_authoritative_empty_label_list` and the Azure Pipelines line above). Compliance angle: the receipts are the review evidence for this change; every finding has a disposition here. Revisit if: slicing recurs (then a `--to <sha>` option on `fresh-review`).
- Out of v1 and named: Azure DevOps Server (no Markdown fields, Entra identities, group transition rules or auditing; XML process model on old collections), Jira, service hooks for work-item events (label-lint runs nightly and on demand only), a token cache, an MCP server, policy evaluations inside `pr_checks` (builds only; the evidence pack carries evaluations), GHAzDO as a dependency (Secret Protection recommended at work: 19 USD per active committer per month), organisation audit streaming (5–15 USD/month at work; the in-product log keeps 90 days). Compliance angle: deferred controls are listed so that absence is never read as presence.

## 2026-11-01 — guard 0.5.1: `rules_off` for local development

- `rules_off` (rule names) in the gitignored `.claude/guard.local.json` (one checkout) or `KZ_RULES_OFF=a,b` (one shell) makes `run_hook` silence those rules' asks; a deny stands whatever the list says (force-push, protected branch, tracker writes cannot be switched off locally); honoured only when the committed `.claude/guard.json` sits beside `.git` and says `boundary: own` itself (the default, a missing key, an unreadable file, an ancestor's file or `KZ_BOUNDARY=own` do not count), nothing but `rules_off` is read from the local file (a malformed list silences nothing, an unknown name is reported on stderr), and a committed `rules_off` is a reviewed policy change like any other key, so a client repo's policy is exactly its committed file; a silenced rule still runs and is logged as `skipped` in `decisions.jsonl` (the ledger prints the count), so the override stays measurable; why: the ADO session's telemetry showed 63 of 67 guard prompts were `delete-guard` and `guard-config` asks on the owner's own repo (cleaning scratch dirs, editing the guard under test), and the prompt-fatigue rule above says such rules are widened or demoted; accepted: with `guard-config` off a session can widen its own override (asks only, never a deny), at home only; audit trail: the committed policy carries no diff and the override is local, invisible to CI and to the evidence pack; revisit if: a rule stays off for a month in every own checkout (then change the committed default), or Copilot at work needs the same relief (then a client-boundary variant with an allow-list that never includes `guard-config`).
- Independent review of #28 (`bin/fresh-review`, Opus, receipts under `.git/fresh-review/` keyed by head SHA): run 1 BLOCKER (the local file merged every key but `boundary`) → only `rules_off` is read, type-checked, unknown names reported; run 2 COMMENT (fail-open without a committed policy; no telemetry for skipped rules; `KZ_RULES_OFF` is per shell; a committed `rules_off` undocumented) → all four taken; run 3 COMMENT (`own` came from the default, not the file; an ancestor's `guard.json` or `KZ_BOUNDARY=own` counted; denies could be switched off) → all three taken, as above; run 4 COMMENT (no stderr note when the override is ignored; the local file is gitignored in the kit only) → a stderr note, and the README tells task repos to gitignore it since the installer never edits `.gitignore` (an accidentally committed local file is reviewable like any other diff); those two landed unreviewed, as one print and one sentence; USD 0.86 + 0.19 + 0.22 + 0.21 = 1.48.

## 2026-11-03 — CI runner image pinned

- The GitHub workflows run on `ubuntu-24.04`, not `ubuntu-latest`, as the Azure pipelines already did; why: on 2026-11-03 `ubuntu-latest` moved to 26.04 and `actions/setup-python` has no 3.9 build for it, so the test matrix could not start and the `ci` gate went red on a docs-only PR (#32, run 41623557052); 3.9 stays the floor because work machines run it; a pinned image also makes the runner a known quantity in the validation evidence; revisit if: the 3.9 floor is dropped (then `ubuntu-latest` again), or GitHub retires the 24.04 image. 0 CHF/month.

## 2026-11-03 — step 3.8: guard 0.5.2, the Copilot CLI shape and the last temporary allowance

- `Event` parses `toolArgs` sent as a JSON string (the Copilot CLI form; `testguard` and `latest_tag` already did), with a shape fixture; why: a string used to read as "no arguments", so every call in that shape passed every rule, a fail-open on one harness shape; an empty string is no arguments; a string that is not JSON, nests past the recursion limit or decodes to anything but an object makes the PreToolUse hook `ask` with the reason, because before the tool runs "no arguments" would be a fail-open, unlike the PostToolUse hooks where the tool has already acted; revisit if: a capture from the work machine shows a third shape.
- Generated fixtures now say `synthesized by make_fixtures.py from _shapes/<harness>/<shape>.json` in `_fixture.source` instead of repeating the shape's own capture note; why: 538 generated files claimed to be captures (ALCOA+ original/accurate); only the `_shapes/` files are captures or synthesized envelopes, and `test_corpus_matches_generator` proves the rest derive from them; revisit if: never.
- The temporary `gh issue create --label agent-loop|handoff` allowance is gone from the tracker-guard (two fixtures move from allow to deny); the private app repo's loop moves onto `tracker claim|block|review` in that repo, recorded there (#13, third criterion, the owner's); revisit if: never.

## 2026-11-13 — orchestration and CI plan rev 2 approved (docs/PLAN-2026-11-orchestration-ci.md)

- Orchestration = three levels with `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH=2`: a Fable session orchestrates, Opus workers take one packet each in a worktree, read-only sub-workers only; why: each layer multiplies tokens and level-3 output has no reader; the default depth moved twice in 2026 (2.1.217, 2.1.219); compliance: one packet = one tracker item = one PR keeps attribution; revisit if: a task needs a delegating helper twice in a month.
- Headless orchestration = saved workflows started by name with a `Workflow(<name>)` allow rule under `dontAsk`; workflows are flat (tested 2026-11-13 on 2.1.323), so the verify stage is in the script; why: the plan becomes a reviewed file and the allow rule names exactly what CI may run; revisit if: workflow agents gain a spawn tool.
- Every headless Claude job follows one contract: explicit permission mode, budget and turn caps, background tasks off, JSON-schema output, and a deterministic post-step that fails on denials, `null` stages or missing output; why: a denied or empty run exits 0 (tested) and the action can end before background agents report (action issues #1499, #1852, #1646, open); compliance: the receipt is the validation evidence, not the job colour; revisit if: those issues close and a probe confirms it.
- Model review in CI is advisory and label-triggered (`review/panel`: three lenses plus one verifier); the gates stay `ci` and the human approval; cost: API-equivalent about $1–3 a run by estimate, up to $12 by one third-party report, so $10–30 a month at ten labelled PRs, and $0 in money while CI uses the subscription token; capped per run by `--max-budget-usd`; revisit if: the API-equivalent monthly figure passes $20 or the panel finds nothing the single review missed over 20 PRs.
- CI model access on own repos = a subscription token in a GitHub environment secret, for now (cost: $0 extra, runs draw on the plan's limits); a recorded deviation from "no long-lived keys", own non-client repos only, mitigated by the environment scope, the label trigger, a read-only job token and calendar rotation; client repos use Bedrock or Foundry through OIDC, always; revisit if: API billing is set up, a second person needs the job, or the job reaches a client-adjacent repo (then workload identity federation).
- Agent identity = a GitHub App (settles Q6 of the 2026-10 plan): sessions commit and push as `<app>[bot]` with installation tokens that expire within the hour; the private key stays in the OS keychain; why: a real bot principal in every harness, so the approval check and the ruleset can tell agent work from the owner's; compliance: attributable (ALCOA+), the person appears as approver and not as author of agent work; revisit if: the key cannot be kept out of sessions.
- `agent-approval-check` is a required status with one approval; on a solo repo the owner's `/approve <head-sha>` is the recorded approval; the ruleset's required approvals go from 0 to 1 once the App authors the PRs; compliance: approval is bound to the exact commit and goes stale on a push; revisit if: a second maintainer joins (then two approvals).
- The guard gates workflow launches (`ask` unless the name is listed) and counts workflow agents in the fan-out total; the guard hook sets `onFailure: "block"`; why: workflow agents bypassed the ask (five spawned unprompted on 2026-11-13) and a timed-out hook failed open; revisit if: never.
- Cloud features (Projects, Code Review, ultrareview, routines, PR auto-fix, self-hosted environments) are not used for client repos; why: they run on, or send the transcript to, vendor infrastructure; client CI uses the action or `claude -p` on our runner with Bedrock or Foundry through OIDC; revisit if: a feature documents an in-boundary mode that covers the transcript.
- Orchestration assumptions live in `tools/orchestration/verified.json` and are re-probed when the CLI version moves (about $0.05 a run); why: three defaults this plan rests on changed within four months; revisit if: never.
- The Gemini plan review is not proposed or run for now (Kacper, 2026-11-13), which suspends the "Gemini plans only" part of the routing decision of 2026-10-29; a second vendor for reviews (an OpenAI model) is undecided and would enter as one panel lens behind the egress screen, on client repos only through an in-boundary endpoint; revisit if: Kacper asks for it again, or the panel has 20 runs in the ledger.
- Skeleton vs production-grade at the end of Phase 2: review job and approval check are production-grade except for model access, where the subscription token is the interim; the panel's cost figure is an estimate until 20 runs are in the ledger; a writing agent in CI, the Azure Pipelines port and the Copilot nesting story are not built.

## 2026-11-13 — orchestration step 0.1: the canary probe and its first record (#37)

- `tools/orchestration/probe.py` re-runs seven canaries (eight `claude -p` runs) and judges each from hook rows, marker files and fields of the run's JSON, as `holds`, `fails` or `inconclusive`; missing evidence is `inconclusive`, and a full run writes `verified.json` even when a canary fails; why: fact 3 of the plan says the exit code and the prose are not evidence, and two findings of 2026-11-13 ("blocked", "no spawn tool") rested on the model's own report; compliance: the record is the dated test evidence for the tool behaviour the controls rest on (CLI version, date, model, observed counts, cost; no session ids, no paths); first record: CLI 2.1.323, seven of seven hold, from the fourth full run (the first and the third each had one `inconclusive` canary; the causes and the two changes they led to are in the lines below); revisit if: the CLI documents a machine-readable record of tool lists and denials per agent.
- Probe sessions are hermetic: `--setting-sources project`, `dontAsk` with allow rules that name only the scripted commands, a fresh scratch project per run outside the repository, Haiku as the actuator, constant prompts; why: a canary has to report a change in the CLI, not in the owner's user settings; compliance: no repository or client content reaches the model; cost: $0.03–0.04 a full run, measured four times (API-equivalent; about $1 a month at one run a day), each run capped at $0.25; revisit if: a canary needs user-scope behaviour, or Haiku stops following the scripts (then Sonnet for that canary, a few cents).
- The depth pin is shown with a control: the same three-layer chain at pin 3 and at pin 2, both set in the scratch settings `env`; why: at the limit the CLI withholds the `Agent` tool (docs, read 2026-11-13), so there is no refusal event to observe, and an absent third layer alone would not tell a working pin from a model that did not try; measured: three layers at pin 3, two at pin 2, and `subagent_stats.max_depth` agrees; revisit if: the CLI reports depth refusals for ordinary subagents.
- Scripted commands are recognised by file name (`touch <path>` with the marker's base name) in the hook, the judges and the allow rules (`Bash(touch NAME)` plus `Bash(touch */NAME)`); why: on the first full run a custom subagent rewrote the relative path as an absolute one, the exact-match hook blocked the control command and the canary was `inconclusive`, as it should be; the wildcard rule was checked by hand under `dontAsk`, because the recorded run used the relative form; revisit if: never.
