- Heads five and six were reviewed without the generated fixture files: the egress screen holds a diff above 200,000 characters and these two have 574,721 and 441,143, because the fixtures arrive in the same commits as the code, so the commit-range slices used for PR #27 do not help; the review ran on the diff without `tools/guard/tests/fixtures/` (69,791 and 148,714 characters) through a scratch driver over `tools/review/fresh_review.py` (the same prompt, screen, model and schema), and the receipts are named `<head>.no-fixtures.json` and carry the scope, the path left out with its file count (432 and 250 files), and the size of the whole and of the reviewed diff; what that leaves unread: the fixture files themselves were neither reviewed nor screened; `make_fixtures.py`, which writes them, was, and `test_fixtures.py` fails when a file differs from its output; the review of head six ran at `0c7b171`, and the commit after it (`2d7a5d8`, three lines of this log) was not reviewed again; cost: within the $5.49 above; compliance: the scope of a review is part of its record, and a receipt that covered less than the diff says so in its name and its fields; skeleton: the driver is a scratch script, not part of the kit; revisit if: a second pull request hits the cap (then `bin/fresh-review` gets a path filter that writes the left-out files into the receipt, and the screen runs over the left-out files separately).

## 2026-11-17 — guard 0.7.0: every shell rule reads each command, the hook answers in time, the log names the guard and the call

- `git-guard`, `tracker-guard`, `secret-shell`, `protected-branch` and `host-install` read a shell line the way `deploy-ask` and `delete-guard` do since 0.6.1: each keeps its first pass over `segments()` as it was and adds a reader pass, in which every command word of every simple command that `nested_commands` finds is judged by the same code, nested scripts included; a deny of the first pass returns before the reader runs, and a line the reader cannot read, or an error inside it, asks with the reason. There are two readings: the full one (`git-guard`, `tracker-guard`, `secret-shell` and the two rules of 0.6.1) also reads what runs inside a container or on another host; the local one (`host-install`, `protected-branch`) ends a simple command at the first command word that names a container or remote tool, because an install inside a container is the agreed route for installs and a commit there lands on the branch checked out there; why: measured on 0.6.1 with 71 wrapper and nesting forms times 13 payloads, 476 of 923 cells were silent or weaker than the plain command (`timeout 30 git commit --no-verify -m x`, `bash -c 'git push origin main'`, `timeout 5 gh issue close 3`, `bash -c 'cat .env'`, `su -c 'npm install'`), and now all 923 hold; approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 (item h of that day's list); cost: none (0 CHF/month); compliance: these five rules keep the commit gate, the tracker, the protected branch, the secret files and the developer machine from being passed by, and a wrapper in front of the command was enough to pass them without a line in `decisions.jsonl`; revisit if: a form passes that this block does not name as a limit (add it as a failing test first).
- Two things the reader made necessary. In the reader pass of `secret-shell` the pattern operand of a search tool (`grep`, `egrep`, `fgrep`, `zgrep`, `rg`, `ag`, `ack`, `git grep`) is no path: the first build read it as one, and four of 2,366 real commands got a new ask because their pattern named a secret file (`grep -rn '\.env|\.pem' tools`); when `-e`, `--regexp`, `-f` or `--file` names the pattern (also glued, with `=`, or among other short options as in `-rnf`), no operand is dropped and the file of `-f` is read (`bash -c 'grep -rnf .env notes.txt'` asks); the first pass is unchanged, so what asked at 0.6.1 still asks. And a `git push` that the full reading finds and the local reading does not runs in a container or on another host: without a refspec, or with the destination `HEAD`, it asks and says that the branch checked out there is not known, whatever branch this machine is on (the first build judged it by this machine's branch: a deny on `main`, silence elsewhere, both guesses); force, `--all`, `--mirror`, `--no-verify`, a `+` refspec and a named protected destination deny there as here.
- A delete, a deploy, a forced push, a tracker bypass and a secret file count behind a runner and inside a container too, and an install inside a container stays silent (the local reading): the owner's decision of 2026-11-16 on item j, taken as the widest of the choices offered and named in the question as the noisiest, because `docker run --rm …` is also the route for installs. The reader follows a tool plus a subcommand that runs a command: on this machine `uv|poetry|pdm|pipenv|hatch|conda|mamba|micromamba run`, `mise exec|x`, `asdf|bundle|npm|pnpm|yarn exec`, `direnv exec DIR`, `busybox` and `toybox`, `nix-shell --run|--command '…'`, `nix develop|shell … -c|--command …`, `npx -c|--call '…'`, `npm exec -c|--call '…'`, `fd|fdfind -x|-X|--exec|--exec-batch`, and the body of `coproc NAME { …; }`; in a container `docker|podman|nerdctl run|exec` (also after `container` and `compose`), `docker-compose|podman-compose run|exec`, `kubectl|oc run|exec|debug`, `oc rsh` and `devcontainer exec`; why: at 0.6.1 `uv run --offline rm -rf build`, `docker exec dev git push origin main` and `docker compose exec web rm -rf /data` were silent, as were 46 of the 82 rows of the runner table; now all 82 hold; cost: none; what it costs in prompts: `docker run --rm -v "$PWD":/w alpine rm -rf /w/build` and its relatives ask now; measured on what is actually run: of 114 everyday commands none changes its verdict, on a feature branch or on `main`, and over the 2,727 distinct Bash commands of the transcripts of the last 30 days (97 files) no rule asks or denies more than 0.6.1 did; revisit if: the container route for installs is decided (then the install forms inside `docker run` get rows of their own), or the asks behind `docker run` turn out to be noise in real use.
- `deploy-ask` and `delete-guard` ask for the verbs their tables never had: the 31 commands of the 2026-11-15 review and their families, as token rows (the text expressions are unchanged, apart from the two of the bullet on prose below). `deploy-ask`: `docker|podman|nerdctl image|manifest push`, and `build`, `buildx build` and `buildx bake` with `--push`; `terraform|tofu force-unlock`, `workspace delete`, `state replace-provider`; `kubectl label|annotate|set|run|taint|expose|autoscale|debug`; `gh run rerun|cancel|delete` and `gh workflow enable|disable`; `argocd app`; `flux reconcile|resume|delete|create|bootstrap`; the guest verbs of `qm` and `pct`; `skopeo` and `crane`; for `aws` every operation that starts with `delete-`, `terminate-` or `batch-delete-`, `ecs update-service`, and `lambda update-function-code`, `update-function-configuration` and `update-alias`; for `az` the words `deploy`, `update` and `up` where the verb stands, not as the value of an option (`az repos pr update` is exempt, `az extension update` belongs to `host-install`). `delete-guard`: `docker image|container rm`, `network rm|prune`, `buildx rm|prune` and `context rm`, with the same rows for `podman` and `nerdctl`; `pipx uninstall|uninstall-all`, `uv pip|tool uninstall`, `helm repo remove|rm`, `helm plugin uninstall|remove|rm`; `git checkout` with `-- <path>`, with `-f`, with the operand `.`, or with two or more plain operands (a ref and paths) unless an option names a new branch, and `git switch` with `--discard-changes` or `-f`. A help word in front of the first `--` keeps a 0.7.0 row silent (`kubectl label --help`); the 0.6.1 rows ask for it as they did; why: each of these changes a live environment or discards work, and passed in silence: 106 of the 178 rows of the verb table were red at 0.6.1, none is now; approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 (item k); cost: none; revisit if: a verb of these tools turns up that the table does not have (add the row and its test).
- The pre hook answers within 3.5 s (`HOOK_BUDGET_S`): it checks the rule names, then runs the rules in a forked child that writes one line per verdict to a pipe; the parent reads until the child is done or the time is up, then kills and reaps the child; the verdicts that arrived stand (a deny stays a deny), and a call the rules did not finish gets one more ask, logged under the name `budget`, which says that the call could not be judged in time, how long the command is and which rules did not judge it; a child that died is treated the same way, and an error inside a rule is reported as before (exit 1). `segments()` is linear now (it copied the token list once per wrapper and option); why: both wirings cut the hook at 5 s and a cut hook fails open, so a call that no rule had judged ran, without a line in the log: measured on 0.6.1, eight generated shapes of 100 kB ran past the cut (`find` 6.7 s, `;A=` 12.5 s, `git push` 12.3 s, `xargs` and `az x (` more than 20 s); now 160 hook runs (32 shapes at 100 kB, 300 kB, 500 kB and 1 MB on Python 3.9.6, and at 1 MB on 3.13.13) all end with exit 0 within 3.64 s in each of five runs (within 3.60 s in four of them), and the shapes the rules cannot finish get the budget ask (on 3.9.6, 9 of the 32 at 100 kB in every run and 16 to 18 at 1 MB: a shape that ends close to the budget falls on either side); approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 (item i); cost: none; in time: a plain `ls -la` call takes 0.030 to 0.031 s where it took 0.026 s (medians of 20 on Python 3.13.13, measured four times; 0.078 to 0.079 s against 0.074 s on 3.9.6); compliance: a call that no rule judged can no longer run without a record: it prompts, and its line in `decisions.jsonl` says `budget`; revisit if: a harness changes the 5 s cut, or the budget ask shows up in real use (then the slow expression gets fixed and the budget stays).
- Wrong path, backed out: the first build of the budget was a timer (`SIGALRM`, commit `346af3e`), and it did not hold on Python 3.9, which is the `python3` of macOS and one of the two CI versions: there a signal handler does not run inside one `re` search of the kind the delete and deploy expressions make, so a line of `rsync a ` repeated answered after 6.64 s at 500 kB and after 26.02 s at 1 MB (measured through the real hook; Python 3.11.15, 3.13.13 and 3.14.6 do interrupt such a search, 3.10 was not measured). Two smaller repairs were weighed and dropped on measurements: a watchdog thread does not run while the search holds the interpreter (in a probe it woke only when the search had returned, after 3.76 s on 3.9.6 and 3.40 s on 3.13.13), and rewriting the slow expressions or capping the line length rests on finding every slow one, which a scan of the guard's 53 expressions did not do (it flagged four and missed one that was known to be slow). A second process is a boundary that a search cannot hold, so commit `d1d9d78` replaced the timer. Named limits: without `os.fork` (Windows), or when a fork fails, the rules run in the hook process without a budget, as before 0.7.0; `hook post` and `capture` run without it; a child killed at the budget can leave a rule's own log line unfinished (the spawn log of `fan-out`); and the new tests assert wall-clock times (an answer within 2 s or 4.5 s), which a loaded build agent can miss.
- A line of `decisions.jsonl` holds `guard` (the version that judged) and, when the event has one, `call` (the harness's id of the tool call). A new hook target, `guard.py hook post`, wired in `.claude/settings.json` under PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure, appends a line with the verdict `ran` when the call it reports was asked about in the same session (it looks for the ask in the last 128 kB of the log and writes one `ran` line per call); `ledger.py cost` prints `ran X of Y` per rule, where Y counts the asks that carry a call id and X those whose call went ahead; why: the open item was to log the answer to an ask, and there is no event for the answer: Claude Code fires no hook when a prompt is declined (hooks reference, read 2026-11-17: PostToolUseFailure "doesn't fire for tool calls rejected before execution", a permission denial among them, and PermissionDenied "only fires in auto mode: it doesn't run when you manually deny a permission dialog"), so "the asked call ran" is the evidence there is, and an ask without a `ran` line was declined, is still open, or was let run and then interrupted (the reference says that cancelling a running tool does not fire PostToolUseFailure); the version closes the gap named in the 0.6.1 block (which guard judged a command followed only from the commit checked out at the time); approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 (items g and l); cost: none; in time: one more hook process per call of the matched tools, 0.028 s on Python 3.13.13 and 0.077 s on 3.9.6; compliance: ALCOA+ attributable (a line names the version of the tool that judged) and complete (an ask is tied to what happened next by session and call id, where the harness sends one); the log still holds no command text; limits: Copilot sends no call id, so its asks get no `ran` line and never count in Y; an ask that has left the last 128 kB of the log before its call ends (a long build while other sessions write) gets no `ran` line, so X can undercount; a repository that had its `.claude/settings.json` before 0.7.0 keeps it on install (`init` in `kit.manifest`), so the two entries are added by hand there; skeleton: the lookup by the log's tail; revisit if: the harness gains an event for a declined prompt (then log the answer itself), or `ran X of Y` is to be used as a control figure (then key the lookup by call id instead of the tail).
- Two delete expressions no longer match prose, the one place where 0.7.0 asks less than 0.6.1 on purpose: `del\s*\(` became `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])del\s*\(` ("Data model (" and `load_model(x)` are silent; `del (x)`, `client.del(key)` and `cache_del(k)` ask), and the lower-case word `truncate` asks as at 0.6.1 everywhere but in two places, inside a quoted string of a command line and in the text of a script the command names: there it counts only where a statement can start (a line start, also one written `\n` in a string; behind `;`, a quote, a parenthesis or a backtick), in front of `table`, or, on a command line, anywhere when the line names a SQL client whose TRUNCATE needs no TABLE keyword (`psql`, `mysql`, `mariadb`, `cqlsh`, `snowsql`, `duckdb`, `cockroach`, `hbase`, `clickhouse`, each as a word of its own); the upper-case keyword and the coreutils `truncate` command ask as before; why: replayed over one week of this project's Bash calls (1,883), the delete expressions would have asked 117 times, and about 8 of those were deletes or discards; 37 were runs of `tools/tracker/tracker.py`, whose comment "Data model (step 2…" matched `del\s*\(`, and `evidence_export.py` tripped the other expression on "may truncate the description"; with the fix, 64 of the 2,727 distinct commands of the last 30 days stop asking (222 asks become 155) and no other verdict changes; approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 with a list of 20 cases, which are tests now. The narrowing of `truncate` let real commands pass five times, and the rows written with each fix did not show it. The fresh review of the branch: SQL after a block comment, inside a `DO $$ BEGIN … END $$` block, unquoted in front of a pipe (`echo truncate table audit | psql`), behind `\n` in a `printf` string, and names that end in `_del(` had stopped asking (13 of a probe list of 27 forms; commit `7013b5b`, which brought the client names). The review of the later commits: a client name counted only with a space or the line end behind it, so `bash -c "echo truncate audit | psql"` and `echo truncate audit | psql;` were silent (16 of 20 forms; commit `0e7c26b`). A wider probe of that fix, 54 forms from other tools: no list names every tool that takes the text, and `gtruncate -s 0 app.log` (the Homebrew name of the command), `hdfs dfs -truncate 0 /f` and `echo truncate users | pgcli` were silent (15 of the 54; commit `86136ec`, since which the word asks wherever it stands outside quotes and in a here-document). Wrong path, backed out: `86136ec` found the quoted strings with an expression of its own, and the review of that commit showed ways past it, an escaped apostrophe (`echo don\'t; echo truncate users | pgcli; echo won\'t`), an apostrophe in a comment, and a `$( … )` or backticks inside double quotes, which the local shell runs; commit `c864964` removed the expression and asks the reader of the token rules, which reads these forms as the shell does: the word is outside quotes when it is a word of its own in a simple command of the line or of a script the line nests, so `bash -c "echo truncate users | pgcli"` asks too. And a probe of what the cut still gave up (18 forms) found a line break written as `\n`, which until then asked only on a line with a listed client (`printf 'begin\ntruncate users\n' | pgcli`; commit `42df566`). Of the 27 forms two differ from 0.6.1 now, both prose in quotes, of the 54 one (`mycli -e "x truncate users"`), and of the 18 six; cost: none, and three of the 2,727 commands ask again since `86136ec`, each of them prose in a here-document, as at 0.6.1; compliance: a deliberate cut in a control, recorded with what it gives up: the lower-case word without `table`, away from a statement start, inside a quoted string that the reader does not take for a script, on a command line that names none of the nine clients: SQL behind a comment (`pgcli -c "/* cleanup */ truncate users"`), and a script piped to a shell or held in a variable (`echo 'hdfs dfs -truncate 0 /f' | sh`, `C='gtruncate -s 0 app.log'; $C`), which the reader follows for no rule; and the same word in the text of a script (mixed case, `Truncate`, never asked); skeleton: the client list is nine names, and what stands outside quotes is what the reader reads, with the reader's limits (the line on what it does not cover); revisit if: a real TRUNCATE passes (add the form as a failing test, then widen), or prose asks again (the same in the other direction).
- How it was built and checked: two Opus engineers built it in parallel, each in a worktree of its own, from one shared brief and a brief each (one: the prose expressions, the budget, the log fields; the other: the reader for the five rules, the verbs, the runners), test first, against tables of expected verdicts written before the build (923 reader cells, 178 verb rows, 82 runner rows, 26 rows that must stay silent, 20 prose cases). Two Opus reviewers with different jobs (one hunted for commands that pass without a prompt, one for regressions against 0.6.1, noise, cost and crash paths) returned 18 findings and no blocker (6 major, 11 minor, 1 nit); this session's own checks added three (a held-out matrix of 88 forms times 23 payloads the engineers never saw: of its 2,024 cells 960 were weaker than the plain command at 0.6.1 and 19 still were after the build, all one form, `coproc NAME { …; }`; the replay of real commands, which found the search patterns; and comments that cited numbers of a brief). Of the 18, eight were closed, three in part, and seven lie outside the approved scope (last bullet); a third Opus engineer closed the rule findings and the first one, resumed, replaced the timer. A fresh-context review of the whole branch (Opus without tools, through the egress screen, on the diff without the 164 new fixture files: 164,496 of 355,392 characters; receipt `88e7a5a3d6e1d03b4fffe7756b7f59f1ed1fcf45.no-fixtures.json`; COMMENT, 6 findings, 2 major, $1.70) found the SQL forms above and eight tests of the budget that returned early, and so passed without testing anything, where the platform has no `fork` (they carry a `skipif` mark now, commit `f29c5d0`); its other findings are limits named in this block and the missing decision lines, which this block is. A second fresh-context review, of the commits after `88e7a5a` (53,341 characters, nothing left out; receipt `b361701bd3ac5c23e6e4b69f405c58bd3ad3d4cb.no-fixtures.json`; COMMENT, 3 findings, 1 major, $0.35), found the client names of the line above, the tests they lacked, and a sentence of the README that left out one reason for a missing `ran` line (commit `5ed4d98`). A third, of the four commits after `b361701` (42,323 characters; receipt `26381612da5b7c61307cc58effb60ab936633dff.no-fixtures.json`; COMMENT, 3 findings, 2 major, $1.08), found the ways past the expression for quoted strings (line above) and two figures of this block that a second measurement had replaced without a word. The session wrote ten of the code and test commits itself (short options in one word for the search pattern, the version number, the SQL forms, the `skipif` marks, the client names, the word outside quotes, the reader in place of the expression, the line break written as `\n`, and the two commits of test rows that the fourth and the fifth review asked for), because the five agents the working agreement allows without asking were spent. The replay and the time of a plain call were measured more than once: first at `f29c5d0` (2,608 distinct commands, of which 64 stopped asking, 219 asks became 152; 0.031 s and 0.079 s for the plain call) and last on 2026-11-17 at the last code commit, when the transcripts had grown by this session's own commands; the replay figures in this block are those of the last measurement. On the last code commit (`42df566`): `ruff` clean; 4,302 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.9 (2,403 at 0.6.1); the four tables hold in full; no cell of the held-out matrix is weaker than its plain command outside 173 cells of forms named as limits below; the replay and the timings are those quoted above. A fourth review, of the three commits after `2638161` (41,561 characters; receipt `8784110ac929e8e53c1197837cd793a9a10e9120.no-fixtures.json`; COMMENT, 2 findings, both minor, $0.32), and a fifth, of the six test rows the fourth led to (1,057 characters; receipt `71ddcfbc3543433ab5eea6b937c1706bb055820a.no-fixtures.json`; COMMENT, 1 finding, minor, $0.15), found nothing to change in the guard and asked for tests. The fourth read the new expression as asking for a word that ends in `truncate` (`--no-truncate`) even at the end of a command; the condition of 0.6.1 in front of that expression keeps such a command silent, and rows now pin it. The fifth was right that the first row for the written `\r` held without the rule it was written for (checked on a scratch copy of the guard without that rule: the first row holds there, the new one does not). Seven rows in two commits (`71ddcfb`, `c2c4095`), each with the same verdict at 0.6.1; with them 4,309 passed, 9 skipped on both Pythons. The five reviews cover the branch up to `71ddcfb`; the one row after it and the commit that brings this sentence are for the review of the pull request, and its body names that receipt. Cost: 0 CHF/month to run; the build cost about $23 for the five agents by the ledger's own count and $3.60 for the five reviews, and the session's own share is in the ledger line it writes when it ends.
- A correction of the record: the commits `f421cb5`, `346af3e` and `9761546` name Claude Fable 5.2 as co-author, and they were written by the first Opus engineer (`claude-opus-5-5` in its transcript); the shared brief of this session gave both engineers the session's own trailer, and one of them followed it. The history is left as it is, because later commit messages and the review receipt cite these hashes; this line is the correction (ALCOA+ attributable). The briefs of the fix pass told each agent to use the trailer of its own session.
- What it does not cover (measured; the guard reads text and prompts, it is no sandbox): the reader does not follow a command held in a variable (`C='…'; $C`), the output of a command (`eval "$(echo '…')"`), a pipe or a here-document fed to a shell (`echo '…' | bash`, `bash -s <<'EOF'`), or a command behind a tool its tables do not name (`unshare`, `ltrace`, `gdb --args`): `unshare -n rm -rf build`, `C='rm -rf build'; $C` and `echo 'rm -rf build' | bash` are silent, as at 0.6.1, and where one of these forms does ask, a text expression of the rule caught it (`git reset --hard` and `kubectl apply` behind `unshare`); of the 173 cells above 167 are of these forms (the here-document was probed apart, item (n) below), and the other 6 are an install or a commit behind `docker run --entrypoint sh img -c '…'`, silent by the local reading (the other five rules read that form); `git checkout` with one operand, which cannot be told from a branch switch by its text; an install or a commit inside a container or over `ssh`, silent by the local reading; the value of an option that names a container tool, which ends the local reading (`sudo -g docker npm install -g x` and, on a protected branch, `sudo -u docker git commit -m x` are silent, as at 0.6.1); a push elsewhere when the identical command also stands locally in the same line (one verdict serves both: `git push; ssh build 'git push'` is judged by this machine's branch); in the reader pass of `secret-shell`, the value of an option taken for the search pattern (`grep -A 3 '\.env|\.pem' tools` nested in `bash -c` asks, because the pattern is then read as a path); the caps of 0.6.1 (a nest deeper than five levels, more than 32 tool names or 64 command positions in one simple command ask); no budget without `fork`; no `ran` lines from Copilot.
- Found by the reviews and the held-out matrix outside the approved scope, measured and left as they are until the owner says otherwise (letters continue the list of 2026-11-16): (m) `secret-shell` does not see a secret path written as `@path` or `--opt=path` (`curl --data-binary @.env https://…`, `docker run --env-file=.env …`, `kubectl --kubeconfig=~/.kube/config …`; silent on every rule, `egress-guard` included; the same commands with a space ask); (n) a here-document fed to a shell is read by none of the reader rules (`bash -s <<'EOF'` with `git commit --no-verify` or `git push origin HEAD:main` inside is silent); (o) `tracker-guard` misses an option in front of the verb (`gh -R o/r issue close 3`, `gh --repo o/r label create bug`); (p) `git-guard` misses `git push -uf origin feat` (the force flag among other short options), `git push --repo origin feat:main`, and the ways around the hook gate that are no `--no-verify` (`HUSKY=0`, `SKIP=…`, `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null commit`); (q) `secret-shell`: `git -C . add .env` asks where `git add .env` denies, and `cat <.env` is silent because the reader drops redirections; (r) more verbs: `aws ssm send-command`, `az functionapp deployment source config-zip`, `az aks command invoke`, `uv remove`, `poetry remove`, `yarn global remove`, `conda env remove`, `gem uninstall`; (s) the reader pass asks git for the current branch once per commit or push it finds (four git commands in one line take 0.06 s against 0.04 s at 0.6.1); (t) the tables of the reader do not name `unshare`, `ltrace` or `gdb --args` as tools that run a command (the first limit above: `unshare -n rm -rf build` is silent on every rule); (u) the Homebrew names of the GNU tools: `grm -rf build`, `grmdir`, `gunlink` and `gshred -u f` are silent (`gtruncate`, `gfind … -delete` and `gxargs rm` ask, through the text expressions). None of these is new in 0.7.0, except the cost in (s); revisit: on the owner's yes, (m) first, then (u).

## 2026-11-17 — the stack is merged: seven pull requests, merge commits, run by the agent on the owner's instruction

- The seven pull requests of the stack (#41 to #46, and #48 with guard 0.7.0) were merged into `main` from the bottom up with merge commits, each after its base had been moved to `main`, so that it held only its own commits (1, 1, 1, 15, 3, 10 and 31); these are the first merge commits of this repository, where every earlier pull request was merged by rebase (137 commits, none of them a merge); why: a rebase merge writes every commit anew, so the hashes that this log, the commit messages and the review receipts cite would not exist on `main`, and in a stack each next pull request still carries the commits below it, which a rebase merge would have to drop or write a second time (not tried); with merge commits `main` holds the reviewed commits themselves, and `git log --first-parent main` lists the seven pull requests; alternative not taken: rebase merges from the top down, each pull request into the branch below it and #41 last with all 62 commits (a linear history, but new hashes, and six pull requests recorded as merged into a branch); chosen by the owner on 2026-11-17, asked with both options; evidence: the tree of `main` after the last merge is identical to the reviewed head `7d14c6b`; CI ran on each of the seven merge commits and every run passed; the last slice of guard 0.7.0, the two commits after `71ddcfb`, was reviewed before the push (APPROVE, no finding, $0.19, receipt `7d14c6bbdea7854e470e930b8cad71a0dc5a11f1.no-fixtures.json`; $3.79 for the six reviews of that branch); cost: none; compliance: what was reviewed is what was merged, hash for hash (ALCOA+ original); revisit if: the owner wants a linear history back (then single pull requests by rebase as before, and a stack from the top down).
- The agent ran the seven merges, with the owner's token, on the owner's instruction of 2026-11-17: asked who should run them, he chose that the agent does; the other option was a prepared script that he starts himself. The instruction is in the transcript of Claude Code session `94132852-ba43-4819-82c0-abc5ed99d729` on the owner's machine (his message "also merge it", then his answers "You merge for me" and "Merge commits (Recommended)" to the session's two questions); it is not on the forge unless the owner confirms it there, for example with a comment on #48. GitHub records `kacper38` as the one who merged (#41 `53a6831`, #42 `2aceaed`, #43 `e785a73`, #44 `e156ac8`, #45 `e1204bf`, #46 `aa91036`, #48 `9a817f3`; 13:44:30 to 13:45:19 UTC); what that gives up: this kit counts the merge as the human approval, `tracker owner done` reads who merged from the forge and refuses a pull request that no human merged, and for these seven the forge cannot tell the owner's hand from an agent with his token; plan rev 3 says "No agent decides a merge" and that a local session is not meant to merge either (a working-agreement rule; fact 22 of the plan: only the working agreement stops a session, the guard allows `gh pr merge`): the decision was the owner's and the agent carried it out, which is an exception to that rule, made by the owner; the session named the approval point in its question and read the plan's sentence only afterwards, while writing this line; the pull request that carries this block (#49) is merged the same way on the same day: asked who merges it, with his own merge as the recommended option, the owner chose the agent again ("You merge it"); compliance: this line is the record of who did what (ALCOA+ attributable), and an instruction in a session is weaker evidence than a merge by the owner's own hand or an approving review; revisit when: agent sessions have their own identity and the approval is a required check (steps 2.1 and 2.2 of the plan), so that an agent's merge shows as the agent's and can only merge what the checks already allow.
- The tracker record of these changes is incomplete: #37, #38, #40 and #47 (opened by the session on 2026-11-17 for guard 0.7.0) are in `status/backlog`, because `owner ready` is the owner's verb and was not run before the work or before the merge; the pull requests name their items with `Refs`; what remains: `owner ready` for the four, then `claim` and `review` by the agent (`review` accepts a pull request that is already merged), then `owner done`; the time stamps will show the scope approval after the merge, and the approvals themselves, given between 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-16, are in this log; open until: `owner done` has run for all four (`tracker list --all` then shows them as done); from the next change on, the item is made ready before the first commit.

## 2026-11-17 — guard 0.7.1: egress-guard names Hugging Face and ten more inference API hosts

- Hugging Face is not integrated into the kit: no dependency, no hosted inference as a reviewer, no MCP server (the session's recommendation of 2026-11-17 to the owner's question whether to integrate it; he took the three follow-ups of this block from it and asked for nothing more); why: the kit is change control, each of the three adds an outside connection, and Inference Providers hand a request on to third-party hosts (the Inference Providers page of the Hugging Face docs, read that day: "each request goes to a provider serving the model"), so the retention terms that D15 wants quoted before a first use would multiply; cost: none; revisit if: the owner wants a further model family for the second reviewer in open mode.
- Inside a client boundary `egress-guard` now asks for the Hugging Face CLI (`hf`, and `huggingface-cli`, the name the tool had before; it is not on the docs page read that day and is kept for installs that still carry it) and for `curl`, `wget`, `http`, `https` and `xh` to `router.huggingface.co`, `*.endpoints.huggingface.cloud` and `*.hf.space`; why: measured on guard 0.7.0 on 2026-11-17, `hf upload <repo> <folder>`, `huggingface-cli upload …` and a `curl` with a request body to each of these hosts were silent, while `gemini …` and `curl https://api.openai.com/…` asked, so a client's material could go to another vendor's endpoint without a prompt; the three hosts were read in the Hugging Face docs the same day (Inference Providers, the Inference Endpoints guide of `huggingface_hub`, "Embed your Space"); compliance angle: the client boundary (data stays in the agreed region and tenant) and the decision log, which now holds an `ask` line for these calls; cost: none; revisit if: a client agrees a Hugging Face endpoint as its own (limit (5) below).
- Every call of the CLI asks, a download too (owner, 2026-11-17: "every call"); the other option put to him was a pass list for `download`, `cache`, `env` and `version`; why: two names in a set, the treatment `ollama` and `openai` already get, and no list of verbs to keep in step with a CLI whose guide showed about twenty command groups that day; the price: `hf download <repo>` asks although nothing of the client's leaves, and in the Copilot cloud agent an ask is a deny; revisit if: such an ask shows up in the decision log of a client repository.
- The bare `huggingface.co` stays out of the host list, so a download stays silent (`curl -L https://huggingface.co/<repo>/resolve/…`, `wget`, `git clone`; a row and the probe hold this). `api-inference.huggingface.co`, the earlier serverless inference host, is left out as well: on 2026-11-17 the name has no address record (`dig`: status `NOERROR`, no answer), so no request reaches it; revisit if: the name answers again.
- Second commit, on the owner's word the same day ("second commit now"): ten more API hosts, each read in the vendor's own documentation and resolved by DNS on 2026-11-17: `api.together.ai` (the Together docs use it now; the list had only `api.together.xyz`, which still resolves and stays), `api.groq.com`, `api.deepseek.com`, `api.x.ai`, `api.cohere.com`, `api.fireworks.ai`, `api.cerebras.ai`, `api.perplexity.ai`, `api.replicate.com`, `api.deepinfra.com`. Hosts that can be a client's agreed endpoint (Azure OpenAI, Bedrock, Vertex AI) stay out, as before. The list is a list: a vendor it does not name is silent; revisit if: a decision log or a review shows a call to one.
- How it was checked: rows before the change (16 ask rows and 3 allow rows per harness; 12 and then 20 ask fixtures failed before the matching change), `ruff check tools` and `pytest` on Python 3.13 and 3.9 (4,347 passed, 9 skipped on both; 4,309 before), and a probe through `guard.py hook pre` with 33 forms that are in no row (other clients, an absolute path, a pipe, the own boundary, look-alike hosts): 32 gave the expected verdict, and the one that did not is limit (1).
- What it does not cover (measured on 2026-11-17; (1) and (2) hold for the old entries too, checked with `gemini` and `api.openai.com` on 0.7.0): (1) the rule sees a tool at the head of a simple command and behind `sudo`, `env`, `nohup` or an assignment (`X=1 hf …`), and no further: `bash -c 'hf upload a b'`, `timeout 5 hf upload a b`, `docker run --rm img hf upload a b`, `ssh host hf upload a b` and `xargs hf upload …` are silent; criterion 5 of tracker item #50 (guard 0.8.0) is this gap; (2) the host expression is case-sensitive: `curl https://API.GROQ.COM/…` and `curl https://API.OPENAI.COM/…` are silent, although a host name is not case-sensitive for the client; outside the scope the owner approved, put to him as a proposal; (3) a call from inside a program is no shell command: `python3 send.py` with an SDK is silent, for every vendor; (4) a write to the bare `huggingface.co` through `curl` is silent, the price of silent downloads; (5) a dedicated endpoint that a client has agreed as its own asks every time, because the guard has no list of agreed hosts.
- Deferred, noted by the owner for later ("make note to do 2,3 later"), nothing built: the provenance rules for open models in `skills/rag-stack` (tracker item #51) and the identity of a local model in the second reviewer's receipt, D15 (tracker item #52); both are in `status/backlog`, and each needs a plan and his `owner ready`.
- Where this change left the usual path: (a) no tracker item for the change itself: the owner left the question to the session ("however you recommend") and asked for delivery and merge in one go ("just deliver and merge it"), and an item would have needed his `owner ready` before the first commit; the pull request and this block are the record, as for #46; (b) the code was written by the session model (Claude Fable 5.2) and not by an Opus engineer, as the routing table has it for code: nine lines added and two removed in `guard.py` (two lists and the version) and 21 lines of rows; a fresh Opus context reviews the diff before the merge (`bin/fresh-review`; the pull request names the receipt); (c) the owner named the agent as the one who merges this pull request, with a merge commit and his token, as for the stack (the block above): an exception for this pull request, and his message is in the transcript of Claude Code session `d2421b89-4b5e-4224-a514-240a5fb0baec` on his machine.

## 2026-11-18 — guard 0.8.0, group A: a secret path or a gate bypass written another way is read like the plain form

- Scope, as the owner set it on 2026-11-17 ("A: secrets and bypasses, B: tables, C: fewer prompts and cost"): two pull requests against `main`, A first, then B together with C; only additions: nothing that asked or denied at 0.7.1 may stop doing so (group C's list of false asks is the one exception, and it is not in this change); out of scope: a command held in a variable, and anything that needs a sandbox. Group A is tracker item #50: eight shell rules and the three secret rules already guard a secret file, a skipped commit gate, a forced push, a tracker bypass and another vendor's LLM tool, and each could be passed by writing the same thing another way; why: measured on 0.7.1 against rows written before the build, 304 of 597 shell rows and 34 of 66 file rows were silent or weaker than their plain form (`docker run --env-file=.env …`, `echo 'git commit --no-verify -m x' | sh`, `gh -R o/r issue close 3`, `git push -uf origin feat`, `HUSKY=0 git commit -m x`, `timeout 60 codex exec …` inside a client boundary, `SUDO rm -rf build`, `Read .ENV`); now all 597 and all 66 hold; cost: none (0 CHF/month); compliance: the commit gate, the tracker, the secret files and the client boundary are controls only as far as a respelled command cannot pass them, and the decision log holds an `ask` or `deny` line for these forms now; revisit if: the replay over real commands (owed, see below) shows more than a handful of new prompts a week.
- What 0.8.0 reads now. `secret-shell`: a secret path as the value of `--name=value`, as the rest of a short option glued to its value (`-T.env`, `-i~/.ssh/id_ed25519`), behind `@` (`-d @.env`, `file=@".env";type=…`), and as the source of an input redirection, also glued to the word in front (`cat <.env`, `cat<.env`, `<>`); the value of an option that names a pattern stays out (`--exclude=.env`, `--glob`, `--filter`); `git add|stage` of a secret file denies behind git's own options (`git -C . add .env`). The reader: a script fed to a shell is read like the script of `bash -c` by every shell rule: a here-document of `sh bash zsh dash ksh fish ash`, `$SHELL`, `sudo -s|-i` or `su` without a script of their own, of `ssh host` with no command or with a quoted remote command that runs such a shell, and the body of a `cat` without a file or the words of an `echo` or `printf` in front of one pipe (`|` or `|&`) into one of these; a body is data wherever no shell reads it (`cat <<EOF > notes.txt`, `git commit -F - <<EOF`, `python3 - <<EOF`, and `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF' … EOF)"`, the form this harness commits with); a `<<-` body ends at a closing line indented by tabs. `tracker-guard`: `gh` with `-R`/`--repo` before or after the command group, and `gh api` with the method and the write flags glued or with `=`. `git-guard`: short options in one word (`-uf`, `-nm x`, with the letters that take a value read as such), a long option cut to a prefix git accepts (`--no-verif`, `--force-with-leas`, `--mirro`; a prefix two options share is refused by git itself, so the over-match costs nothing), the hook managers' off switches in the environment of a git call that runs hooks (`HUSKY=0`, `LEFTHOOK=0`, `SKIP=…`: as an assignment in front, through `env`, exported earlier in the line, also by `declare -x`, `typeset -x`, `set -a` or a later `export NAME`, and in front of a shell whose script holds the git call), and `core.hooksPath` set for one call (`-c`, `--config-env` as one word or two, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_<n>`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`); `git config` that sets or unsets `core.hooksPath`, or removes or renames the section `core`, asks; the kit's own `.githooks` is the one silent value. `delete-guard`: `git push -d`, `--delet`, `--prune`. `egress-guard`: the reader pass with the full reading, so another vendor's tool behind a wrapper, in a nested or fed script, in a container or over `ssh` asks inside a client boundary.
- Capitals, added by three answers of the owner on 2026-11-17 after the body of #50 was frozen ("0 do 0.8": an LLM host in capitals asks like the plain host; "group a": a tool name in capitals is read like the plain name; "yes": a secret file name in capitals is classed like the plain name); why: his file system ignores case, so `GIT`, `/usr/bin/GIT`, `SUDO` and `BASH -c` run the real tools and `.ENV` opens `.env` (measured on his machine that day), and every table and expression of the guard compared exact case. The design: two readings, not a `.lower()` at each comparison. Each of the eight shell rules answers for the command as written and, when a known tool name is written with a capital letter, also for the text with those names folded to the spelling the tables use; a deny from either wins and asks are combined, so nothing can get weaker, and matches that are case-sensitive on purpose stay so (the SQL keyword `TRUNCATE`, `$SHELL`). Only a known name is folded: never a verb, an option, a file name or the value of an assignment (`FOO=GIT`). `secret_class` takes the stricter class of the path as written and of the path in lower case, so `.env.EXAMPLE` keeps asking, `.ENV.EXAMPLE` stays silent and `~/.AZURE/prod.tfvars` is in a secret directory. The host expression of `egress-guard` ignores case. What it leaves: a name no table holds (`GFIND`, `GXARGS`: the 4 weaker pairs of 2,802, group B), a verb in capitals (`git PUSH` is refused by git; `brew UNINSTALL` works on macOS and passes: open), a shell builtin in capitals (the shell does not find it; reading it like the builtin is harmless), `npm INSTALL x` asks because `install` is a known name, and a command with a capital name is read twice; the price the owner accepted: on a case-sensitive file system `.ENV` is another file than `.env` and asks all the same; revisit if: a client's build agents show these asks in the decision log.
- The rows, and what grew after `owner ready`: 229 rows frozen with #50 and 10 added the same day inside its second criterion (a script for another host), then 358 shell rows and 66 file rows for the three answers on capitals, which came after the owner had run `owner ready`; this line is the record that the acceptance set of #50 grew by his words and not by the session's own count. The fix passes added 111 shell rows and 12 file rows from the review. On 0.7.1, 293 of the 597 shell rows and 32 of the 66 file rows held; at the head all 708 shell rows and all 78 file rows hold, and every one of them is a case of the test module `tools/guard/tests/test_bypass_forms.py`.
- A correction of the record: item (p) of the 0.7.0 block names `git push --repo origin feat:main` as a push `git-guard` misses. It is no missed push: git reads the word that follows `--repo <name>` as the repository and not as a refspec (measured on 2026-11-17 with git 2.55.0), so that command pushes nothing to `main`, and `git push --repo=origin` is judged as before. The short option cluster and the ways around the hook gate that item (p) also names are closed by this change.
- How it was built: test first, against the rows, one commit per step, by one Opus engineer (`python-engineer`). Steps 0 to 2 were built on the owner's machine on 2026-11-17 and stopped there on his word; steps 3 to 11 were built on 2026-11-18 in a Claude Code cloud session by a fresh engineer, because the first one's context could not be resumed there (cost: it read the brief and the code again; 461,307 subagent tokens for steps 3 to 11 and both fix rounds, by the harness's count). The orchestrating session checked every commit before it pushed it: the rows, forms the engineer never saw, and the rows of groups B and C that must not move.
- How it was reviewed: one separate Opus agent with the reviewer's role and a brief of its own, three looks (378,583 subagent tokens), and it fixed nothing. First look, on the build: BLOCKER, 18 findings (1 blocker, 7 major, 10 minor). The blocker was weaker than 0.7.1: since `git config core.hooksPath …` asks, `git-guard`'s first pass could return an ask, and its loop returned on the first verdict, so `git config core.hooksPath /tmp/h; git commit --no-verify -m x` asked where 0.7.1 denied; the rows had no line with an ask in front of a deny. One fix pass by the same engineer closed it first and alone, then 14 more findings and three of the session's own (`ssh build 'bash -s' <<EOF`, `--config-env` as two words, the `<<-` closing line). Second look, on the fix pass: its own findings closed, and four new ones that the fix pass had brought in: a here-document inside `"$( … )"` was lexed as commands when its body held a quote, so a commit message that quoted a command made `git-guard` deny the commit (the form this harness commits with); an apostrophe in a comment or in `$'…'` did the same to the next data here-document; a chain of `ssh` hops took time that doubled per hop (1.2 s at 16 hops, 18.8 s at 20); and the environment in front of one shell reached a sibling script. A second fix round closed the four. Third look: all four closed, nothing weaker than 0.7.1 or than either earlier head, two minor findings left as they are (next bullet). Why a second round, where the plan of 2026-11-17 named one fix pass: the first one made the daily commit form deny, and that could not go to `main`; no agent was added for it; compliance: every finding, its probe and the verdict before and after are in the reviewer's three reports, kept with its probe files in `evidence/2026-11-18-guard-0.8.0-group-a/reviewer/`, and the rows of both rounds are tests.
- What the checks showed at the head (2026-11-18, a Linux container with Python 3.13): `ruff check tools` clean; 5,271 passed, 9 skipped (4,347 at 0.7.1); forms the engineer never saw: 138 of 152 hold (66 at 0.7.1) and the 14 others are outside the scope (last bullets); fed scripts: 14 payloads in 40 fed forms, 560 cells, none below its reference form (`bash -c '…'`, `ssh host '…'`, `docker exec … sh -c '…'`), 308 cells of data forms unchanged; capitals: of 2,802 pairs of a command and the same command with a name in capitals, 4 are weaker (232 of 2,354 at 0.7.1); over 9,822 commands in three contexts (a feature branch, `main`, a client boundary; 78,576 verdicts) no rule answers weaker than 0.7.1; each new code path was broken once in a scratch copy and a test failed every time (51 breakages); one hook call through the real entry point, median of five on that machine: plain 65 → 71 ms, 100 kB 459 → 491 ms, 100 kB with a name in capitals 466 → 1,010 ms (measured after step 10).
- What was not run before the merge, and why (named, not hidden): Python 3.9 has no interpreter in a cloud container, so the 3.9 run of this change is the pull request's CI job and not a local run; a static check of the 3.9 grammar and of a list of later names found nothing. The replay over 45 days of real commands, which criterion 6 of #50 asks for (nothing weaker, and how many prompts the build adds), needs the owner's transcripts on his machine: owed, and until it has run the number of new prompts in daily work is not known. The hook timing on his machine: owed. `bin/fresh-review` (a diff, through the egress screen, to one Opus context without tools) ran three times. On `04665e7`, the head before this block was added, against `main`: COMMENT, 4 findings, 0.91 USD; its major finding was that the docs name this block and the diff did not hold it, which the block's commit closed, and its three minor findings were measured and stand in the bullet on what the change does not cover. On `9cc43e1`, the head with this block, against `main`: COMMENT, 4 findings, 0.99 USD; two major ones, on the record and not on the code (the proof of the silenced ask lay only in the container: closed by the folder `evidence/2026-11-18-guard-0.8.0-group-a/`; a merge before the replay needs the replay or the owner's sign-off on the pull request: (b) in the next bullet), and two minor ones that were already listed. On `178055a`, over the three commits after `9cc43e1` only, because the whole diff had grown to 307 kB and the egress screen lets 200 kB through: COMMENT, 3 findings, 0.74 USD; its major finding is (e) in the next bullet, and its two minor ones are closed in the evidence folder (the two tools behind the reviewer's probes, the sums of the copied files). The commit that answers that third run was not read by a fresh context. The tracker verbs: `tracker.py review 50 --pr …` cannot run from a cloud session (GitHub's GraphQL API is not available there, and `gh issue view` needs it), so #50 stays `status/in-progress` until it is run on his machine; the item's label was not changed by hand.
- Where this change left the usual path. (a) It was built in a cloud session. Every edit under `tools/guard/` gets the `guard-config` ask, and in a cloud session an ask waits for the owner, so the build could not start; on 2026-11-18 the owner chose, in a dialog of that session ("Here, with the override", where the session had recommended his own machine), to create `.claude/guard.local.json` with `rules_off: ["guard-config"]` in the container, the setting his own machine has (the guard's own ask for that write: (e)); 140 calls ran with that ask silenced, each a `skipped` line of the container's decision log: 139 by the engineer (85 edits of `guard.py`, 48 of the test module, 2 of `make_fixtures.py`, the 4 fixture files) and 1 by the session (the version line); that log goes with the container, so the log, its join with the file each call touched, the dialog in the owner's words and both review receipts are kept in `evidence/2026-11-18-guard-0.8.0-group-a/`. Plan rev 3 says `rules_off` is ignored in a cloud session; that is not built, and this is the case it would refuse; revisit when rev 3 is decided. (b) A merge before the replay. The owner said in the same session that the agent may open the pull request and merge it after a review by a separate agent ("You can create pr and merge it after reviewing it on separate agent"). That review found that a merge before the replay needs the replay or his sign-off on the pull request, so the session opened the pull request and did not merge on its own reading of the earlier message; who merged, and on which word, is on the pull request. (c) The four fixture files of step 11 were written by hand and are compared byte for byte with the generator's output by `test_corpus_matches_generator`, because a run of `make_fixtures.py` asks (it holds delete forms as data) and an ask stops a cloud session. (d) Two fix rounds instead of one (above). (e) A gap of the control itself, found here and not fixed: in this cloud session an ask of the guard did not reliably stop a call. The guard asked about five calls of the session; the harness refused one at once, and four waited for the owner. Three of the four then ran right after a restart of the session's worker: the hook judged the waiting call again (`ask`), and the harness let it run and recorded decision `accept`, source `config`, reason `mode` (the session ran in the permission mode auto): a scratch write on 2026-11-17, the write of the override file on 2026-11-18, and a shell command with `rm -f` of two scratch files of the session, which the session had run without probing it first; the fourth was closed unanswered and did not run. No record names a person for the three, and only the owner can say whether he tapped. So an `ask` line of a cloud session in the decision log is no evidence of a human approval; why this change goes on all the same: the three calls are known and listed in the evidence folder, the one that mattered carried out the owner's own choice from the dialog, and the other two wrote and removed scratch files outside the repository; what would change it: plan rev 3, whose probe 4.1 takes for granted that an ask is not approved on its own, and a `deny` in place of an `ask` for the guard's own paths in a cloud session, which the harness cannot wave through; cost: none; revisit before the next build in a cloud session.
- What it does not cover (measured at the head; the guard reads text and prompts, it is no sandbox). Left by the review: a `case` pattern's `)` inside `"$( … )"` closes the walk's context early, so `git commit -m "$(case x in a) cat <<'EOF' … EOF ;; esac)"` with a quoted command in the body is denied (a false deny on a rare form; silent for `git-guard` at 0.7.1), and a shell fed inside such a `case` is not read (as at 0.7.1); 100 kB of comment lines that each hold `<<WORD` take 17.5 s where they took 0.2 s, and the budget turns that into an ask (about 45 kB reaches the 3.5 s budget, by the quadratic figure); a line of 100 kB with a name in capitals is read twice and `printf 'a;…' | sh` of that size passes the budget (3.7 s in process), which asks; `{ bash; } <<EOF`, `ssh build -T <<EOF` (an option behind the host) and `LEFTHOOK=false` are not read; `HUSKY=0 ssh build 'git commit -m x'` and `bash -c 'export HUSKY=0'; bash -c 'git commit -m x'` deny although the variable does not reach the git call; `--exclude-from=.env` names a file that is read and stays silent, by the decision on pattern options; `SKIP= git commit -m x` denies with an empty value. Left by the fresh-context review: `eval 'export HUSKY=0'; git commit -m x` and `eval 'set -a; HUSKY=0'; git commit -m x` stay silent as at 0.7.1, because an export inside a nested script is read after the commands of its line (`export HUSKY=0; git commit -m x` and `eval 'export HUSKY=0; git commit -m x'` deny); a NUL character anywhere in the command switches the reading of a here-document as a script off, since the reader marks the bodies with that character, and the command is judged as at 0.7.1 (`bash <<EOF` with `git commit --no-verify -m x` and a NUL is silent, without the NUL it denies; a script through a pipe is still read); a process argument cannot hold a NUL, so `bash -c` never gets such a command, but a shell that reads its command from standard input drops the character and runs the rest (measured with bash and sh), and no test holds the case; the test of the `ssh` chain asserts a wall-clock bound of 1.0 s on a reading that takes 0.005 s, so a runner 200 times slower would fail it. Outside the scope, as before: a script the guard cannot read from the line (`cat run.sh | bash`, `bash < run.sh`, `echo "$C" | bash`, `base64 -d x | bash`), `gh api graphql` mutations, `git update-ref` and `git commit-tree`, `printenv` and a bare `env`, `curl -F "notes=<.env"`, `docker build --secret id=env,src=.env`, an assignment in front of a command (`KEY_FILE=server.key ./run.sh`), `HUSKY_SKIP_HOOKS=1`, `python3 -m llm`, a `--no-verify` inside an `alias.*` value, `npx --no-install <tool>`, the tools that run a command and are in no table, the Homebrew names of the GNU tools, and `command -v codex`, which still asks inside a client boundary. Four questions are the owner's and undecided: the guard's own configuration path in capitals passes `guard-config` (9 of 9 spellings), the GitHub API host in capitals passes `tracker-guard`, a verb in capitals for `brew` and `gem`, and `gh pr merge`, which no rule reads.
- Decided on 2026-11-17 for the second pull request, groups B and C (the owner's "do decisions as recommmended" on five recommendations; nothing of it is built, and draft rows are on the working branch `docs/handover-2026-11-17`). (3a) Group C takes these asks away: `find . -name yarn`; `git restore --staged f`; a search pattern such as `grep -rn .env docs`; the previews `git clean -n`, `kubectl delete --dry-run=client`, `git worktree prune --dry-run`; a help probe such as `rm --help` when the help word is the sole argument; `ls -la .env`, `stat .env`, `test -f .env`, `[ -f .env ]`; `git check-ignore .env`, `git ls-files .env`, `git status .env`; `tar --exclude .env`, `echo .env >> .gitignore`, `command -v codex`; and keeps `find . -name .env`, `cat ca.pem`, `cat fullchain.pem`, `chmod 600 .env`, `cp .env.example .env`, `touch .env`, `git reset f`, `git rm --cached f`, `git branch -d x`, `wc -c .env`, `ssh-add`. (3b) Group B gets a safety net beside the tables: `git`, `gh` and `az` are judged wherever the name stands as a word, and the answer is an ask when the first word is unknown; nothing like it for `rm`. (3c) Gaps found while measuring: `gh api graphql` mutations go into B (issue and label mutations deny, others ask); `git update-ref` on a protected branch yes, `git commit-tree` no; `cat run.sh | bash` and `base64 -d x | bash` ask "could not read"; commands that print a secret from a store go into B as a table; `az` writes go into B. Deferred: code in another language gets its own plan; `aws` and `gcloud` writes come after the pilot; `printenv` and a bare `env` are left; why recorded here: the next pull request starts from these words, and the handover they were written down in is on a branch that is not meant to merge.

## 2026-11-18 — guard 0.8.0, group A: the replay and the hook timing on the owner's machine, owed since the merge of #54

- The replay that criterion 6 of #50 asks for ran on the owner's machine on 2026-11-18 at 15:09 UTC, after the merge. `a_mac_closeout.sh` (branch `docs/handover-2026-11-17`, folder `docs/handover-2026-11-17/g080-heldout/`, run at `f9e40af`) takes guard 0.7.1 and guard 0.8.0 out of git (`e5826b7`, `6ff07cf`), tests the replay tool on a made-up transcript with a known result (7 calls, 5 stronger occurrences in 3 cells: found), and hands every recorded call of a tool the hook is wired for to the 12 rules both guards have; nothing is run, and `fan-out` is left out because it counts spawns and would count the replay. The window asked for was 45 days. It holds every transcript on that machine, and they reach back 36 days: 577 files from 72 project folders, the first call on 2026-10-14, calls on 14 days. Over 18,061 calls (Bash 13,693, Read 2,141, Edit 1,246, Write 918, Agent 63; 17,636 distinct, 211,632 verdicts) no rule answers weaker than 0.7.1 with the own boundary, and none inside a client boundary (a second pass a moment later, three calls more); no rule crashed. The zero is no empty result: over the same calls 1,969 verdicts of guard 0.7.1 with the own boundary are an ask or a deny, 1,276 of them from `host-install`, which the replay judges as on a developer machine (counted in a third pass over 18,069 calls: the session's own commands enter the transcripts while it works).
- What 0.8.0 adds in daily work, by the same replay: 2 asks with the own boundary in those 36 days, and 10 more when the same calls are judged inside a client boundary; no new deny. By kind, read on that machine from the local logs, which hold real commands and stay there: (1) `git-guard`, 2 calls: a real `git -C <scratch repository> config core.hooksPath .husky`, the installer's own test of a foreign hook path. ## 2026-11-18 — plan rev 3: step 1.7 is pulled forward and takes the harness's own GitHub tools

- Decided by the session on the owner's word of 2026-11-18 ("do 3,4 for me", block above): point 4 of the list was this decision, and the session had advised taking it before the next build in a cloud session. He reads it in the pull request that carries this block and can strike it there. (a) Step 1.7 of `docs/PLAN-2026-11-orchestration-ci.md` is the next change to the guard after guard 0.9.0 (#57), ahead of step 0.3 and steps 1.1 to 1.6; its row in the plan names no earlier step as a condition. It comes after #57 because that item brings the ask for a merge which this step turns into a deny in a cloud session, and because two builds do not change `guard.py` at once. It is built on the owner's machine. Until it is merged no build is started in a cloud session, and a cloud session that runs all the same keeps the two rules the session of 2026-11-17 and 18 followed: a call that would ask is not issued, and a merge waits for the owner's word in the chat. (b) The step also takes the harness's own GitHub tools (`mcp__github__…`). A call of such a tool gets the verdict of the same act in a shell or with a file tool where the guard has one (denied: an issue or label write, a file written on `main`, a secret path; asked: the guard's own paths and, from guard 0.9.0, a merge), and a tool with no such twin asks unless the step's plan names it as a read or as harmless; creating a branch, opening a pull request and changing its text are meant to stay silent, so that a cloud session can still hand over a branch and a pull request. The rule for the unnamed is the one the owner chose for `az` the same day. With the step's rule that every ask of a cloud session is a deny, such a session can then no longer merge or approve a pull request, switch on auto-merge, write an issue or a label, write a file on `main`, or write the guard's own paths or a secret path on a branch; why: #54 was merged through exactly these tools. In that session the hook did not run for them, because its matcher names the shell, file and agent tools; the guard would have said nothing had it run (15 made-up events, all silent); and the harness asked nobody, so what held the merge back was the session's own rule (`docs/HANDOVER-2026-11-18.md` on the branch `docs/handover-2026-11-17`, "The harness's GitHub tools pass every rule"). The deny for `gh pr merge` that the step names closes a way that session could not take at all (`gh` had no valid token there) and leaves the one it took. And an ask is no stop there: three of four asked calls ran after a restart of the session's worker (first block of 2026-11-18, (e)), which is why the step does not wait for its place in Phase 1; cost: none per month. In work: the plan sizes steps 1.7 and 1.8 together at about half a working day, and the tools add the matcher in both harness files, a table from tool name to act and fixtures, by the session's estimate a quarter of a day more (not measured). In use: changes to the guard and merges leave the cloud, and the build of 2026-11-17 and 18 would have stopped at its first edit under `tools/guard/`; compliance: this kit counts a merge as a person's approval and reads it from the forge. After the step a merge, an issue write or a write to the guard's own files out of a cloud session is refused wherever the hook sees the call, with a deny line in the decision log for each (audit trail); the forge goes on naming the owner for what a session does with his token (Q2, step 2.1); revisit if: a probe shows that no hook is called for those tools (then their rule comes out of the step and step 2.2 is pulled forward in its place), the harness gets a switch of its own for these tools that a repository can set, or cloud sessions are no longer used for this kit (then the step goes back to its place in Phase 1).
- What this does not give, and what stays open: the step is still "a guard change, so it waits for the owner's go-ahead at the step" (plan, row 1.7); this block moves it forward and widens it and starts nothing. Next comes its plan with acceptance rows and a tracker item, and nothing is built before he has approved that plan and run `owner ready` for the item. Not measured: whether a hook is called for a tool of the harness itself once the matcher names it. Until a cloud session the owner starts by hand shows a refused call, as probe 4.1 would, the rule for these tools rests on fixtures, as the plan already says of the step's cloud branch. Named residual, unchanged: the guard and the hook configuration are files in the session's checkout, and a session with a shell can rewrite them (D11); the boundary is the ruleset on `main` with required checks and the recorded approval (step 2.2), which is not built. That the harness let three asks run after a restart looks like a defect of the harness and is worth a report to its vendor (handover, same section); that report is the owner's to send.

## 2026-11-18 — the owner's standing word: a session merges its own pull requests until step 2.2, and a plan is approved once per phase

- Asked and answered. On 2026-11-18 at 19:00 UTC the owner wrote in a session on his machine: "can I just ask you to finish that without asking me for permissions every 5 minutes ?" (transcript of Claude Code session `423d9d67-30e5-48ed-af65-0a06ef4b93d8`). The session put one dialog of two questions to him, and he answered both at 19:06 UTC. First question: "In agent-change-control, may I merge my own pull requests without asking you, once CI is green and the fresh-context review has no open finding?", offered "Yes, all, until step 2.2 (Recommended)", "Yes, except control changes" and "No, ask me per PR"; he chose "Yes, all, until step 2.2 (Recommended)", under which the dialog said: "Covers guard, hook and settings changes too. Each merge is recorded in DECISIONS.md as made by an agent under your account on your standing word. It ends when the bot identity and the approval check (plan steps 2.1 and 2.2) exist. You can revert any merge." Second question: "How should plan approval work from now on in agent-change-control (today: a plan and a go-ahead per step, and a separate go-ahead for each guard change)?", offered "One plan per phase (Recommended)", "No approval, just record" and "Per step, as today"; he chose "One plan per phase (Recommended)", under which the dialog said: "I write one plan per phase (next: Phase 1, starting with guard step 1.7). You approve it once, and that is also the go-ahead for its guard steps. Open questions inside the phase I settle with the option I would recommend and record them in DECISIONS.md for you to strike later." Three more messages of his followed in the same session while this block was being written and reviewed: at 19:14 UTC "it seems you run a lot of commands that are classified risky, thats why maybe better idea would be to let you run in container, when I dont care that much ?>", at 19:17 UTC "because I accept most commands anyway, id rather have you work uninterrupted...", and at 19:23 UTC "i mean to loosen rules now, deliver that repo and then use them ...".
- What the first answer decides. A session may merge a pull request it built in this repository without asking him when two things hold on the head that is merged: the checks the pull request runs are green, and `bin/fresh-review` has left no open finding. Changes to the guard, the hooks and the settings are included by the text he chose. Changes to the workflows are not: that text names three kinds where the second option named four ("Pull requests that touch the guard, hooks, settings or workflows still wait for your click."), and the workflow files decide which checks a pull request runs, so a pull request that touches `.github/workflows/` still waits for him until he says otherwise. So does one that needs the label `test-removal-ok`, which the job `guards` asks for when a test file or a test function is removed. What a pull request merged by a session can still change about its own gate, named: the scripts the checks run (`tools/testguard/`, `tools/commit_msg.py`) and what a test that stays asserts; only the review stands against those. "No open finding" is read as he was asked: every finding the review gives is fixed, or shown in the pull request not to hold, before the merge; a finding that holds and is left, whatever its severity, keeps the merge with him. A fix that changes code, a test or a check gets a new review; a fix in prose does not. The drafts of this block were first looser than his answer (a MINOR finding could stay if the pull request named it; the first review marked that) and then tighter (a new review after every fix, and a stop at the third review); the tighter draft went after his message of 19:23 UTC. It ends when steps 2.1 and 2.2 of `docs/PLAN-2026-11-orchestration-ci.md` are in force (the agent identity and the approval check); until then it stands in place of his exceptions per pull request of 2026-11-17 and 2026-11-18 (blocks above), and the rule of plan rev 3, D14, "No agent decides a merge", is set aside in this repository for that span; a note at the head of the plan points here, and the plan's text is otherwise unchanged. Every pull request merged under it adds a line here before the merge that names the session as the merger, and he can revert any of them. The pull request that carries this block is the first, and this sentence is its line: the session of the transcript named above merges it with the owner's token, and its checklist names the head, the checks and the three reviews.
- What the three later messages add. The rules this repository ships are for use once it is delivered; while it is being built he wants them loosened so that the work is not held up, and he would rather have a session run uninterrupted, in a container where he does not mind what it runs. Read by the session as: (a) where this block had read his answers more tightly than he gave them, the tighter reading goes (bullet above); (b) a container to run sessions in becomes the first step of the Phase 1 plan; the block of 2026-11-16 on installs names the container route as not existing yet; (c) nothing else is loosened by these messages alone. Which further rule is relaxed while the repository is built (a tracker item for every pull request, the owner verbs, the limit of five agents) is proposed in the Phase 1 plan and decided by his approval of it.
- What the second answer decides. The session writes one plan per phase of that plan document and he approves it once. The approval is the go-ahead for every step of the phase, guard changes included; the rows of steps 1.7 and 4.2 had left those to a go-ahead at the step, and so had the block above on step 1.7. A question that comes up inside an approved phase is settled by the session with the option it would have recommended and is recorded here, marked as his to strike. `AGENTS.md`, non-negotiable 1, is unchanged: a plan is still presented and approved before code is written, and what changes is its grain, a phase where it was a step. The next plan is the one for Phase 1, which starts with step 1.7. Step 0.3, the one step of Phase 0 that is still open, is taken into the Phase 1 plan and approved with it; step 4.2 is approved with the plan of Phase 4.
- What it does not change. The tracker's owner verbs (`owner ready`, `owner done`) stay with him at his terminal: the tracker gives them to no session, and the session does not go round it. So do the acts that need the owner of the account (the GitHub App of step 2.1, the scratch repository and the token of step 0.3, the ruleset, the cloud probe of step 4.1), and deletes, deploys and force pushes. The limit of five agents without asking and the rule on installs (`AGENTS.md`) stand. The word is for this repository, whose boundary is `own`; a repository that holds client data gets nothing from it.
- why: his words; and the asks of the tools were no longer what interrupted him: the guard's log on his machine holds 13 answers of `ask` for 2026-11-16 to 2026-11-18 over every repository that wires the guard (3, 7 and 3 by day, counted at about 19:10 UTC on the 18th; the log is `~/.claude/telemetry/decisions.jsonl` there, read by its fields `ts` and `verdict`), while each pull request, each step and each open question went to him as a question of a session; cost: none in money beyond the fresh-context review a pull request gets ($0.38 to $1.37 a run in four measured runs, plan rev 3, section 11; the three runs on this block cost $0.96, $0.99 and $0.19, and their receipts are `fresh-review/<head>.json` in the git directory on his machine for the heads `3544448`, `de06676` and `e0b268d`; neither the log nor the receipts are in the repository); compliance: until step 2.2 a change can reach `main` in this repository with no act of a person on it. The session did not ask him for one: while a session works with his token the forge shows `kacper38` for both, so a comment or an approval under his account would show no more than the transcript does. ## 2026-11-18 — the owner switched the `fleet` verifier off for this repository on his machine

- Recorded, not decided by a session: on 2026-11-18 at about 19:42 UTC the owner set `FLEET_VERIFY=off` under `env` in this repository's `.claude/settings.local.json`, a file git does not track, by running one line the session gave him. Why: the verifier hook of the `fleet` plugin runs before every shell call, marks a script run by its path as risky, runs a check by a second model and then asks him even when the check finds nothing; measured in the transcripts that evening, two script calls of one session waited 111 and 255 seconds for his answer, and another session lost more than half an hour, 18 minutes of it on one `tracker.py claim`. Who: the session first tried the edit itself and auto mode's classifier denied it as an edit that lowers oversight of the session; the session did not go round the denial, and the owner made the change. What it gives up: in this repository on his machine nothing asks before a script file runs, also one that deletes, because the asks of `delete-guard` are already off here by his own local file (`.claude/guard.local.json`, `rules_off` with `guard-config` and `delete-guard`, since 2026-11-01); the rules of his user settings that ask on a typed `rm`, `git reset` and the like stay, and other repositories keep the verifier. Cost: none. The guard's log does not show these asks, so a count from that log alone understates how often he is interrupted; the session made that mistake twice that evening before it read the text of one prompt. What would change it: boxes in use, after which host sessions run few scripts and he can set the value back to `agent`; or a script run by path that does damage. Follow-up: neither this switch nor the `rules_off` of his local guard file is tracked by git, so nothing in the repository shows when one changes; step 1.6 of the Phase 1 plan gives each a dated row in `docs/RULES.md`, with 2026-12-31 as the latest end, which names them and cannot read them.

## 2026-11-19 — guard 0.9.0, groups B and C: tools, verbs and secret stores the tables did not name are read, and eleven kinds of false asks are gone

- Scope: tracker item #57, the second of the two pull requests the owner set on 2026-11-17 ("A: secrets and bypasses, B: tables, C: fewer prompts and cost"), built from his answers of 2026-11-18 (block of that day on groups B and C). Group B: the guard judged an act only where one of its tables named the tool, the verb or the store, so the same act through a name no table held was silent. Group C: eleven kinds of asks that fired where nothing the rule guards is read or changed. Outside group C only additions: nothing that asked or denied at 0.8.0 may stop doing so, apart from the cells a row written before the build asks for (bullet "The rows"). Out of scope, by the item: a cloud-session mode, a rule for a harness's own GitHub tools and a shell reading for `guard-config` (plan rev 3, step 1.7), `aws` and `gcloud` writes, code in another language, `printenv` and a bare `env`, ways to move a protected branch that are no commit and no `git update-ref`, a `|` inside a quoted word of a tool that is no search tool, and anything that needs a sandbox; why: measured on 0.8.0 against rows written before the build, 523 of the 968 shell rows and 36 file rows were red: silent where an ask or a deny was due (`unshare -n rm -rf build`, `grm -rf build`, `uv cache clean`, `mytool git push --force origin feat`, a `closeIssue` mutation through `gh api graphql`, `git update-ref refs/heads/main abc123`, `cat run.sh | bash`, `vault kv get secret/app`, `az group delete -n rg`, `gh pr merge 3`), or asking where the owner's list says silent (`ls -la .env`, `git clean -n`, `rm --help`); at the head all 1,004 hold; cost: none per month (0 CHF). In prompts, by the replay (bullet "The replay"): 27 new asks in 45 days, every one a merge of a pull request, and 2 asks fewer (18 fewer inside a client boundary); what the build cost is in the bullet on how it was built; compliance: the decision log now holds an `ask` or `deny` line for a merge, a GraphQL write, a stored secret that is printed, an `az` write and a script the guard could not read, each of which ran unrecorded at 0.8.0; and an ask that fires on `ls -la .env` teaches the reader of that log to confirm asks unread, so the eleven kinds that go away make the asks that stay worth reading (ALCOA+: accurate); revisit if: the merge asks are confirmed unread (then a deny, as for the Azure DevOps twin), the `az` asks pass a handful a week where `az` is in daily use (then a list of write verbs), or a report that is silent now turns out to print the content of the file it names.
