No cap on the number of seats is built (the plan's "three at most" describes the setup, it is no mechanism). why: the host and each box are separate workers under one login, and one item each keeps the rule's point (one branch per worker). Compliance: a seat comes only from a root-owned file, and each claim records it. Residual: on the host, whoever can write `/etc/agent-box/box.json` (root) gets a second seat, and in exchange loses the owner verbs there. revisit if: boxes run under logins of their own; then the seat is the login.
- D7 and D8, version and tests. Version 0.1.0 -> 0.2.0; the usage lines show the new forms. Tests reach the marker only through `BOX_MARKER` patched into `tmp_path` (an autouse fixture in `tools/tracker/tests/conftest.py`), so no test reads or writes `/etc`; this matters inside a box, where the real marker exists. Expected outputs first (`7476701`), then one commit per topic.

## 2026-11-20 — guard 0.10.0: the boundary only tightens, cloud launches are denied, the harness's GitHub tools are read (item #65, plan step 1.7)

- How it was built: held-out hash first (`bdff5d5`, host), the rows before any code (`04f2361`), the probe's two canaries (`ce0b309`) and its record (`e1ebdb6`, host, owner's Mac), then one commit per topic (`be2212a` test isolation, `193f51b` profile, `00582b1` boundary, `614c407` cloud-launch, `c235eb5` guard-config paths, `5e56c4d` shell writes, `cc46ea5` credentials, `5972ae3` refs, `1569abc` approval, `e3153a6` github-tools, `c35c554` matchers and tool kinds, `fe71a6e` version and docs; the box pushed the last three as `257c548`, `69d0def` and `a7590ae`, and the host reworded the subject of the first, 79 characters where the commit gate allows 72, which rewrote all three with an identical tree), built in a box with the design D1–D12 settled by the host session. Compliance angle for every line below: each deny in a cloud session leaves a decision-log line naming its profile, and every changed 0.9.0 verdict is listed in `tools/guard/tests/test_never_weaker.py` with the decision that made it.
- D1, the profile. What: `profile()` says `cloud` when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` is true (the truth test of `disposable_environment`), else `host`; `PROFILES` has one entry per profile (does `rules_off` count, what an ask becomes), so step 1.0b's `box` is one entry; in `run_hook`, where `rules_off` is applied, `host` keeps 0.9.0 exactly and `cloud` ignores `rules_off` from every source and turns the ask into a deny with the prefix `cloud session (CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE): …`; the `host_install` knob is no `rules_off` and still silences `host-install` under `auto`; every log line has `profile`. Nearest choice inside the scope: the table turns the combined ask, after `combine()`, not each ask before it, so a genuine deny keeps its own reasons and a converted ask is not joined to it (the row "a deny is unchanged in a cloud session" asks for this; the effect on every ask is the same). Why: nobody can answer an ask in a cloud session, and a silenced ask there would be a merge or a write with no person behind it. Revisit if: a cloud session gets a channel to the owner, or step 1.0b adds the box profile.
- D2, the boundary. What: a committed value other than `own` or `client` counts as `client`; `KZ_BOUNDARY=client` sets client, `own` changes nothing, any other value (`Client`, `OWN`) counts as client; a refused loosening and an unknown value print a note; `rules_off` needs the committed `own` and an effective `own`; the default without `guard.json` stays `own`. Why: whoever starts a session sets the variable, so it may only add care. Revisit if: the installer writes `boundary: client` by default (step 2.3 of the orchestration plan).
- D3, `cloud-launch`. What: deny `claude` with `--cloud`, `--remote` (or `=value`) or an argument that runs `/autofix-pr`, read through segments (also an unbalanced line) and the reader; `Agent`/`Task` with `isolation` remote (any case; not text: ask); the `schedule` skill (after a `plugin:` prefix and a `/`); a `Workflow` whose `script` or `scriptPath` file matches `isolation: 'remote'` (unreadable: ask); every `RemoteTrigger` call (decided here, not in the plan). `CronCreate`, `ScheduleWakeup` and `loop` stay silent. Host's answer after the first run: `DesignSync` asks through this rule's tool table, because it sends material to a service of the account and the guard cannot read what. Named limits: a `Workflow` by `name` is not resolved (step 1.2); a Workflow script that builds `isolation` at run time is not seen. Why: cloud work runs outside the boundary and outside these hooks. Revisit if: the CLI adds another launch form (the tool-list canary names a new tool).
- D4, matchers and tool kinds. What: the guard's pre and both post matchers in `.claude/settings.json` add `Monitor|ExitWorktree|Skill|Workflow|RemoteTrigger|DesignSync|mcp__github__.*` (the probe's `mcp-hook` holds, so the last part stays); `CLAUDE_TOOLS` gains `Monitor` (`shell`: every shell rule judges its `command`), `ExitWorktree` (`worktree`: `action: remove` asks through `delete-guard`, `keep` is silent), `Skill`, `Workflow`, `RemoteTrigger`, `DesignSync`; an `mcp__github__<op>` tool has kind `github`. `.github/hooks/guard.json` has no matcher, and a Copilot tool named `github-mcp-server-<op>` is read like `mcp__github__<op>`: unverified, fixtures only. Why: a hook that is not called judges nothing. Revisit if: a capture of the work harness shows the Copilot name form, or the probe's `mcp-hook` stops holding.
- D5, `github-tools`. What: the op after the prefix gets the verdict of its twin, built as an event and judged by the existing rules (deny wins), reason `github-tools: <tool> is the same act as <twin>: <reason>`: `get_*`/`list_*`/`search_*`/`*_read` silent, `get_file_contents` a `Read` of its path; branch, pull request, its text, comment reviews silent; merge, auto-merge and an approving review are their `gh` twins (ask); issue and label writes the `gh issue|label` twins `tracker-guard` denies; `create_or_update_file`/`push_files`/`delete_file` a `Write` of each path for `secret-write`, `guard-config`, `scratch-md` (never `protected-branch`), `git rm` for a delete, and a push to the branch (or `main` when it is missing) when that branch is protected; any other op asks; input it cannot read asks. Why: the same act through another door gets the same answer. Revisit if: the GitHub MCP server renames or adds write ops (they ask until named).
- D6, an approval. What: `gh pr review … --approve`/`-a` asks in `tracker-guard`, in the merge's style; `--comment` and `--request-changes` stay silent. Why: an approval counts as a person's; it also gives D5's approve tool a twin. Revisit if: the ruleset stops counting a session's approval.
- D7, `guard-config`, wider. What: also any file below `.github/workflows/`, `tools/testguard/`, `tools/scan/`, `tools/tracker/`, and `tools/commit_msg.py`, matched by path parts as `tools/guard` is (so `docs/tools/scan/x` asks too; the row of the first run that wanted it silent now names `docs/scan/x.md`); shell writes to every guarded path ask (redirections with `>|` and continued lines, `tee`, `cp`/`mv`/`install`/`ln`/`rsync` with `-t`, `dd of=`, `sed -i`, `perl -i`, `truncate`, `touch`, `chmod`/`chown`, `curl -o`, `wget -O`, `patch`/`git apply` by operand or by a diff header in a here-document, `git checkout <ref> -- <path>`, `git restore`, and inline `python -c`, `node -e`, `perl -e`, `ruby -e` code that names such a path and holds a write word), through the reader and both name foldings; reads stay silent. Named limits: a branch switch, merge, pull, rebase or stash that changes these files; a patch whose target is not on the line; obfuscated interpreter code; inline code that names a guarded path only to read it while writing elsewhere asks. Why: the guard and the gates are what keeps a session honest, and a shell was the open door. Revisit if: the false asks of inline code show up in the decision log.
- D8, the stored GitHub login. What: `secret-shell` denies `gh auth token`, `gh auth status -t|--show-token`, `git credential fill|approve`, `git credential-<helper> get` (any helper), `security find-*-password` with `-w`/`-g`, `security dump-keychain`, a shell read of `~/.config/gh/hosts.yml` or `~/.git-credentials` (matched by the path's end, any case), `unset GH_TOKEN|GITHUB_TOKEN`, `env -u` of them, an empty `GH_TOKEN=`, and `env -i` in front of `gh` or `git` (direct or through a shell's `-c`); `secret-read` denies the `Read` tool on the same files. Departures of the first run, accepted by the host: `gh auth token --help` stays silent; the empty assignment is read from the line's text. Why: a session holds its own scoped token; the owner's login reaches every repository. Revisit if: a session needs `gh auth status -t` for a diagnosis (it does not today).
- D9, the refs. What: `git-guard` denies `git update-ref` naming `refs/remotes/` (and `--stdin` when `refs/remotes/` is on the line), every writing form of `git replace`, `git symbolic-ref` writing or deleting under `refs/remotes/` (one operand is a read and stays silent); asks on `git remote set-url|rename|remove|rm`, `git config` writing a remote's `url`, `pushurl` or `fetch` (or dropping or renaming its section), and a fetch into an explicit `refs/remotes/` destination. Named limits: `git fetch . <sha>:<other ref>` and direct writes under `.git/` match nothing. Why: the guard and the tracker read `refs/remotes/` as the remote's state. Revisit if: a rule starts reading the remote's state over the network instead.

The change is prose in this file and in the plan's status; no workflow file and no test is touched.

## 2026-11-21 — guard 0.11.0: a box profile that logs or denies and never asks (item #66, plan step 1.0b)

- How it was built: held-out hash first (`ed62dcd`, host), the rows and their tests before any code (`72e540b`), the scratch-md fixture fix (`091e5b5`), then one commit per topic (`09642a3` facts and predicates, `b6c4059` configuration in a box, `9b30de8` local deletes, `c0201b6` the merge, `356c766` a row correction, `2b3a344` the profile, its table and the logged line, `a1f57fc` the wrapper, `157b60e` version and docs, `dfa28e5` the demonstration), built in a box with the design D1–D10 settled by the host session. Compliance angle for every line below: every act the profile lets run leaves a `logged` line in the decision log with the rule, the call id and the guard's version, so an auditor can list what ran unasked; every deny leaves a line naming profile `box`.
- D1, facts, one seam. What: `BOX_MARKER = "/etc/agent-box/box.json"`, `DOCKERENV = "/.dockerenv"`; `box_facts()` is the only code that touches them and gathers `dockerenv`, `marker_exists`, `marker`, `marker_uid`, `marker_writable`, `marker_dir_writable`, `guard_writable`, `euid`; any exception gives the facts of no marker; called once per hook call through the module global and passed on. Departure: the marker is read with `O_NOFOLLOW`, only when it is a regular file, and a marker over 64 kB counts as no JSON (`marker` None), each a tightening. Why: one seam the tests and the held-out rows replace. Revisit if: the marker's path or schema changes (`tools/box/marker.py`).
- D2, two predicates. What: `in_box` = `/.dockerenv`, a marker, and a guard file and folder this process cannot write; `box_valid` adds a root-owned marker neither it nor its folder writable, a guard not running as root, `schema: agent-box/1`, `boundary: own` in the marker and an effective boundary own. The `guard_writable` fact is no security check: it tells the judge (a read-only mount at `/judge`, the only copy the box's hooks run) from a working copy such as `/work/tools/guard/guard.py`, which the tests and the session run, so the work clone's test suite answers in a box as in CI; a session that makes a copy read-only gains nothing, because its hooks still run the judge. Why: criterion 2. Revisit if: the box's hooks stop running from a read-only judge.
- D3, configuration in a box (any marker, valid or not, since it only tightens). What: `load_config` reads `<guard root>/.claude/guard.json` (three folders above the guard's real path, `/judge` in a box), without its `rules_off` and without `guard.local.json`; the work clone's nearest `guard.json` counts only as a tightening to client; `KZ_RULES_OFF`, `KZ_PROTECTED_BRANCHES`, `KZ_SCRATCH_MD`, `KZ_HOST_INSTALL` are ignored, each with one stderr note; `KZ_BOUNDARY` (tightens only) and `KZ_TELEMETRY_DIR` are read. Without a marker `load_config` is the 0.10.0 code (only `KZ_BOUNDARY`'s reading moved into `_env_boundary`, unchanged). Departures: the work clone's `guard.json` counts as client for any `boundary` other than `own` (an unknown value tightens, as 0.10.0's D2), not only for `client`; `load_config` takes the facts as a second argument and reads them itself when none are handed in. Named residual: on the host `KZ_PROTECTED_BRANCHES` and `KZ_SCRATCH_MD` can still loosen (PR #76, D12 (e)); the box closes it only for the box. Why: a session must not configure its own judge. Revisit if: the host gets a judge copy of its own.
- D4, the profile. What: `profile(environ, facts, cfg)`: `cloud` when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` is true (it wins), else `box` when `box_valid`, else `host`; `PROFILES["box"] = {"rules_off": False, "ask": None, "table": BOX_TABLE}`; `run_hook`, `run_post` and `log_decision` pass the facts they have. Departure: a call with no facts (the one-argument call of 0.10.0) reads `box_facts()` and loads the configuration of the process's cwd; every caller in the guard passes both, so this path serves only outside callers. A box with an invalid marker runs `host` with D3's configuration, so its asks stay asks. Criterion 7: the probe of step 1.0a showed that an ask does not pass unanswered with permission checks off (`tools/orchestration/verified.json`, `box.questions.hook-ask-checks-off`: holds), so its condition is not met and nothing changed for it. Revisit if: that probe answer changes in a later CLI.
- D5, the box table. What: one entry per ask class (the rules of `RULES` and `unreadable-args`, `budget`): `log` for `delete-guard` (local deletes only), `guard-config`, `scratch-md`, `fan-out`; `merge` for `tracker-guard`; `deny` for every other; a missing class is a deny (`BOX_TABLE.get(name, "deny")`) and a test fails when the table's keys differ from the classes. A rule's deny is untouched; an ask a rule gives because it could not read the call (`_is_reader_reason`, built from `READER_MSG`, `_PIPE_NOT_READ`, the reader's stop messages and the crash text, with a test over each) is a deny whatever the class; any other ask is turned by its entry, a deny reason prefixed `box session: no one answers an ask here, so it is denied.` A call with a deny answers deny and logs each rule's verdict after the table; a call with only logged rules answers nothing (exit 0) and logs one `logged` line; no `skipped` lines in a box. Departure: each rule's ask is turned in the rules' child process (`_rule_verdict`), not in the parent after it, so the checks of D6 and D7 (the latter runs `git`) stay inside the 3.5 s budget; a rule that does not finish in time is the `budget` class, a deny. Why: no one answers an ask in a box, and a silenced ask would be an act with no record. Revisit if: a class turns out to need a person (it moves to `deny`).
- D6, local deletes. What: `_box_local_delete(ev)` re-reads the call with the rule's own expressions and readers (text, folded names, here-documents, each script the call names, every simple command) and answers True only when no form reaches past the box: the `rm` class, `find -delete`, git's local verbs, `update-ref -d refs/heads/…`, the code forms and `ExitWorktree` remove are local; any push that deletes or forces, `update-ref` outside `refs/heads/`, every tool of `TOOL_RE`/`_DELETE_TOOLS`, SQL, a shell fed by a pipe and any error are not. Departure: a script the call names that cannot be opened counts as holding nothing, as the rule itself scanned nothing there (the ask then comes from the line's own text, which is checked). Why: deleting files in the box's own clone is the work; the remote and the host's services are not the box's. Revisit if: the replay shows a local form denied.
- D7, the merge. What: a `tracker-guard` ask is logged only for one simple command `gh pr merge N --match-head-commit SHA` (or `=SHA`), with at most one of `--merge`, `--squash`, `--rebase`, nothing else, a full 40-character lower-case hash, and the newest `fresh-review` receipt for that hash under the git dir of the call's folder (`git rev-parse --git-dir`, 3 s) a JSON object with that `sha` and verdict `APPROVE` or `COMMENT`; else a deny naming the first condition that failed. The harness's merge tools (`mcp__github__merge_pull_request`, auto-merge) carry no head hash this check reads: denied by `github-tools`'s entry. Departure: a receipt over 1 MB counts as no receipt. Named limits: the receipt is a file in the box's own clone, which a session could write itself, so the check stops a forgotten review, not a hostile session (plan section 6, decision 7); GitHub enforces `--match-head-commit`, the guard does not compare the pull request's head itself. Revisit if: receipts are signed or written outside the box.
- D8, never weaker. What: `baseline-0.10.0.jsonl`, written by 0.10.0 (`6a1e3f6`) over the corpus of `test_never_weaker.py` plus the text tables of 0.10.0 (`SHELL_ROWS`, `TOOL_ROWS`; the docstring of `test_box_profile.py` says what is not covered and why); without a marker (injected, and the real `box_facts` here) every input answers exactly as 0.10.0; with a valid marker no deny changes and no ask remains; the table test; rows for every kind of plan section 4, the merge rows on scratch repositories with receipts. The 0.9.0 test is unchanged. Departure: `356c766`, after the rows, corrects one row: with a valid marker an input whose boundary is client gets no box profile (criterion 2), so it answers exactly as 0.10.0 and keeps its asks; the run's guard root carries the corpus's `host_install: ask`, since a box ignores `KZ_HOST_INSTALL`. Why: criterion 8. Revisit if: a later version changes a deny (it gets its own listed change).
- D9, the wrapper. What: `tools/box/box.py` has no `RULES_OFF` and `docker_argv` sets no `KZ_RULES_OFF`; `test_rules_off_names_only_rules_the_guard_has` keeps its name and checks both; the only mention left under `tools/box/` and `bin/` is the docstring that says it is gone. Plan section 5, row 1 ends here. Why: the profile replaces the silencing. Revisit if: never (a silencing comes back only through a new plan row).
- D10, version and docs. What: `VERSION = "0.11.0"`; `README.md` and `docs/SYSTEM.md` name the profile, its table, the merge form and receipt, the configuration beside the guard and the end of `KZ_RULES_OFF`. Named limit: the decision log lives in `/box-home/telemetry`, a folder the session can write, so its lines record and do not prove; the host's copy of the log and the pushed history are the evidence. Revisit if: the log moves to a folder the session cannot write.
- Demonstration (criterion 9), `tools/guard/tests/evidence-0.11.0-box.txt`: re-recorded in this box at `2864f83` (marker `root:root 0444`, marker judge `6a1e3f6e813d`, `/.dockerenv`, uid 1000, `KZ_RULES_OFF` still set by this run's wrapper and ignored), seven PreToolUse events through a read-only `mktemp -d` copy of the guard whose sha256 `192ea9c5fa4c` equals `HEAD:tools/guard/guard.py` `192ea9c5fa4c`, in a scratch repository cloned from `/work` that holds the judge's commit, head A adding `docs/demo-a.md` and head B changing `tools/guard/guard.py`, both from the judge's commit, nothing run: `rm -rf build` silent, exit 0, line `delete-guard: logged`; `git push origin --delete x` deny, exit 2, `delete-guard: deny`; `cat .env` deny, exit 2, `secret-shell: deny`; `gh pr merge 76 --merge --match-head-commit <A>` with no receipt deny, exit 2, `tracker-guard: deny`, reason "no fresh-review receipt exists"; the same after a COMMENT receipt for A silent, exit 0, `tracker-guard: logged`; `gh pr merge 77 --merge --match-head-commit <B>` with a COMMENT receipt for B deny, exit 2, `tracker-guard: deny`, reason names `tools/guard/guard.py`; the same after `git replace <B> <A>` (plain `git diff --name-only <judge> <B>` then shows only `docs/demo-a.md`) still deny, exit 2, `tracker-guard: deny`, reason names `tools/guard/guard.py`. Every line has `guard: 0.11.0`, `profile: box` and the call id. Replaces the record at `814a2e0`, which ran in a fresh repository without the judge's commit (review finding: no end-to-end record of the merge check against the real marker; the unit rows replace `box_facts`).
- Hook cost (one `hook pre` through the real entry point, read-only copies in this box, Python 3.13, median of five): `ls -la` 87.4 ms at 0.10.0, 87.0 ms at 0.11.0; the merge row 89.2 ms (ask) and 92.6 ms (logged, the receipt check's `git rev-parse` included). The budget test passes.

Held-out rows X19 and X20 had hashed a logged merge of a hash that is no commit in their repository; the file keeps them as written and their test names the change. A network lookup of the base was not taken: hooks never call the network (`AGENTS.md`, harness notes). A head built on a newer main than the judge is measured against the judge and may be denied for changes it did not make; that fails closed, and the owner merges it. Minors: the test-file check now also sees `test/` folders, `*_test.py` and `conftest.py`; a row covers a shell suffix run by another interpreter; named limit: a non-shell script whose `git` and `push` are more than 80 characters apart, or whose command is assembled at run time, is not seen by the script check, which reads text; held-out X19 and X20 lose their own coverage of a logged merge, which PATH_ROWS `docs` and `edit-a-test` keep.

The object store is not re-hashed: named limit, and this is where the hardening stops on purpose. The box merge check stops a careless merge of a change to the guard, CI or the tests, not a hostile session: such a session can forge objects, and it holds a token that merges through GitHub's API from Python with no shell form the guard reads (plan section 12: inside a box the guard is the only gate and it matches command text). Against a hostile session the controls are the token's scope (this repository, 30 days) and Phase 2's ruleset with its required review. ## 2026-11-21 — the task page (#77): a local, read-only HTML record of a task, planned by an Opus session

- What: plan `docs/PLAN-2026-11-dashboard.md`, approved by the owner on 2026-11-21 ("i agree to 77") with the four recommended answers of its section 6. One command writes one static HTML file from files already on the machine: tasks grouped by branch, sessions of the host and of the box, agents, every tool call joined with the guard's verdict on the call id, review findings and their re-tests, evidence paths, cost.
- Why a static file and not a console: a dashboard is attack surface (916 internet-exposed consoles of the ARTEX pentest tool on its ports 8787 and 8788 and trojanized images of it were reported in November 2026: SOCRadar, via https://malware.news/t/artex-916-exposed-ai-pentest-consoles-mapped-in-the-u-s/127242, read 2026-11-20), and transcripts may hold client data. So: no server, no port, no network call, no JavaScript, the CSP `default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'` (inline styles accepted because the page has no stylesheet file and `check_page` refuses `url(`, `url=`, `@import` and `://` in `<style>` and `style=`), every text escaped, the file 0600 outside any git work tree, never committed or published. Boundary: titles, folders, branch names and finding summaries stay on the page and can name a client; the page covers `boundary: client` repositories too, because it holds a subset of the transcripts beside it on the same machine, and it never leaves the machine (no ticket, pull request, chat or evidence pack). A branch name from a transcript is checked by `git check-ref-format --branch NAME` (the name as its single argument; that verb takes no `--end-of-options`, exit 129 on git 2.55.0) and reaches `git log` only after `--end-of-options`, when it holds no `@{` and came back unchanged. Cost: about $0 a month; a few USD of tokens to build. Compliance angle: the page shows the audit trail (guard log, review receipts, evidence paths); it is no evidence by itself, the files it reads are. Revisit if: live state is needed beyond what a terminal multiplexer gives (then a Claude Code panel bound to localhost), or a QA reviewer wants the page as a record (then it goes through the evidence export with its sha256).
- Why no tool-call content: commands, paths and outputs can carry client data or secrets; the timeline shows the tool name, the verdict and the time. Revisit if: an open-mode repository needs the content (then an opt-in with redaction, never in `boundary: client`).
- Deferred, named: live refresh, tracker titles from the forge (the branch and PR number name the task, no network), tool-call content, Copilot sessions (Copilot leaves the guard log only), an exploration graph, a re-test button.
- Model routing exception: the plan was written by the Opus session, not by `platform-planner` on Fable, because the Fable limit was reached on 2026-11-20 (reset 2026-11-24) and the owner chose not to wait ("moze zrob plan samemu, nie ma co czekac"). Cost: about $1 of tokens. Revisit if: the owner wants a Fable review of the plan once the limit resets.
- Related, the owner's tool: herdr (herdr.dev, Apache-2.0, a local server `herdr-core`, SSH to other machines, a phone app) was considered as the live view and is complementary, not a replacement: it shows which agent needs attention now and shows no tool calls, guard verdicts, findings or cost. The owner installs it himself (an agent installs nothing). A box tab runs `bin/box run NAME`; its phone link stays off for box tabs, since a session in the box runs with permission checks off; no use in `boundary: client` repositories until a line here says so. Cost: $0. Revisit if: herdr is wanted on client work, or it stops showing agent state through `docker run -it`.
- The fresh review at `08f51de` (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR, 4 MINOR, $0.22), fixed in the next commit: the plan's "no `http`" check could not pass beside the CSP tag's `http-equiv`, so steps 2 and 5 now test for `<script`, `src=`/`href=` other than `#` anchors, `url(` and `@import`; branch names from transcripts reach git only after `--end-of-options` and `git check-ref-format --branch`, with tests; the boundary of the page is named (above and in the plan's section 3); the plan's status names the promotion of #77; the ARTEX figure has its source and read date.
- The fresh review of the delta `--base 08f51de` at `d47f758` (COMMENT, 3 MINOR, $0.23), fixed in the next commit: the external-load check is now `check_page`, an allowlist over the page parsed with `html.parser` (tags and attributes the renderer emits, `href` only as `#`, `meta` only as charset and the CSP tag, no `://`, `url(`, `url=` or `@import` in attribute values or `<style>`), shared by the tests and the live run, so escaped text never counts and meta refresh, `action=` and `srcset=` are caught; a branch holding `@{` or changed by `check-ref-format` gets no evidence lookup.
- The fresh review of the delta `--base d47f758` at `6b8ea06` (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR, 2 MINOR, $0.23), fixed in the next commit: `check_page` requires the CSP tag exactly once with exactly the planned content (missing, doubled or `default-src *` are breaches, each with a test); its allowlist is a fixed list of its own, never derived from the renderer; the `check-ref-format` call takes the name as its single argument, as probed on git 2.55.0 (`--output=x` refused, `--end-of-options` exit 129, `@{-1}` expanded to `main`), and the tests run against the git on the machine.
- The fresh review of the delta `--base 6b8ea06` at `0bc542b` (COMMENT, 3 MINOR, $0.23), answered in the next commit, which is prose only: the full CSP and why inline styles are accepted (above); the other two are written into the plan as requirements of steps 1 and 2 (`refs/heads/NAME` and `--` for `git log`, a branch absent from the clone shown as such, git tests that assert outcomes, build their own reflog and fail when git is missing). With this line every finding of the four reviews is fixed or carried into a named step.
- Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18 and his "you can merge when its green" of 2026-11-21: Claude Code session `e7803278-c604-491c-9e5a-00628a1fbc58` on the owner's Mac merges the pull request of this block with the owner's token, after the checks are green on its head, with `gh pr merge --match-head-commit` on that head and the fresh-review receipts of this block, whose findings are all fixed or carried into a named step; the last commit is prose. Docs only: no workflow file is touched and no test is removed. The owner promoted #77 to `status/ready` himself on 2026-11-21.

## 2026-11-21 — orchestration: assert_run.py with merge-ready, the skill orchestrate, boundary client from the installer (item #68, plan steps 2.3, 1.3, 1.8)

- How it was built: in the box (seat `orch`) with the design D1–D10 settled by the host session; the expected outputs first (`5ca40a8`: tests and synthetic fixtures, no implementation, run and failing on the missing modules and behaviour), then one commit per topic (`9d9b36f` run and worker, `1082e45` merge-ready, `9c486d8` brief_lint and the skill, `c77e845` the AGENTS.md section, `c734439` the installer's boundary, `4c68fd2` manifest and README). Cost: $0 a month (standard library, no service, no runtime dependency); the build's tokens only. Compliance angle for every line below: each check is a deterministic script whose output (one line per problem, a summary line, an exit code) can go into a pipeline log or an evidence pack; a gap never passes, so missing evidence reads as missing, not as green.
- D1, the command line of `assert_run.py`. What: modes `run FILE`, `worker FILE [--repo DIR]`, `merge-ready N [--repo DIR]`; exit 0 pass, 1 fail or gap, 2 usage; `FAIL:` / `GAP:` lines, then `assert_run <mode>: PASS` or `FAIL (<n> problems)`; pure check functions (`check_run`, `check_worker`, `check_merge`) on parsed data, small gather functions for files, git and gh, which the tests patch; no network in a test. Why: the result of an agent is data, judged by code, never by its prose (plan D1, D4). Revisit if: a fourth mode is needed (then it follows the same split).
- D2, mode `run`, the D4 post-step. What: the last `"type": "result"` message of a JSON object, array or JSON lines is judged: an error or a subtype other than `success` fails; a denial on any result message fails naming the tool (deduplicated by `tool_use_id`); the field missing on the judged message, no `structured_output` or no `subagent_stats` is a gap; a `null` top-level value of `structured_output` or a `null` element of its `stages` fails; `spawned` > `completed` + `failed` + the sum of `killed`, or `queued_turn_count` > 0, fails. Departures, each a tightening or a silence of the design: non-integer counts in `subagent_stats` are a gap; a missing `queued_turn_count` is not reported (the design names only > 0). Assumption: that `spawned - completed - failed - killed` counts unfinished agents is inferred from the field names of CLI 2.1.323, not tested. Named limit: the mode checks the D4 contract on the output, not that the job ran with the flags (`--permission-mode dontAsk`, `--max-budget-usd`, background tasks off); that stays the job definition's and its review's. Revisit if: probe 0.3 or a run shows otherwise, or the CLI renames a field.
- D3, mode `worker`. What: a `worker-result/1` object (`branch`, `head` as 40 lower-case hex, a non-empty `checks` list of `command` and `exit_code`, a `not_verified` list); the tip is the local branch, else `origin/<branch>`, by `git rev-parse` with a 10 s timeout and no stdin; a head that is not the tip fails, an exit code that is not the integer 0 (a bool is not) fails, every `not_verified` entry prints as `NOT VERIFIED:` on pass and fail. Departure: a `checks` entry with no string `command` is a gap naming its index. Named limit (fresh review): the exit codes are the worker's own report and nothing is re-run, so a pass means the worker claimed them; the skill tells the orchestrator to re-run the done evidence. A branch name that is not a valid ref name (`feat/x~1`, `@{-1}`) is never looked up, so it cannot resolve to another commit (`git check-ref-format`). Why: a worker's "done" is a head commit and exit codes, not a sentence. Revisit if: workers need to report more than one branch per packet.
- D4, mode `merge-ready N`. What: `gh pr view N --json number,state,headRefName,headRefOid,statusCheckRollup,files`, origin's tip by `git ls-remote`, the local tip when the branch exists; open state, head equal to both tips, checks non-empty, each `CheckRun` completed with `SUCCESS`/`NEUTRAL`/`SKIPPED` and each `StatusContext` `SUCCESS`, at least one success; the newest receipt under `<git dir>/fresh-review/` (`<sha>.json` is number 1, `<sha>.<n>.json` with n >= 2, the highest wins, read at most 1 MiB) with that `sha` and verdict `APPROVE` or `COMMENT`, the same rule as guard 0.11.0's box merge, written again here rather than imported; a `DECISIONS.md` entry with additions > 0. On a pass it prints `gh pr merge <N> --merge --match-head-commit <full sha>`, the one form the box profile accepts, and merges nothing. gh and `ls-remote` run with a 60 s timeout (not named in the design). Named limits: it reads gh and git at one moment, so a push after it is caught only by GitHub's `--match-head-commit`; the receipt is a file a session could write itself, so it stops a forgotten review, not a hostile session (Phase 1 plan section 6, decision 7); a COMMENT receipt can carry open findings, which the tool does not check, so the skill and `README.md` say to answer each before the merge; "green" covers the checks the rollup lists at that moment, and a required check not reported yet is not seen, so branch protection stays the backstop; receipts sit in the git folder of the worktree where the review ran (a linked worktree has its own), so `merge-ready` runs there. Why: the merge preconditions the owner checks by hand become one command with evidence. Revisit if: the receipt naming of `tools/review/fresh_review.py` changes (the tests name files with its `receipt_path`, so they break first), or receipts move off the clone.
- D5, `brief_lint.py`. What: the eleven `## ` fields of the template, each once, none empty after HTML comments are removed, a code span or fenced block in `Done evidence`, a digit in `Size limit`; other headings ignored; `brief_lint: <file>: <problem>` or `ok`. Departures: `## ` lines inside a fenced block are not headings; an empty field is reported once as empty, not also as lacking a command or a number, so the bare template gives exactly eleven problems; an argument starting with `-` is usage (exit 2). Why: a worker briefed with a blank field guesses; the linter makes the omission visible before the brief is sent (plan rev 3, row 1.3). Revisit if: a field turns out to be optional in practice for three briefs running.
- D6, the skill `skills/orchestrate/`. What: `SKILL.md` (the three levels and depth two, the shape follows the job, packets, the linted brief, the first packet end to end, at most five agents, a missing result is a gap, the D4 contract with `assert_run.py run`, `merge-ready` before a merge and the printed command only where the standing rules let an agent merge, the flat fan-out in Copilot), `brief-template.md` (bodies only comments; it fails the linter, and its output format is `worker-result/1`), `brief-example.md` (a fictional packet; it passes). Why: one procedure the orchestrator follows the same way every time. Revisit if: the first real multi-packet task shows a step the procedure lacks.
- D7, `AGENTS.md` `## Orchestration`: eleven lines with its heading (ten before the fresh review added who runs the merge) after "Model routing", tested by `tools/orchestration/tests/test_skill.py` (at most 12 lines, the skill's three files, the template fails and the example passes). Why: the working agreement names the rule; the skill holds the procedure. Revisit if: the section needs more than 12 lines (then the detail moves into the skill).
- D8, the installer's boundary. What: `install.sh --into <repo> [--boundary own|client]`, default `client`, written into a new target's `.claude/guard.json` by replacing the kit's one `"boundary": "own"` line (checked to match exactly one line before any copy; otherwise an error naming the file); any other value refused before any copy; an existing file is never touched, also not with `--force` (it is `init`), with a note and `boundary: left as is (existing file)` in the summary; a second run changes nothing. Why: until now every target copied this repository's `own` (fact 24), the permissive mode, into client work by default; the strict mode is now the default and the permissive one is a deliberate flag. Compliance angle: the boundary of a client repository no longer depends on someone remembering to edit a file. Revisit if: targets need a third boundary value, or an upgrade path that rewrites an existing file is wanted (then a separate, explicit command).
- D9, packaging and docs. What: `kit.manifest` lists `tools/orchestration/assert_run.py` and `brief_lint.py` as managed; the skill comes with the existing `map skills .claude/skills` line (the install test checks all three files in a target); `README.md` has the `--boundary` paragraph in Install, which is also the upgrade note (a target installed before this change keeps `own` and its owner should check the line), `orchestrate` under Skills and an Orchestration subsection under Reference. Revisit if: `probe.py` should ship to targets too (not now: it spends tokens and needs this repository's canaries).
- Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18: Claude Code session `afc2ed89-c939-483f-a3b5-e9666943677f` on the owner's Mac merges the pull request of this block with the owner's token, after its checks are green on its head, by the command `python3 tools/orchestration/assert_run.py merge-ready <N>` prints for that head (`gh pr merge <N> --merge --match-head-commit <sha>`), so the merge needs a fresh-review receipt for the final head. Reviews: round 1, the whole diff at `59bdb3e` ($0.88), COMMENT with 1 MAJOR and 6 MINOR, all fixed in `07613d6` (code, two tests) and `2e6196b` (docs, D3, D4, D7); round 2, the delta `--base 59bdb3e` at `2e6196b` ($1.16), COMMENT with 1 MINOR (the guard's test could pass without the guard), fixed in `f177d2c` for the three names git does resolve (`feat/x~1`, `feat/x^`, `feat/x@{1}`) and shown by a planted break (the guard line removed from a scratch copy: those three cases fail on Python 3.9 and 3.13); round 3, the delta `--base 2e6196b` at `08543b4` ($0.24), COMMENT with 1 MINOR: the other three names (`feat/x:y`, `@{-1}`, `feat/x..y`) git does not resolve under `refs/heads/` or `refs/remotes/origin/`, the two places `branch_tip` looks a name up (D3), so their test passes with or without the guard. Named, not fixed: it pins the outcome, and its docstring says so (`10a5bf4`); round 4, the delta `--base 08543b4` at `10a5bf4` ($0.19), COMMENT with 1 MINOR: a bare `@{-1}` resolves once HEAD's reflog has a branch switch. Answered by hand in a scratch repository with such a switch: the bare form resolves, `git rev-parse --verify -q 'refs/heads/@{-1}^{commit}'` resolves to nothing and `git check-ref-format 'refs/heads/@{-1}'` exits 1; round 5 asked for the fallback too, and in a clone with a branch switch `refs/remotes/origin/` followed by `@{-1}`, `feat/x:y` or `feat/x..y` resolves to nothing as well, so the claim holds for both anchored lookups. History rewritten once: the first commit's subject had 74 characters, over the commit-msg gate's 72, and CI's `commit-msg` check failed on it (the box's clone has no `core.hooksPath`, so its hook never ran, and the host checks did not run the range check; the follow-up is backlog item #85, CI's check stays the backstop until it is done). The host reworded that one subject and replayed the branch onto it, and the owner pushed it with `--force-with-lease`, since the guard denies a force push to a session. Evidence that the content is unchanged: `git rev-parse <old>^{tree} <new>^{tree}` gives the same tree for all 13 pairs below. The trees of the four heads reviewed before the rewrite are `417340cfe57a3f38d64d4b87154dd85b1eda9242` (`59bdb3e`), `bcb5cfd2eb59c2de9230a946de115650c74e9617` (`2e6196b`), `b33dec70a9c8ec828fc823dad4794f9758da237f` (`08543b4`) and `3258e3ca07b5248f73032e4c09c02118b5e9737c` (`10a5bf4`). Each receipt's `diff_sha256`, the sha256 of the diff it reviewed, is reproduced from the new commits for all eight rounds by `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null git diff --abbrev=7 <base>...<head>` (git 2.55.0; this clone sets no local `diff.*` or `core.abbrev`). For rounds 1–4, whose receipts name old commits, the values are `0be00a4611a69d81fd7d91950ff6d5e93f526e473dca031e3201d98b2f61bacf` (`14a999a...59bdb3e`), `05135e1dc21981388e8b663434105aac8ad469fd2fcecae1a9948561503c154d` (`59bdb3e...2e6196b`), `c9020598f88045879438aff6b5ce365c19716a61d3eef5a573c1237ccce223de` (`2e6196b...08543b4`) and `b87e557d1c8ea7762b2b462d440a05dc711bcd9af3c242ac4edb92b6b86f3f80` (`08543b4...10a5bf4`). Named limit: `--abbrev=7` is a minimum, and git lengthens an id that becomes ambiguous, so a later recomputation can fail with the content unchanged. Backlog item #86 carries the fix: a `--full-index` hash with config isolated, the tree ids in the receipt, and receipts kept beyond the local git folder. The attestation that these diffs were reviewed is the receipts themselves. The originals are in the reviewing worktree's git folder, and a verbatim second copy of rounds 1 to 7, with the recomputation, is in https://github.com/kacper38/agent-change-control/pull/84#issuecomment-7079606923. ## 2026-11-21 — guard 0.12.0: depth pin, a guard hook that blocks on failure, the rule workflow-launch (item #67, plan steps 1.1, 1.2)

- How it was built: in the box (session `621d5db6`, profile `box` checked at the start), the design D1–D11 (D10 is this block) and the answers A1–A5 settled by the host session. Rows and tests first (`67d2051`), then one commit per topic: `2391022` settings, `7e14f6f` workflow-launch, `b82af33` the fan-out total, `e0780c6` guard-config, `9c84cc9` the probe canaries, `b992aad` version and docs. Compliance angle for every line below: a timed-out or crashed guard no longer lets a call through, every workflow launch leaves a row with its name, and only a reviewed, committed list starts a workflow unasked. Cost: $0 a month; the probe runs cost cents. The 11 `workflow-launch` fixtures were written by a snippet that builds them as `make_fixtures.py` does, because the box denies running `make_fixtures.py` (its delete commands are data, which the box cannot tell from local deletes); `test_corpus_matches_generator` shows they equal the generator's output byte for byte.
- D1, settings. What: `"env": {"CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH": "2"}` and `"onFailure": "block"` on the PreToolUse guard hook entry only; `Workflow` was in the matcher since 0.10.0; `_about` names both; `test_the_settings_pin_the_depth_and_block_on_a_failed_guard` (in `test_known_tools.py`) checks the pin, that exactly that hook blocks, and the matcher. Why: measured by the host on CLI 2.1.323, a pre hook that times out or exits 1 lets the call run unless its entry says block; post, start and session hooks block no call, and a blocked post hook would hide a result. Named consequence: with block on, a guard that runs past its 5 s timeout blocks the call instead of letting it run; the guard's own budget (`HOOK_BUDGET_S = 3.5`) answers first in every case it can measure. Revisit if: the CLI changes the key or its default, or a guard ask turns out to block a call it should not.
- D2, two probe canaries (code and offline tests; the host runs them live and commits the record). What: `hook-failure-blocks` (step 1.1, fact 6): three runs of `RUN_PROMPT`, the recorder as pre hook sleeping 8 s past `timeout: 2` without `onFailure` (control), the same with `onFailure: block`, and an exit 1 with block; holds when the control ran and has a hook row and both block runs have a hook row and no marker, fails when a block run has the marker, else inconclusive. `workflow-agents-total` (step 1.2): a saved workflow `kit-probe-six` starts six agents in sequence, the guard's fan-out SubagentStart hook on, both routes allowed; holds when the session's sixth SubagentStart row has `total == 6`, fails when six rows exist and the sixth's total is missing or wrong, else inconclusive. The recorder gets `--sleep` and `--exit-code` (after the row); `Run` gets `pre_timeout`, `on_failure`, `hook_mode`, whose defaults leave every other scratch project byte-identical (a test builds each with and without them). Departure: `agent()` has no tool option, so "no tools" is the `LEAF` prompt ("Use no tools"). `test_committed_record_matches_the_canary_list` fails until the host commits the live record. Revisit if: a canary turns inconclusive twice in a row.
- D3, the rule `workflow-launch` (after `cloud-launch` in `RULES` and `PRE_RULES`). What: a `script` or `scriptPath` key asks (`WORKFLOW_SCRIPT_MSG`), also beside a listed name; a `name` that is no non-empty string asks with a reader reason (`READER_MSG`, so a box denies it); a name not in `workflows` asks (`WORKFLOW_UNKNOWN_MSG`); a listed name gets no answer. `workflows` comes from the committed `guard.json` as the configuration is read (host: the nearest; box: the judge's); `guard.local.json` adds none; non-strings are dropped with a note, a non-list is empty with a note. `BOX_TABLE` denies its asks; cloud turns them into denies; `rules_off` can name it on the host in an own repository. `DEFAULTS` and this repository's `guard.json` get `"workflows": []` (step 1.4 adds `review-panel`). `KNOWN_CLAUDE_TOOLS["Workflow"]` is `judged`. Fixtures: allow, ask, crash (the crash cases are ask fixtures: the format has allow, ask and deny only), Claude only; the cloud and box cases and the deny (A3) are rows of `test_guard_0120.py`. Why: a saved workflow fans out agents with no Agent call to ask at; only a reviewed, listed script should start unasked. Revisit if: Copilot gets a workflow tool, or a listed workflow needs per-run arguments judged.
- D4, the fan-out total. What: every spawn row carries `tool` and `total`; the total of an Agent/Task/task call is the larger of the session's counted PreToolUse rows (no `tool`, as in 0.11.0, or a spawn tool) and its SubagentStart rows, plus one; a SubagentStart's is the larger of the PreToolUse count and the starts with itself; a `Workflow` call logs one row (`workflow`, `script`, the current total) and is not counted; the ask comes once when the total crosses 6, 16, 26, …; a plain int from `log_spawn` (the stubs of older tests, A2) reads as total `n`, previous `n - 1`. Without SubagentStart rows (Copilot) this is exactly 0.11.0. Departures: a spawn log row that is JSON but no object is skipped (0.11.0 crashed on it, which now would block the call); `FANOUT_MSG` says "workflow agents included"; a Workflow row's `agent` field holds the workflow name, as the old name lookup gives it. Named consequence: the ask can now come before the sixth Agent call (workflow agents count) and is never skipped by a jump in the total; a session that only runs listed workflows gets no fan-out ask, because a SubagentStart cannot ask: its agents count toward the next Agent call, and caps on listed workflows come with step 1.4 (item #69). Revisit if: the owner's log shows a session past 16 agents with no ask.
- D5, `guard-config`. What: a path below `.claude/workflows/` (not the folder itself) is "a workflow script", for Write, Edit, the shell writers and the GitHub file tools; a read, `cat`, `docs/workflows/`, `.claude/workflows-notes.txt` stay silent; in a box it logs, in cloud it is a deny. The rows are in `test_guard_0120.py` only: new guard-config fixtures would move the 0.10.0 corpus `test_box_profile.py` compares. Why: a listed name starts what that folder holds. Revisit if: workflows move out of `.claude/workflows/`.
- D6, never weaker (criterion 7). What: `baseline-0.11.0.jsonl` (5,186 inputs) from `git show 1b35952:tools/guard/guard.py` over `test_box_profile.py`'s corpus plus its LOCAL and REMOTE delete tables; `test_guard_0120.py` checks `pre`, `rule` and, beyond the design, `box` (the answer behind a valid marker) against it. `CHANGED` lists eight: fixture `cloud-launch/claude/allow/09-workflow-review-panel` and `TOOL_ROWS` 21–23 of 0.10.0, each `pre` silent → ask and `box` silent → deny, each a Workflow call (D3, A1); a test checks that every changed input is a Workflow call. `test_box_profile.py` exempts Workflow calls and the new rule's fixtures from its 0.10.0 equality (A1). The cloud profile is not compared: it is the host's answer with every ask a deny, which 0.12.0 does not change. Revisit if: a later change lands an entry in `CHANGED` that is no Workflow call.
- D7, rows before the code. What: `67d2051`; at 0.11.0, 107 tests fail (the new behaviour: W2–W5, W7, the workflow-launch half of W6, every F row, the G1 rows, version, registry, messages, the fixtures, A5, D1, and the 8 CHANGED entries that had not happened) and the rows that state 0.11.0 hold (W1, the cloud-launch half of W6, W8, the G2 neighbours, the plain-int reading). Why: criterion 1.
- D8, hook cost. What: median of five `hook pre` calls through the real entry point, 0.11.0 and 0.12.0 from the same folder: plain Bash 16 → 17 ms, a listed Workflow call 16 → 16 ms, an Agent call with a seeded `spawns.jsonl` of 1,000 rows 17 → 16 ms (the log is still read whole at each spawn; far under 0.5 s). A copy run from `/tmp` measured 86–91 ms for both versions: the location, not the code. `test_hook_budget.py` passes. Revisit if: the log passes 100,000 rows or a spawn hook passes 0.5 s.
- D9, version and docs. What: `VERSION = "0.12.0"`; `test_the_version_is_0_11_0` keeps its name and checks 0.12.0; `README.md`, `docs/SYSTEM.md` (its header, the guard's and the spawn log's rows) and `docs/USING-AT-WORK.md` updated. Departure: USING-AT-WORK §5 said a Claude Code hook timeout blocks the call by default; the host's measurement says it lets it run, so the row now says so and names `onFailure`. Copilot: no `onFailure` (its timeouts fail open) and no Workflow tool, so nothing of this applies there and `.github/hooks/` is unchanged.
- D11, the Workflow tool's arguments: `name`, `script`, `scriptPath`, `args`, `resumeFromRunId`; a saved workflow started through `Skill(<name>)` ends in a `Workflow` call, so no Skill rule; row W8: a Skill call with a workflow's name answers as 0.11.0.
- A1–A5: A1 as D6; A2 as D4; A3 the deny is a combined row (W6: `workflow-launch` asks, `cloud-launch` denies); A4 `test_every_rule_has_a_corpus` names `(workflow-launch, copilot)` as having no corpus (Copilot has no Workflow tool) and checks that folder does not exist; A5 `test_subagent_start_does_not_count` keeps its name and checks that ten SubagentStart rows never answer and make the next Agent call ask at #11.

## 2026-11-21 — the task page (#77) built: `tools/telemetry/page.py`, steps 1–5 of its plan

- Step 1, which sessions a page holds: those of the repository the page is for (`project`): a host session belongs to it when a `cwd` its transcript names is that folder or lies under it (worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` do; scratch worktrees elsewhere, such as a session's scratchpad, do not), found through the Claude Code project folders whose name starts with the project's encoded name and confirmed by the `cwd`, so a sibling folder of the same prefix (`repo-2`) stays out. A box session is one whose `cwd` is `/work`, where every box mounts its clone; `collect()` reads the homes it is given, and the CLI (step 3) decides which. Cost: $0. Revisit if: sessions of one repository regularly run from folders outside it.
- Step 1, a session's task: the branch most of its lines carry (a tie goes to the branch seen last); every branch and PR number it saw stays on its row. Named limit: a session whose folder is the main checkout and that works in a worktree by absolute paths is grouped under the main checkout's branch, since its lines carry that branch (the session that built this page is one); its PR numbers from `pr-link` lines still name the task. Revisit if: Claude Code records the branch of the worktree a call touched, or the tracker's claim is read (forge data stays out of this version, answer 2).
- Step 1, how a review is tied to a task: a receipt holds no branch, so it belongs to a task when its `sha` is a value `refs/heads/NAME` has had (its reflog, `git log -g`) or a commit of `origin/main..refs/heads/NAME`; the reflog keeps the tie after a merge or a rebase, where `origin/main..NAME` alone is empty once the branch is in main. Receipts are read from the repository's git common dir (`fresh-review/` and every `worktrees/NAME/fresh-review/`), not only from the git dir of a session's folder, because a review run in a worktree lands in that worktree's git dir. A receipt tied to no branch on the page is counted under the reviews, not shown. Limits: a branch deleted, or a reflog older than git's expiry (90 days by default), loses the tie; a branch made from another's tip also shows the other's review at that tip. Revisit if: `fresh-review` writes the branch into its receipt.
- Step 1, git calls: `origin/main` is checked once per page (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options refs/remotes/origin/main^{commit}`), because `git log origin/main..refs/heads/NAME` exits 128 both for a branch absent from the clone and for a clone without `origin/main`; without it every branch says "origin/main not in this clone" instead of blaming the branch. Git runs only in the project folder, never in a box's clone (the box session controls that clone's `.git/config`, which is why `tools/box/box.py` reads it in a throwaway container). On git 2.55.0 every name `check-ref-format --branch` changes also holds `@{` (`@{-2}` comes back as `main`), so with the real git the two checks of section 3 hide each other; a stub that answers `check-ref-format` tests each alone, beside the tests against the real git. Compliance angle: the evidence lookup is read-only and local, and each refusal is shown on the page rather than dropped.
- Step 2, `check_page` holds more than the plan's list, each with its own test: a backslash in `<style>` text or in an attribute value is a breach (a CSS escape such as `\75 rl(` spells `url(` past a text search); a comment, a processing instruction and any declaration but `<!DOCTYPE html>` are breaches (the renderer writes none); the CSP tag must sit inside `<head>` (a browser ignores it elsewhere); every `href` is checked, not only the last of two (a browser takes the first, `dict()` the last); a `<meta>` is the charset or the CSP tag by its full attribute list, so a doubled attribute is a breach. The page has no inline `style=` and no `<div>` outside `.wide` wrappers around tables, so the allowlist has neither `style` nor any event attribute. Planted breaks: 18 of 18 caught (one more, `convert_charrefs=False`, changes nothing, since `html.parser` unescapes attribute values either way and never `<style>` text, so the argument was dropped). Revisit if: the page needs an element or attribute off the list (then the list in `check_page` grows by hand, with a test).
- Step 3, the command `page.py [--days 7] [--session ID] [--out PATH]`: the page is for the main work tree of the repository the current folder lies in (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), so a run from a worktree shows the whole repository; the box home is read only when that repository has `bin/box`, since a box clones only the repository that starts it, `tools/box` is not in `kit.manifest`, and a box session holds no repository name, only `/work` (a task repository's page shows no box sessions, and says so in its header). The refusal "inside a git work tree" covers the default place as well as `--out` (`KZ_TELEMETRY_DIR` can point into a repository, and some owners keep `~/.claude` under git), and it walks up the folders for a `.git` entry instead of asking git: stricter than git's discovery, with no git, no config and no `safe.directory` or ceiling setting that could turn it off. A new file only (`O_CREAT | O_EXCL`, so an existing file or a link in its place stays as it is), mode 0600 whatever the umask, the default folder set to 0700 at every run; the page passes `check_page` before it is written; stdout holds the path alone, so a shell can open it. `--session` takes a full id or a prefix that fits one session, and the task's figures then count that session only. Git variables that point at another repository (`GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and the like, set inside a git hook) are dropped for every git call. Planted breaks: 15 of 15 caught. Cost: $0. Revisit if: a box session starts to record the repository it cloned (then its sessions can be matched without `bin/box`).
- Step 4, into the kit: one `managed` line in `kit.manifest` (`tools/telemetry/page.py`, beside the ledger it loads by path), rows in `docs/SYSTEM.md` for the script and for its pages with their data class, a README entry. `install.sh` needed no change, though the plan's step 4 names it: `--into` copies every manifest entry, and the home install copies agents and skills only; shown by an install into a scratch repository, where the copy is identical and `page.py --out` writes an empty page that says the box home was not read. Revisit if: the home install starts to ship tools.
- Step 5, the live run on this repository tied 28 of 57 reviews to no task; 26 of them were on branches whose sessions ran from the main checkout and so sat under its branch (the limit named in step 1), among them every review of guard 0.9.0. Fix: when reviews stay untied, each other local branch (`git for-each-ref refs/heads/`, every name through the same `branch_lookup` and its checks as a name from a transcript) whose reflog or commits hold one becomes a task with no session: its cost reads n/a, its last activity is its last review, and the page says why it has no session. A `--session` page shows that session's task only, so it gets none. Limit: the sessions that did that work still sit under the main checkout's branch; only their reviews move. Planted breaks are a scratch check, not evidence: the lists and the script that runs them stay in a session's scratch folder, the counts are that script's output (the last run is on the pull request), and the committed tests are what an auditor can rerun. The lists behind the counts in the step 1–3 lines were lost with such a folder and rebuilt wider for this head, 74 breaks over steps 1, 2, 3 and 5; six of them showed a behaviour no test held (the branch most lines carry, the stronger of two guard lines, a full id over the ids it is a prefix of, `URL(` in capitals, a worktree outside the repository whose `.git` is a file, a page that breaks its own check is never written), each now has a test, and all 74 are caught. Cost: $0. Revisit if: Claude Code records the branch of the folder a call touched.
- Step 5, the fresh review of `4d344d2` (COMMENT, four MINOR, $1.30), each answered with a test: the commit range names `refs/remotes/origin/main`, the ref the base check verified, since a local branch or tag `origin/main` comes first in git's lookup of the short name; commits come from `git rev-list` and the paths from `git log -z --name-only --format=`, so a path git would C-quote (`"`, `\`, bytes above 0x7f) stays on the evidence list; `--days` runs from 1 to 3650, since a far larger value overflowed `datetime` into a traceback. The fourth (the docs said no tool input, yet each agent's label carried the description its Agent call gave): `81448b2` kept the descriptions and named them in the docs, which was not mine to decide, since the owner's answer 1 in the plan keeps tool-call content off the page and acceptance criterion 5 of #77 leaves any opt-in to him; the next commit takes it back: an agent appears by its type, a workflow without its script's name, and the fixtures plant the marker in a description and in a script's name. Revisit if: the owner opts in to descriptions.
- Step 5, the fresh review of `01ac45a` (COMMENT, five MINOR, $1.25): `check_page` ends the head at any start tag that cannot stand in it, as a browser does, not only at `</head>`; a reflog read that fails gives the lookup `error` instead of an empty reflog (a branch with no reflog exits 0), and a failed `git for-each-ref` is named on the page; an unreadable transcript line is counted once (the ledger's count, which covers the same lines); the planted-break counts are named a scratch check (in the step 5 line above). Decision on retention: a page is kept 30 days, the default `cleanupPeriodDays` of Claude Code's own transcripts, and every run that writes to the default place says on stderr how many of its pages there are older than that; it deletes none, since a delete on the owner's machine stays the owner's act. Cost: $0. Revisit if: old pages pile up all the same (then `page.py` removes its own pages past the 30 days, by the time in their name).
- Step 5, the fresh review of the delta `--base 01ac45a` at `dd2fa2f` (COMMENT, five MINOR, $1.36): the head opens once and ends at `</head>`, at a start tag that cannot stand in it, or at text outside `<title>` and `<style>` (HTML's own whitespace excepted, so a no-break space ends it), and a second `<head>` reopens nothing; an agent's type comes from a `type` field the ledger now writes beside its label, never from cutting the label; a test against the real git shows a branch with no reflog stays `ok`; the docs say the retention notice gives a count. Not changed: the count of unreadable lines, since both readers parse a line alike and the ledger also counts usage it cannot read, so its count holds the page's (answered on the pull request). Revisit if: the ledger stops writing `type`, or the page reads a transcript the ledger does not.
- Step 5, the fresh review of the delta `--base dd2fa2f` at `41dc07c` (COMMENT, four MINOR, $0.48) found two more places where a browser ends the head and the check did not (text before `<head>`, `</body>` inside it). Instead of following the HTML parser further, `check_page` now asks that the page open with `<!DOCTYPE html>`, `<html>`, `<head>`, `<meta charset>` and the CSP tag, in that order with nothing between, where every browser applies the tag; the head rules of the line above are gone, and the count of CSP tags refuses only a second one, since the opening holds the first. The renderer writes exactly that. The docstring of `agent_label` names the ledger's kind `missing` (an agent with no transcript keeps its type), and the line above has its revisit condition. - Step 5, the full fresh review of the merge `9af810c` (COMMENT, two MINOR, $1.08), both fixed: a box run's `result.json` that does not parse, or names no session id a session file can have (the CLI wrote no JSON, a run that never got going), was dropped without a count, against step 1's line that each refusal is shown; it now counts as a file not read (ALCOA+ complete: the page says what it could not tie), and the page's notes say what `not read` covers. ## 2026-11-22 — the review panel and guard 0.12.1: three lenses, one verifier, a listed workflow bound to its file (item #69, plan steps 1.4, 1.5)

- How it is built: on the host, in the worktree `.claude/worktrees/review-panel` (branch `feat/review-panel` from main `1cacf7e`), by Claude Code session `02c6f4b0`, one commit per topic. Not claimed while it was built: the host seat is one item at a time (tracker 0.2.0), and another session was finishing #77 unclaimed and needed the seat for its claim; the owner chose "build now, claim later" in this session on 2026-11-22. Claimed on 2026-11-22 once #77 was in review (PR #89).
- Guard 0.12.1, the limit #67 carried to this item (its tracker note of 2026-11-22). Decision: an entry of `workflows` binds a name to the sha256 of its reviewed file, chosen by the owner in this session over the other way the note named (deny writes under `.claude/workflows/**` in a box). Why: it closes the gap on the host as well (there an edit asks, but the edited file then started unasked), a box can still build and fix a workflow, and only a committed hash starts one unasked; cost: no money, one hash to write at each reviewed edit (the static test of step 1.5 fails on a stale one); compliance: the list names the exact script a session may start without a question, tied to the commit that wrote its hash, and the review of that commit is the fresh-review receipt the merger line names (ALCOA+: attributable, original). The hash binds the script (which agents, how many, which model), not the agent files its `agentType` calls read their tools and prompt from (named limit below, item #90); revisit if the harness pins a saved workflow's content itself, or a plugin's workflow has to be listed. Departure from the frozen scope of #69: `tools/guard/guard.py` and its tests are outside it; the note made the fix a condition of listing `review-panel`.
- What 0.12.1 does: an entry is `{"name": text, "sha256": 64 lower-case hex}`; a name may carry several entries (any of its values matches). At launch `workflow-launch` takes every file `cloud-launch` reads for the name (`_workflow_files`: `.claude/workflows/<name>.js` in the call's folder and each folder above it, in CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR and in the user's Claude folder), reads its bytes (at most 1,000,000, a regular file) and needs each to hash to one of the values. It asks for a bare name (`WORKFLOW_UNBOUND_MSG`, also an entry whose sha256 is upper case, short or missing), no file found (`WORKFLOW_NOFILE_MSG`), a file that differs (`WORKFLOW_CHANGED_MSG`, naming the path) and a file it cannot read (`WORKFLOW_UNCHECKED_MSG`); in a box and in cloud each is a deny (the profiles, unchanged). An entry with no name as text is ignored, with a note on stderr. `cloud-launch` reads the same files as before (`_named_workflow` now built on `_workflow_files`).
- Rows before the code (`1e14177`): 29 rows in `test_guard_0121.py` through `hook pre` with real files (an edited file, CRLF bytes, a symlink, a folder, a file over 1 MB, a file above the repository, in CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR and in the user's folder; box, cloud, rules_off); the fixtures of `workflow-launch` carry their file (`_fixture.files`) and gain three asks. At 0.12.0 38 tests fail.
- Never weaker than 0.12.0: `test_guard_0120.py`'s check over the 5,186 inputs of `baseline-0.11.0.jsonl` passes with its CHANGED list untouched, as it did at 0.12.0, so every input of that corpus answers as at 0.12.0 (the corpus lists no workflow). What moved is a bare name, each now stricter: 8 rows of `WORKFLOW_ROWS` (`CHANGED_BY_0_12_1` in `test_guard_0120.py`), 5 held-out rows of 0.12.0 (W01, W12, W15, W16, F10; `CHANGED_BY_0_12_1` in `test_heldout_0_12_0.py`, as 0.11.0's held-out test records its review's changes) and one note test; the hashed rows file is unchanged.
- Hook cost: median of seven `hook pre` calls, a 14 kB listed workflow, both versions from the same folder while the full suite ran: plain Bash 50.7 ms (0.12.0) and 53.1 ms (0.12.1), the listed Workflow call 52.5 ms and 52.8 ms: within the noise of the loaded machine.
- Limits, named: a workflow a plugin holds is still not read: listed, it asks when no other file is found, and when a matching file is found elsewhere the guard cannot tell which of the two the harness starts; the file can change between the guard's hash and the harness's own read (the same call, milliseconds); the hash is of the bytes on disk, so a checkout that rewrites line endings asks (it fails closed; `.gitattributes` sets `.claude/workflows/*.js -text` since review round 1, so git converts none); which of several files the harness starts is not known, so every one found must match, and the walk goes on above the git root (kept: it fails closed, and whether the harness reads a folder above its start folder is not known; the README says a worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` meets the main checkout's copy). The agent files: a listed workflow's `agentType` calls take each agent's tools and system prompt from `.claude/agents/<type>.md` (here a link to `agents/`), which neither the hash nor `guard-config` covers, so an edited agent file runs inside a listed workflow unasked. That is no new path: an Agent-tool call of the same type starts unasked as well (the guard counts spawns, it does not read agent files), so the gap is older than the panel; backlog item #90 (2026-11-22) makes an edit to an agent file ask, as one to a workflow script does since 0.12.0.
- The panel, step 1.4 (`1eae9bd`, review round 1 in this block's last bullets). Decision: `.claude/workflows/review-panel.js` takes `{diff: "<absolute path>", head: "<40-hex commit>"}` (a diff the caller prepares, for example `git diff origin/main...HEAD` into a file named by the head inside the git folder, README) and starts four agents at most: the lenses `code-reviewer`, `infra-reviewer` and `gxp-compliance-reviewer` in parallel (a barrier, because the verifier is one batched agent, D5), then one `code-reviewer` as verifier, each with `model: 'opus'` written at its `agent()` call (Opus for review, `AGENTS.md`'s routing table). Why these agents: D5 names "the three existing reviewer agents"; the verifier is an agent type with no Write or Edit (Read, Grep, Glob, Bash) that did not write the findings. Each finding names its evidence (`command` with its output, `quote` with the file and line, or `inference` with the reasoning). The script rejects one with no kind or without the field its kind needs, and lists it under `rejected` with the reason. The verifier re-reads quotes and re-runs the commands the script lets through (the read-only list below), and answers confirmed, refuted or unconfirmed. Inference, and a command off the list, is listed as not re-checked. A verifier that returns null, throws or skips a finding leaves it unconfirmed. Each lens and the verifier report the `git rev-parse HEAD` they saw; one at another head is logged and counted (`lenses_wrong_head`), its findings kept. Nothing is dropped, and `counts` names each group. Cost: four Opus agents a run, cap $15 a run API-equivalent (Q11 of the orchestration plan); the first run (the next bullet but four) cost $7.01. Compliance: each finding carries the evidence a reader can repeat, the result names the head it reviewed and what was not confirmed (ALCOA+: attributable, accurate, traceable).
- The cap. A workflow script sees no dollars: `budget` counts output tokens only and is null without a "+N" directive, and the script has no file access. So the cap is the fixed count of four agents plus a line the run logs ("cap $15 a run …"), and the ledger measures the run (`ledger.py agents --session <id>`). Revisit if the workflow runtime exposes cost to a script.
- No egress screen and no receipt, unlike `bin/fresh-review`. Why: the panel's agents run in the session, inside the boundary the session already has, and read the files of the working tree anyway; the diff file is a focus, not a transfer. The panel is advisory and does not replace `bin/fresh-review` (scope limit of #69); `assert_run.py merge-ready` still needs the fresh-review receipt.
- The verifier runs commands that lens agents wrote, after reading a diff that is data. Decision (review round 1): the script, not the prompt, decides which command the verifier is asked to run: one program of a fixed list (`git log`, `show`, `diff`, `rev-parse`, `ls-files`, `ls-tree`, `cat-file`, `blame`, `check-attr`; `grep`, `cat`, `head`, `ls`, `wc`, `stat`, `readlink`, `shasum`, `sha256sum`, `diff`), in plain words (quotes allowed; no pipe, `;`, `&&`, redirect but `2>&1` and `2>/dev/null`, `$`, backtick, glob or `~` that names a home folder), and for git no long option or prefix of one that writes a file or starts a program (`--output`, `--ext-diff`, `--textconv`, `--filters`, `--open-files-in-pager`, and since round 2 `--show-signature` and any word holding a `%G` format placeholder, which start gpg or ssh-keygen; since round 3 also behind the modifier `+`, `-` or space git reads between `%` and the letter, as in `%+GG`). Anything else is listed as not re-checked with the reason, and the lenses' prompt names the list so they choose a quote or such a command. The verifier is sent only what a check needs (round 2): the title, and the command with its output or the file, line and quote; a lens's detail and any other key of its evidence stay out (the result keeps them). Cost of that (round 3): the verifier judges whether the evidence shows the defect from the title alone, so a short or vague title gives it less to go on, and a verdict on such a finding says less; the reader still has the whole finding in the result. Revisit if verdicts on short titles turn out wrong in runs, or if the verifier becomes an agent with no Bash (then the detail can go back). Why: the guard reads a command's text only, so it says nothing to what a test run, `node` or `python3` does, nor to a push of a feature branch (probe of the infra lens: all three silent); the line this bullet replaces credited the guard with that and was wrong. Cost: fewer findings re-checked (the first run's 18 findings replayed through the new script under the mock harness: 13 carry a command, and 12 of those, probe scripts, pipes and chained commands, are now listed as not re-checked; the lenses' prompt now names the list, so a later run can choose otherwise); compliance: no command line the script lets through to the verifier asks for a write, the change's code or the network. That is a property of the command line only, not a boundary the panel holds: what git's own config starts (the first limit below) can write or reach the network, and what stops the verifier from running a command that lens text asks for is its prompt and the guard (the same limits; reviews 5a and of `d9ee47f`). Limits: git reads its own config and the list checks the command line only, so a program the local config makes git start still runs: a diff driver, a textconv, `log.showSignature`, or (round 4) a signature placeholder such as `%GG` in a `pretty.<name>` alias used as `--format=<name>` or in `format.pretty`, which a bare `git log` reads; these are examples, not all of them; the verifier is itself a `code-reviewer` with Bash, and the titles, outputs and quotes it is sent are still lens text, so what stops it from running a command that text asks for is its prompt and the guard (round 2 corrected this bullet, which said nothing it runs can write); the lenses run with Bash under the guard and the harness's permission mode as well, and their prompt (no tests, no code of the change) is the only stop for what they choose to run, as for any `code-reviewer` call; so the panel is for a change in the session's own working tree, and a change from outside goes to a box.
- The offline test, criterion 3 (`1eae9bd`): `test_review_panel.py` runs the script under Node with a mock `agent()` (`workflow_harness.mjs`: the body as an async function, `parallel` turning a throw into null, `Date.now` and `Math.random` throwing as in the runtime). 20 cases: success, one lens null, a lens that throws, a verifier error, a verifier null, a finding with no evidence kind, seven other unusable findings, a missing verdict, nothing to verify, and five argument forms that are no diff path. No Node on PATH is `pytest.fail`, never a skip; CI's ubuntu-24.04 image and the box image carry Node. Not pinned in CI: the test job sets up Python only, so it runs the image's Node (the box pins 24.22.3, this Mac has 24.15.0); a `setup-node` step edits `.github/workflows/`, which is the owner's (infra finding 7 of round 1). After rounds 1 to 3, 30 cases (the read-only list as 33 commands let through and 69 refused, each with its reason; ten argument forms with no diff path or no head; a lens and the verifier at another head; the verifier sent only what a check needs). Planted breaks, rerun for the evidence folder (last bullet): the first list at `1eae9bd`, 31 of 32 caught; the one that passes there, `meta-not-literal`, is caught by the static test, which came one commit later (`330f0f7`). This line said "32 of 32" before: that count was taken with the static test present. The last list, round 1's 73 breaks with those of rounds 2 and 3 (83), at `0f7f6de`: 83 of 83 caught; again at `7a42f08` with a runner that runs the unbroken file first and names the test that failed (review 5b): 83 of 83, each by a failed test.
- The static test, criterion 4, step 1.5 (`330f0f7`): `test_workflow_pins.py` reads each `.claude/workflows/*.js` with a small tokenizer and runs nothing. `meta` must be a literal at the top. Every `agent()` call must have options written as an object literal at the call, with no spread, naming `model` as a literal tier or `claude-` id, or `agentType` as a literal whose agent file pins a model. `agent` may appear only as a call. It also checks each `workflows` entry against its file. Why a tokenizer and not Node: the test reads text, so a script under test never runs. Review round 1: the limit this bullet named ("a regular expression literal … can only fail a script") was false, as the correctness lens showed: a quote or backtick inside a regular expression hid an unpinned call, and so did a ternary alias, `eval`, and (found by this session's probe, not by a lens) a call inside a template's `${…}`, whose code the reader skipped. Now the code of `${…}` is read (in parentheses, so a comma there cannot pass a fake options object); a `/` where a regular expression may start, or inside `${…}`, fails the script (write `new RegExp('…')`); `eval`, `Function`, `arguments`, `constructor`, `this`, `globalThis` and a few more fail wherever they stand; an object key is only an `agent` after `{` or `,`; a computed key, method or getter in the options fails. Limit, named in the test's docstring: it reads plain scripts and is no sandbox; a script built to hide a call (a computed member reaching the Function constructor and a global `agent`, if the runtime has one) is for the review that writes the sha256.

On the real worktree, the worktree's guard 0.12.1 answers a `Workflow` call `{name: "review-panel"}` with silence and `{name: "other"}` with an ask (`hook pre` on stdin, telemetry to a scratch folder; at the head again for the evidence folder).
