## 2026-11-23 — box firewall: the box's GitHub token on the fetch of GitHub's ranges (item #83)

- What: `tools/box/firewall.py` sends the box's GitHub token (`GH_TOKEN`, which `bin/box` sets from the Keychain entry `agent-box-github`) on its one request to `https://api.github.com/meta`, so a box start draws on that token's own limit (5,000 calls an hour) instead of the 60 an hour that every unauthenticated start from the owner's address shares. The token goes in a header urllib does not carry across a redirect (`add_unredirected_header`): a header set the plain way does follow a redirect to another host (checked on Python 3.9.6 and 3.13.13; a test with two local servers pins it), so the token reaches that address and no other host. A token that is not letters, digits and underscores stops the start before the request, because http.client would otherwise put its text into the error on stderr. A 403 or 429 names the rate limit, its reset time and whether the token went with the request, where it said only `HTTP Error 403`; a header value is read only when it is at most ten digits. Without a token (`bin/box doctor`, `box_probe.py`) the fetch is as before; the start's line says which way the ranges were fetched. Why: on 2026-11-21 box `orch` (#68) stopped at start on GitHub's unauthenticated limit, used up by other box starts that hour. Compliance angle: a credential on one more request, to the API host the same token already serves inside the box (gh, git over HTTPS), never printed or logged; the log line records only whether it went with the request (ALCOA+: attributable, without the secret). Cost: $0 a month. Revisit if: GitHub stops serving `/meta` to the box's token, or the ranges get a cache (the scope limit of #83 rules one out today).
- Named, not fixed: `box.py`'s `check_github` sends the same token in a plain header, which urllib would carry to the target of a redirect from `api.github.com` to another host. `box.py` is outside this item's scope, so it is backlog item #92.
- Checks at `335498a` (host): `ruff check tools` clean; `pytest` 9486 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.9 and on 3.13. The 19 new tests in `tools/box/tests/test_image.py` failed before the change (in the rate-limit test the old code printed only `firewall: HTTP Error 403: refused`). Planted breaks: nine one-line breaks of `firewall.py` (the token header set the plain way, no form check, a header without a token, the HTTP error not read, 429 not read as a limit, used up and not used up swapped, the minutes to the reset, a negative header value shown, the start's line always saying "with the token"), each in a fresh copy of `tools/box` whose unbroken run passes 50 of 50 first: 9 of 9 caught on 3.13 and on 3.9, each with the test that caught it named in the run's output.
- Review round 1: `bin/fresh-review --base origin/main` at `325eb3f` (Opus, $0.43, COMMENT, 1 MINOR): a reset value of digits only but out of range made the date raise `OverflowError` inside the handler of the HTTP error, which `main` does not catch, so the start died with a traceback instead of the firewall's line (still exit 1, no session, no token in it); over 4,300 digits `int()` raised a `ValueError` on 3.11 and later. Fixed in `c235b41`: a header value of more than ten digits (epoch seconds up to the year 2286) is not read; a test covers 11, 20 and 5,000 digits and failed before the fix on both Pythons. Round 2, the delta `--base 325eb3f` at `c235b41` ($0.18): APPROVE, no finding.
- Checks at `c235b41`, the last code commit (host): `ruff check tools` clean; `pytest` 9489 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.9 and on 3.13 (22 new tests); the planted breaks again with a tenth (a header value of any length read): 10 of 10 caught on 3.13 and on 3.9, the unbroken copy passing 53 of 53 first.
- Probe record: this item changes `tools/box`, so the start gate would refuse every box after the merge until `box_probe.py` ran again. Run on the host for the image of `c235b41` (CLI 2.1.323): 11 of 11 questions hold, $0.0037, written to a scratch file first and only then taken into the `box` key of `tools/orchestration/verified.json` (`d995e6a`), so a run cut short by the rate limit could not leave an inconclusive record behind, as one did on 2026-11-21. It waited about 20 minutes for GitHub's unauthenticated limit to reset (0 of 60 left at 19:18 UTC, used by other starts); its own box starts fetch without a token and used 7 calls.
- Live start with the token: `bin/box run fw83 --brief` (one headless turn, $0.19, image `d995e6a`, judge `52fb4d2`, 2026-11-23 19:43 UTC): the entrypoint printed `firewall: set, 136 allowed networks from 5 names and GitHub's ranges (fetched with the box's GitHub token)`; GitHub's unauthenticated count for the owner's address stood at 45 before and after the start, so the fetch drew on the token's limit; no string of a GitHub token's form in the run's `stderr.txt`, `out.json` or the host's capture. The box `fw83` and its clone under `~/.agent-box/boxes/` can go (the owner's cleanup).

## 2026-11-23 — the graft test no longer depends on the clock (item #88)

- What: `test_a_graft_does_not_hide_a_change_to_the_guard` (`tools/guard/tests/test_box_profile.py`) failed now and then in full runs under load and passed alone. The cause, measured and written into #88: the test's graft `base head` made a cycle (the head's real parent is the base). Grafts switch git's commit-graph off, so `git merge-base` walks by commit date, and the test's own check that plain git is fooled (`merge-base == head`) held only when both commits fell in the same second. With a git shim that commits the head one second after the base it failed 5 of 5 times, at that assertion, on git 2.55.0. Decision: change only the test. The graft also makes the head a root (`base head`, then `head`), so there is no cycle, and the test pins the two committer dates one second apart, so the case that used to fail runs on every run. Why not only pin equal dates: that would rest on git breaking a date tie by insertion order. The guard is untouched and no verdict moves: its merge check reads no graft (`GIT_GRAFT_FILE=/dev/null` and `--no-replace-objects`, `guard.py:3610-3629`). Compliance: a test that fails at random teaches a rerun until green, which can hide a real failure, so the suite's result now depends on the code alone (validation evidence that can be repeated). Cost: $0 a month. Revisit if: git drops grafts (they are deprecated), because then the check that plain git is fooled fails every time and the test needs another way to fool it; the replace-object test beside it already covers that way.
- Checks at `2f23e6d` (in box `fix88`): `ruff check tools` clean; `pytest` 9489 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.9 and 9489 passed, 9 skipped on 3.13; the graft test passed 5 of 5 on 3.9. On the host before the box (tree of the patch on `3e76334`): `pytest` 9489 passed, 9 skipped on both Pythons; the old test failed 5 of 5 under a git shim that commits the head one second after the base, and passed 5 of 5 without it, while the file's other 242 tests passed under the shim; the fixed test passed 5 of 5 on each Python; the planted break (the fixed test with the old one-line graft put back) failed 5 of 5 at the `merge-base` assertion.
- Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18: Claude Code session `97ebec71-a248-4a16-85f3-1ceb5c867f28` on the owner's Mac merges the pull request of this block with the owner's token, after its checks are green on its head and a `bin/fresh-review` receipt for that head holds no open finding, by the command `python3 tools/orchestration/assert_run.py merge-ready <N>` prints for that head. The forge records the merge under the owner's login, so this line and the pull request body name the merging session (ALCOA+: attributable) until the agent identity of Phase 2 (item #10) replaces the owner's token. The two commits before this line's last edit were made in box `fix88` (seat `fix88`, headless session `b4780320-3113-471e-a1eb-ae158c96d0fa`, $0.49) from this session's brief; the review's one finding (MINOR, this line lacked the two sentences around this one) was answered by the merging session. No file under `.github/workflows/` is touched and no test is removed. #88 stays `status/review` until the owner's `owner done 88`.

## 2026-11-23 — the rules table: `docs/RULES.md` names what enforces each rule of `AGENTS.md`, and each relaxation has an end (item #70, plan step 1.6)

- How it is built: on the host, in the worktree `.claude/worktrees/rules-table` (branch `feat/rules-table` from main `52fb4d2`), by Claude Code session `31115d14` (model Claude Opus 5.5), one commit per topic. Claimed on 2026-11-23 at 19:48 UTC, once #83 was in review: the host seat holds one item at a time, and another session had claimed #83 at 18:56 UTC. The rows were drafted outside the repository while the seat was taken, and no file of the branch was written before the claim.
- Decision: a rule is an item of six sections of `AGENTS.md` (Non-negotiables, Coding principles, Working files, Default stack choices, Model routing, Orchestration): each numbered or bulleted item, its nested items in its place when it has some (NN-6.1 to NN-6.9), and each body row of a table. `## Harness notes` has no rows: it says which harness reads which file. The paragraph under the model table gives two rows of its own (MR-5, the fan-out limit; MR-6, the names at work), which the reader does not count. Each row quotes a fragment of its item word for word. Why: the test then fails when `AGENTS.md` gains a rule with no row, and when the quoted fragment is no longer in the item its id names (a change elsewhere in the item, such as the tier in a row of the model table, passes unseen); the alternative, a list of ids kept by hand in the test, lets a new rule pass unseen. Cost: none.
- Decision: five enforcer forms, `guard:NAME`, `ci:JOB`, `test:PATH::NAME`, `hook:PATH` and `judgment`, each checked from the files: the keys of the guard's `RULES` read with `ast` (the guard is not imported), the jobs of a workflow in `.github/workflows/` that a pull request starts (`pull_request` under `on:`), a test function pytest collects, read with `ast` (at module level or in a `Test*` class without `__init__`, in a file named `test_*.py` or `*_test.py` under pytest's `testpaths`), and a file whose path is one shell word of a hook's command in `.claude/settings.json`. A test marked to be skipped is not looked for: the test guard already fails a pull request that adds a skip marker (`ci:guards`). `hook:` is a fifth form beside the three the item names, because the tag scan and the test guard run as hooks in the session. A row can name enforcers and judgment together; its note says which part is left to judgment.
- What the table shows: of 44 rules, 13 name an enforcer and 31 rest on judgment alone, and no rule is carried by a check alone (every row keeps a part for judgment). The enforced ones are the commit message form, the CI checks with the test guard, history and tracker state (git-guard, protected-branch, tracker-guard), secrets, the box image, image tags, egress and cloud launches, the client boundary of a new target, host installs, model pins and the fan-out limit, and the merge ask. The five judgment controls of plan rev 3, D12, are rows of their own, and the test fails if one names an enforcer. The test requires each of those five rows, the nine relaxation rows and MR-5 and MR-6 by id, and fails on an id the reader does not know or on a second row with one id.
- Decision: nine relaxation rows: the six of the Phase 1 plan's section 5, the owner's two local switches (`FLEET_VERIFY=off` in `.claude/settings.local.json`, `rules_off` with `guard-config` and `delete-guard` in `.claude/guard.local.json`), and a ninth, S-1, the standing word of 2026-11-18, which relaxes the merge and the approval per step and ends at delivery too (section 11); a peer session (`agent-change-control-16`) pointed it out at intake. P5-1 ended on 2026-11-21 with PR #81 and stays as an ended row until delivery, so the record of the relaxation is kept. Settled inside the phase (the standing word): the owner's user-scope hook `~/.claude/hooks/ask-on-delete.sh` gets no row, since it is his user configuration and no switch in this repository; revisit if he wants his user-scope settings named.
- Decision: the relaxation test is dated on purpose (plan section 5): from 2027-01-01, or from a row's earlier end, `test_no_relaxation_is_in_force_past_its_end` fails on every machine and in CI. The fix is to end the row or to have the owner give it a new end (at most 2026-12-31 while the plan stands), never to edit the test. Its cost: a later run of this commit's suite is red once an end has passed. A run that only reproduces the evidence of a commit already merged deselects that one test by name (`--deselect tools/tests/test_rules_table.py::test_no_relaxation_is_in_force_past_its_end`) and says so with its evidence. A branch that is to be merged (a backport included) never deselects it: there the red test is the signal, and the row is ended or the owner renews its end. An ended row must say `ended` with its own end date, and that date must not lie after the run's day.
- Limits, named: the table says what enforces a rule, not how well (the guard matches command text); `ci:` counts every job of a workflow with a `pull_request` trigger and does not read `paths` or `branches` filters, a job's `if:` or `continue-on-error`, so a named job may not run on a given pull request or may not fail it (on 2026-11-24 `commit-msg` runs only on pull requests by its `if:`, no job of `ci.yml` has a trigger filter or `continue-on-error`, and `ci.yml` is the one workflow with a `pull_request` trigger; no test pins this); `test:` reads pytest's default collection rules and only `testpaths` from `pyproject.toml`, not `python_files`, `python_classes`, `addopts` or a conftest's `collect_ignore`, none of which this repository sets today, so such a setting could stop a named test from running with the table green; a hook command `shlex` cannot split stops the test with a traceback and no row's message (it fails closed); nobody counts how often a judgment rule is broken, so "broken twice gets a check or a dated exception" waits for a person to notice; `hook:` reads Claude Code's `.claude/settings.json` only, not Copilot's `.github/hooks/`; the local switches are named and cannot be read, since git does not track their files; the table is not in `kit.manifest`, so a task repository gets no rules table (the ST rows are about task repositories).
- Review: `bin/fresh-review` of `646d6a2` against main (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR and 5 MINOR, $0.65). Fixed in `2c31d20`: S-1 was not required, so its row could go with CI green (MAJOR); two of the five D12 rows and MR-5 and MR-6 were not required either, and a stray id passed; `hook:` matched any substring, `test:` any nested function, and `ci:` did not read a workflow's triggers; four branches of the checker had no test that makes them fire, and an ended row was not checked against its end. Answered in this block: the quote is a fragment and not the whole item (second bullet), and the dated test's cost to a later re-run (the relaxation test's bullet).
- Review, second round: the delta `--base 646d6a2` at `283db5a` (COMMENT, 4 MINOR, $1.64; a first try of that run ended with `claude exited 1` and no verdict). Fixed in `1dbbd51`: `hook:` still matched the tail of a path, now one shell word of the command, with a root-level file of the same name in the test; `test:` accepted a function pytest never collects. A planted break of each (the old match, any class counting) is caught by `test_an_enforcer_counts_only_where_it_runs`. Answered here: `ci:` reads no trigger filter, job `if:` or `continue-on-error` (Limits), and the deselect is for reproduction runs only, never on a branch to be merged.
- Review, third round: the delta `--base 283db5a` at `eaf8adb` (COMMENT, 3 MINOR, $0.37): `test:` reads pytest's defaults and not the settings that change them, a hook command `shlex` cannot split raises, and the limit of `ci:` rests on a claim no test pins. Each fails closed or rests on a setting this repository does not have, so each is named in the Limits bullet and not fixed here. At `eaf8adb` the suite on Python 3.13 had one failure, `test_a_graft_does_not_hide_a_change_to_the_guard` (the flaky test of item #88, a file this branch does not touch), which passed three times run alone; the merge waits for the checks of the pull request.
- Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18: Claude Code session `31115d14-2c43-48b2-8953-764d9092a6d2` (model Claude Opus 5.5) on the owner's Mac merges the pull request of this block with the owner's token, after its checks are green on its head and a `bin/fresh-review` receipt for that head holds no open finding, by the command `python3 tools/orchestration/assert_run.py merge-ready <N>` prints for that head. The forge records the merge under the owner's login, so this line and the pull request body name the merging session (ALCOA+: attributable) until the agent identity of Phase 2 (item #10) replaces the owner's token. No file under `.github/workflows/` is touched and no test is removed. The item stays `status/review` until the owner's line for Phase 1.
- Cost: $0 a month; the test runs in the existing `test` job. Compliance: an auditor reads per rule whether a check or a person carries it, and a relaxation shows its end and turns CI red when it outlives it; the rows are reviewed with the pull request like code. What would change it: a judgment rule broken twice (a check or a dated exception), a new kind of enforcer (the ruleset of step 2.2 lives on the forge, not in the repository, and would need a reader), or the owner striking the ninth row.

## 2026-11-24 — the box clone runs the commit-msg gate (item #85)

- What changed: `prepare()` in `tools/box/box.py` sets `core.hooksPath .githooks` in the work clone at every start, after the clone-or-reuse step and the boundary check, so a clone made by an older wrapper gets it too. It runs inside a throwaway container the way `clone_facts` reads the clone (`--network none`, uid 1000, `--entrypoint sh`, no token), never as the owner's own `git` against the clone, and only when the clone has `.githooks/commit-msg`; a clone without it gets no setting. A failed step raises `BoxError` naming the box. Why: PR #84 failed CI's commit-msg check on a box commit (subject 74 characters), because the box clone had no hooksPath. The README's box section adds the host's check after a box run: `python3 tools/commit_msg.py --range <merge-base>..<head>` in the host worktree, beside `ruff check tools` and both `pytest` runs.
- Limit, named: the session inside the box controls its clone, `.git/config` included, so it can unset the setting or point it elsewhere; CI's commit-msg check stays the backstop. What would change it: a hook the session cannot change (for example from the judge, as the managed settings are).
- Checks: `ruff check tools` passed; `python3.9 -m pytest` 9528 passed, 9 skipped; `python3.13 -m pytest` 9528 passed, 9 skipped; a planted break (the old `prepare`, no hooksPath step) failed all four tests of `tools/box/tests/test_hooks_path.py`.
- Host fix after the box: the box's first version ran the step through `docker_argv`, so the image's entrypoint started the firewall, which fetched GitHub's ranges without the box's token (the step passed no token environment): one more call against the unauthenticated limit of 60 an hour at every start, the limit item #83 moved off, and a start refused once it was spent. The step now needs no network and no entrypoint; the test asserts `--network none` and no token variable (it failed on the box's version), and a run of `set_hooks_path` with `agent-box:57d564b` on two scratch repositories set `.githooks` with the hook and nothing without it.
- Probe record: this item changes `tools/box`, so the start gate would refuse every box after the merge until `box_probe.py` ran again. Run on the host for the image of `5d448e3` (CLI 2.1.323): 11 of 11 questions hold, $0.0034, written to a scratch file first and only then taken into the `box` key of `tools/orchestration/verified.json`, as for item #83; and again for the image of `4e827f9` after the review fix to the test under `tools/box/tests/` (the gate counts every file under `tools/box`): 11 of 11 hold, $0.0016.
- Review: `bin/fresh-review` at `9ced946` (COMMENT, 2 MINOR, $1.18): the test read the host's global git config (`config --get` without `--local`), and no assert pinned the step's `--user 1000:1000` and `no-new-privileges`; both fixed in the commit after it, the second shown by a planted break (the step without `--user` fails the test).
- Built in box `b85` from a brief by host session `31115d14`; the host session added the probe record and the fixes above. Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18: Claude Code session `31115d14-2c43-48b2-8953-764d9092a6d2` (model Claude Opus 5.5) on the owner's Mac, with the owner's token, after CI is green on the head and a `bin/fresh-review` receipt for that head holds no open finding, by the command `python3 tools/orchestration/assert_run.py merge-ready 96` prints. No file under `.github/workflows/` is touched and no test is removed.
- Cost: $0 a month. Compliance: the commit-msg gate holds inside the box at commit time, not only in CI.

## 2026-11-24 — fresh-review receipts: a hash anyone can recompute, and a copy on the pull request (item #86)

- What: `tools/review/fresh_review.py` makes the diff the egress screen reads, the model reviews and `diff_sha256` covers with one command, `git diff --full-index --no-ext-diff --no-color --no-textconv --diff-algorithm=myers --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ --unified=3 --find-renames --no-relative --indent-heuristic --inter-hunk-context=0 --submodule=short --ignore-submodules=none -O/dev/null -l1000 <base sha>...<head sha>` with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, full shas and no ref names (the seven flags after `--find-renames` came with review rounds two, three and six; each is git's default, so a clone with no such config gets the same text). The flags pin what a repository's `.git/config` could change; the two variables remove the system and global files. Each receipt adds `diff_command` (that command as one shell line), `merge_base`, `base_tree` and `head_tree`; no field is removed or renamed, so old receipts stay readable. `base_tree` is the tree of the merge base, not of `base_sha`, because the `...` form diffs from the merge base. A diff that is not UTF-8 is now exit 2 with a message (before, a traceback), so the hashed bytes and the reviewed text stay one. Source: rounds 6 to 8 of the review of PR #84, where an old hash reproduced only with pinned abbreviation and isolated config.
- Decision: the lasting copy is a comment on the pull request (`--pr N`, `gh pr comment N --body-file`, argv and no shell; `--post-only` posts the newest local receipt of HEAD). Why: it costs no commit on the branch and stays with the review on the forge after the clone is gone; a committed receipt would change the head it reviews. This option was recommended in the plan the owner approved on 2026-11-24 ("yeah do them in parallel"); he did not name it separately. A failed `gh` or a body over 65,000 characters (GitHub's limit is 65,536) exits 2, keeps the local receipt and prints the retry `--pr N --post-only`. `assert_run.py merge-ready` and the guard's box merge still read the local receipt in `<git dir>/fresh-review/`, unchanged. What would change it: a forge other than GitHub as the record, or the owner preferring receipts committed in the repository.
- Limits, named: the post goes out under whatever `gh` login runs the review (the owner's on the host, the box token in a box), and a session can skip `--pr`; nothing enforces the post yet. The repository's own `.git/config` keys that no flag pins (for example `core.quotePath`; `diff.suppressBlankEmpty`, which has no flag; `core.bigFileThreshold`, under which a blob above it shows as binary to the screen and the model alike, set only in the reviewing clone's own config, which also writes the receipt) can still change the text in an odd clone. The reviewing process drops every `GIT_CONFIG*` variable before the diff, so config given through the environment (`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` with its `KEY_n`/`VALUE_n`) does not reach it; since the second review round `GIT_DIFF_OPTS` (which overrides `--unified`) is dropped as well, and the recorded `diff_command` starts with `env -u GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS -u GIT_CONFIG_COUNT -u GIT_DIFF_OPTS`, so an auditor's shell cannot change it that way (`env -u` is in macOS, GNU coreutils and BusyBox; not POSIX). Git older than 2.32 ignores `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` without a warning. Attributes are not pinned: `git diff` reads `.gitattributes` from the working tree and `.git/info/attributes`, so a `diff=<driver>` funcname pattern changes hunk headers, and a `-diff` or `binary` attribute shows a text change as `Binary files ... differ` to the screen and the model alike (older than this item). The PR head check compares commit ids only, so a fork with the same head would pass it.
- Checks (in box `b86`): `ruff check tools` clean; `pytest` 9531 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.9 and 9531 passed, 9 skipped on 3.13. Planted breaks: without `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` `test_hash_ignores_a_global_diff_config` fails; with the failed post returning the verdict's code `test_a_failed_post_is_exit_2_and_keeps_the_receipt` and `test_an_oversized_comment_is_not_posted` fail. The config test's global file also sets `core.quotePath=false` beside `diff.algorithm=patience` and `diff.noprefix=true`, and the test repository holds a non-ASCII file name: the flags already override the two `diff.` keys, so with them alone the first planted break passed, and only a key no flag pins shows that the global file is not read.
- Review: `fresh_review.py` of `main` (not this branch's own copy) at `d3668a9` (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR and 4 MINOR, $1.50). Fixed in the host commit after it, each shown by a planted break that fails its test: `--pr N` posted without checking that pull request N's head is the reviewed commit, so a mistyped N or a clone with several remotes put the evidence on the wrong pull request with a success line (MAJOR; now `gh pr view N --json headRefOid` must equal the receipt's `sha`, else exit 2 and no comment); config from the environment changed the hashed text (now dropped); a receipt without `sha` or `verdict`, or not an object, gave a traceback on `--post-only` (now exit 2 with a reason); no test fed a non-UTF-8 diff or a receipt with three backticks (both added). Attributes and git older than 2.32 are named in Limits, not fixed.
- Review, second round: `main`'s `fresh_review.py` at `4b6ac4f` against the new `main` (COMMENT, 6 MINOR, $0.72). Fixed in the host commit after it, each shown by a planted break: `GIT_DIFF_OPTS` overrode the pinned `--unified` (now unset); `diff.relative`, `diff.orderFile`, `diff.indentHeuristic`, `diff.interHunkContext` and `diff.submodule` had flags but were not pinned (now pinned at git's defaults; `diff.relative` with a subdirectory as cwd dropped files from the reviewed diff); the recorded command depended on the auditor's shell (now `env -u ...`); a receipt with `findings: null` or non-string `models` gave a traceback that exits 1, the BLOCKER code (the summary line now reads those fields whatever their type); the comment did not show the range reviewed, so a delta review read as a full one (the summary line now says `reviewed <base>...<head>`); and no test made `gh pr view` fail (two cases added). Git in the box image is 2.47.3 and on the owner's Mac 2.55.0, both past 2.28, which `--no-relative` needs.
- Review, third round: the delta `--base 4b6ac4f` at `d1fe209` (COMMENT, 2 MINOR, $0.54). Fixed in the host commit after it: `diff.ignoreSubmodules` or a `submodule.<name>.ignore = all` that a pull request adds in `.gitmodules` dropped a submodule pointer change from the screen and the model (now `--ignore-submodules=none`); and four of the flags of round two had no test, so the round-two line above claimed more than it showed. A new test sets `diff.orderFile`, `diff.interHunkContext`, `diff.submodule=log` and `diff.ignoreSubmodules=all` in the repository's config, checks that each changes plain git's output, and requires the same hash; removing `--inter-hunk-context=0`, `--submodule=short`, `--ignore-submodules=none` or `-O/dev/null` fails it (four planted breaks). The `.gitmodules` route has its own test (fourth round, below). `--indent-heuristic` has no test that fails without it (a case where the heuristic moves a hunk would be built for the test alone), and `diff.renameLimit` is named in Limits.
- Review, fourth round: the delta `--base d1fe209` at `5f7c1a8` (COMMENT, 4 MINOR, $0.44), all on what the record and the tests claim. Fixed in the host commit after it: the `.gitmodules` route had no test (a new one commits a `.gitmodules` with `ignore = all`, checks that plain git hides the pointer, and requires it in the reviewed diff; removing `--ignore-submodules=none` fails it); the README read as full coverage (now "most" diff settings, the others named here); the reason for leaving `diff.renameLimit` unpinned was imprecise (now stated as above); and the test's plain-git calls read the runner's global config (now isolated, so a global `diff.submodule=log` cannot fail them).
- Review, fifth and sixth rounds, both on the record's wording: the fifth (at `e9013fc`, COMMENT, 2 MINOR, $0.40) found the `diff.renameLimit` text imprecise and the README list read as complete, and `9f26ea5` reworded both (the list is examples now); the sixth (at `9f26ea5`, COMMENT, 2 MINOR, $1.13) found the reworded text still wrong (since git 2.33 cheap steps pair some renames at any limit, and a delete plus an add shows the unchanged lines too) and its reason for leaving the flag unpinned weak. The host commit after it pins `-l1000`, git's documented default, so the key leaves the Limits line; a test sets `diff.renameLimit=1` on a file renamed under another basename and edited, with a second added path, checks that plain git then shows a delete and an add, and requires the same hash; removing `-l1000` fails it.
- Built in box `b86` from a brief by host session `31115d14`; the host session made the review fixes above. Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18: Claude Code session `31115d14-2c43-48b2-8953-764d9092a6d2` (model Claude Opus 5.5) on the owner's Mac, with the owner's token, after CI is green on the head and a `bin/fresh-review` receipt for that head holds no open finding, by the command `python3 tools/orchestration/assert_run.py merge-ready 97` prints. No file under `.github/workflows/` is touched and no test is removed.
- Cost: $0 a month. Compliance: ALCOA+ enduring and original: the review evidence outlives the reviewing clone and an auditor can recompute what the model saw.

## 2026-11-24 — the action probes: four answers from runs of `claude-code-action` v1.0.269 (item #39, plan step 0.3)

- What ran: a `workflow_dispatch` workflow in a private scratch repository of the owner's (created for this item on 2026-11-24, for the owner to delete after it; not named here, by the item's scope limit) ran `claude-code-action` at `61a9c274fa7110a66dfafc1bbbced03833eb25fe` (v1.0.269, 2026-11-06) with the subscription token from a GitHub environment secret that accepts `main` only, on `claude-haiku-5-5` with `--max-turns 8`, permissions as allow rules plus `dontAsk` in the action's `settings` input, and a job token with `contents: read`. The step after the action read its `execution_file` and kept answers only (versions, counts, yes/no, the probe's own commands), never reply or output text. Runs are named here by id; all ran on 2026-11-24 and their answer artifacts keep 7 days, so this block is the lasting record. Three sets: set 1 (five runs at 11:35 UTC) lost four answers to a crash in the reading step (a `message` that was a string), fixed in the scratch repository; set 2 (45388806607 to 45388828876, 11:56 UTC) answered all five cases, but the probe's own allow rules denied its compound `sleep 45 && touch` command, or a detached form of it, in three of the four background cases (bg-bash, bg-bash-off and bg-agent-off; bg-agent, run 45388823016, was denied nothing); set 3 (45401134863 to 45401149678, 13:13 UTC) reran the four background cases with one allowed command, `sh probe/slow.sh`, and also kept each Bash call's command, background flag and error flag.
- Q1, does `execution_file` carry `permission_denials`: yes. In run 45388806607 (case denials-env) the step went green, the result message said `subtype: success`, `is_error: false` after 5 turns, and its `permission_denials` listed the refused `cat /etc/hostname` with tool `Bash`; the result message of every run that answered carried the key, and in run 45388828876 a subagent's refused call is listed in the parent's result. So plan fact 3 holds in the action too (a denial shows only in that list, not in the step's outcome), and the post-step of plan D4, item 4, can fail a job on a non-empty list read from that file. What would change it: an action version that stops writing the full result message to `execution_file`, which only a rerun at that bump shows.
- Q2, does `settings` env reach the CLI: yes, and so do the other two routes. In run 45388806607 a variable in the `settings` input's `env`, one on the action step's `env:` and one on the job's `env:` each reached the CLI's Bash tool with its value. The report quoted in plan fact 2 (a step-level `env:` did not reach the CLI) does not hold at v1.0.269 (a dated note under the plan's status points here), and the action's own comment that its step `env:` shadows job-level variables did not hold for a variable it does not list itself. Decision: plan D4, item 3, stands: the variables the CI job relies on, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS` among them, go through the `settings` input ("Claude Code settings as JSON string or path" in the action's own words); why: it held here, and unlike the two `env:` routes it does not rest on how a composite action passes its caller's env. What would change it: a later action version where the `settings` env does not reach the CLI.
- Q3, does disabling background tasks cure the green-but-empty run: yes for a background subagent, the case of the green-but-empty reports; for a background shell command it turns the loss into an error and a denial, which the post-step can catch. Without the setting a background subagent left a green run with its work never done, not even 60 s after the action, three times (runs 45385601317, 45388823016, 45401144886: the result message came within 3 s); with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1` in the `settings` env the subagent ran in the foreground, its refused call was in the parent's result (run 45388828876) and its file existed when the action ended, the result coming at 55 s (run 45401149678). A background Bash call without the setting started, the result came after 2 s and the command never finished (run 45401134863); with the setting the call came back as an error and the model tried the shell's own `nohup sh probe/slow.sh > /dev/null 2>&1 &`, which `dontAsk` denied (run 45401140332, one run). Decision: plan D4, items 1, 3 and 4, stand with one addition for Phase 2: the job sets that variable through `settings`, its allow rules stay narrow (none admits `nohup`, `&` or another way to detach a command), and its post-step fails the job on a non-empty `permission_denials` and also on any tool call in `execution_file` that asked for the background, an Agent call included; why: the setting cures the subagent case and the shell route stays open to any job whose rules admit it, and the Agent part is fail-closed on purpose, at a cost: with the setting on, one of the two subagent calls still asked for the background and ran in the foreground (run 45388828876), so the rule would have failed that good run, and a rerun can fail again since the model's choice varies; a missed one is the green-but-empty run, for example in a job where the setting did not take effect. Phase 2 narrows the Agent part to a call whose result came back as a background launch, once a probe has recorded what such a result looks like. What would change it: an action release that waits for background tasks before it reads the result (the action's issues #1499, #1852 and #1646 of plan fact 2), or a CLI that also refuses a shell-detached command under the setting.
- Q4, does pinning the action SHA pin the CLI version: yes for the version, not for the bytes. Every run that answered reports `claude_code_version` 2.1.319 (run 45385601317, the five of set 2, the four of set 3), the version `src/entrypoints/run.ts` hard-codes at that commit (line 80). But the action fetches the installer at run time without a hash (`curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash -s -- 2.1.319`, the same file, line 53), and installs its own dependencies with `bun install --production` (`action.yml`, line 223) from its committed `bun.lock` without `--frozen-lockfile`. Decision: pin the action by full SHA and read a CLI version move as an action bump that reruns these probes; a strict repository that must pin the bytes uses the action's `path_to_claude_code_executable` input with a binary whose checksum the job checks. What would change it: an action release that verifies the installer, or a client rule that demands pinned bytes for every repository.
- Limits, named: one model (Haiku), one CLI version, one action version, a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` runner and short prompts; whether background work finished was judged by one file the probe's command writes, not from the CLI's process list; the model's own choices vary between runs (with the setting on, the subagent call of set 2 still asked for the background and the one of set 3 did not; both ran in the foreground). The answers hold for v1.0.269 and go stale with an action bump. Gap, named: the probe's files (the workflow, the case and answer scripts, `slow.sh`) live in the scratch repository and go with it, and the answer artifacts expire after 7 days, so after that nothing here can be recomputed and a rerun at a bump must write the probe again; plan step 3.1, the scheduled probe run, is where they would come into this repository.
- Cost: $0 in money (the subscription token; 14 Haiku runs of 2 to 5 turns against the plan's limits) and about 24 billed minutes of GitHub Actions on a private repository (20 minutes of run time), inside the free allowance. Compliance angle: the token lived only in the scratch repository's environment secret (set by the owner from a local terminal), never in this repository, a transcript or a log; the answer files hold no reply text; the run ids and the date make each answer traceable while the scratch repository and its 7-day artifacts exist; after that only this block's account remains and nothing can be recomputed from it (the gap in the Limits line).

## 2026-11-24 — guard 0.12.2: an edit to an agent definition asks, as one to a workflow script does (item #90)

- Decision: `guard-config` names "an agent definition": `.claude/agents` itself and every path below it (the repo's, a nested project's, `~/.claude/agents`), and every path at or below the folder that the `.claude/agents` link of the call's folder (the event's cwd), of the project folder the harness names (`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, review round 2) or of a folder above either points to (`agents/` in this kit). Only those links are read; the path is compared as text and never resolved, so no folder or mount it names is touched. A Write, an Edit, a shell write or a GitHub file tool there asks on the host, is logged in a box, whose merge check then refuses the head, and is denied in cloud. Why: an agent type takes its tools and prompt from that file in every workflow and Agent call of that type, and the sha256 binding of 0.12.1 covers only the workflow's script (finding infra-1 of the review panel's first run, on item #69). Compliance: the tools and prompt of every agent type change only past a human (the ask on the host, the owner's merge for a box), so an agent that runs is one a human confirmed, and the ask and the `logged` line are in the decision log (audit trail). Cost: $0 a month. What would change it: binding the agent files by hash, as `workflows` binds a script (the item's scope limit).
- "Denied in a box", read as the plan put it and the owner approved ("go", 2026-11-24): as for `.claude/workflows/**` since 0.12.0, the box table keeps `guard-config: log`, so the edit runs and leaves a `logged` line, and the box's merge check refuses a head that changes one. The check reads the link in the judge's folder (`guard_root()`, the read-only export of main), so a session cannot hide `agents/**` by changing its own clone; where the environment also names a `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, that folder's link is read too, which can only add refusals. Refusing the edit itself in a box would need a box entry of its own for this class; not done.
- Version 0.12.2: one more path class in an existing rule, with no new rule or setting, as 0.12.1 tightened 0.12.0.
- The older tests: five inputs of the never-weaker corpus pinned an agent definition as silent (FILE_ROWS:27 of `test_table_forms.py` and the fixtures `.claude/agents/x.md` and `~/.claude/agents/x.md` of both harnesses). The two fixture cases moved to ask (renamed with `git mv`; no test removed), and their allow slots took neighbours that stay silent. The five changes are listed, stricter only, in `test_never_weaker.py` and `test_guard_0120.py` (a box answer from silent to `logged` counts as stricter) and left out of the equality in `test_box_profile.py`, as Workflow calls were at 0.12.0; `test_guard_0122.py` checks that exactly those five are listed or left out. `baseline-0.10.0.jsonl` and `baseline-0.11.0.jsonl` read their corpus from the fixtures on disk, so both were rewritten by their own `--write` commands with guard 0.10.0 (`6a1e3f6`) and 0.11.0 (`1b35952`): sorted, only the four old and eight new fixture lines differ and every other line is byte-identical; the new sha256 of the 0.11.0 file is `4ea117ef2e6080cfaa26ef634151e195fcc7ae6559e7659eccb0c028731eba96`.
- Limits, named: a link in a nested project below those folders, or in another repository the path points into, is not read (the nested project's own `.claude/agents/**` still asks by its parts); a write through another link into an agent folder (`ln -s "$PWD/agents" /tmp/a`, then a write to `/tmp/a/x.md`) is read as written, as every path of `guard-config` is; a harness that sets no `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (Claude Code sets it for hooks, as the hook commands of `.claude/settings.json` use it; Copilot's hook runners are not checked here) reads the link of the session's folder only, so there a session folder outside the project leaves a write to the project's `agents/` silent; a link to a single file inside `.claude/agents` (the owner's `~/.claude/agents` holds such links into another repository) counts when the edit names the link, not its target; the agents folder under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, plugin agent folders and Copilot's `.github/agents/**` are not covered (backlog #100); the shell limits of 0.10.0 hold (a branch switch, merge, pull, rebase or stash that changes these files; a patch whose target is not on the line); the owner's `.claude/guard.local.json` turns `guard-config` off in his clone, so there the new asks stay silent until he deletes it.

- Review: `bin/fresh-review` at `ab91960` (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR and 2 MINOR, $1.67), fixed in the commit after it. ## 2026-11-26 — bin/box keeps the GitHub token off a redirect (item #92)

- What: `check_github` in `tools/box/box.py` sends the box's GitHub token (Keychain entry `agent-box-github`) on its one request to `https://api.github.com/repos/<slug>` in a header urllib does not carry across a redirect (`add_unredirected_header`), as `firewall.py` does since #83. Before, the token sat in `Request(headers=...)`, which urllib copies onto the request to a redirect's target, another host included. Why: a redirect from `api.github.com` must not hand the token to any other host. What would change it: a move off urllib (the new client needs the same test), or a GitHub API host other than `api.github.com`. Cost: $0 a month. Compliance: the credential reaches only the host it is meant for (the agreed boundary), and the test below is the validation evidence. Unchanged, as the item's scope limit says: which token is read, the refusal messages and the host called; `Accept` and `User-Agent` still follow a redirect, as they carry nothing secret.
- One outcome changes, kept fail-closed on purpose: the token is left off a redirect that stays on `api.github.com` too, and GitHub answers a request for a renamed or transferred repository with a 301 to `/repositories/<id>`. For a private repository whose clone remote still names the old slug, `bin/box` then refuses the start with "cannot see the repository", although the token is valid; before this change that start passed. Remedy: point the remote at the repository's current name (`git remote set-url origin <url>`). Not narrowed, because putting the token back on a same-host redirect takes a redirect handler of its own, more code than the case is worth. This repository is not hit: its remote and the seven box clones under `~/.agent-box/boxes/` name `kacper38/agent-change-control`, and it is public, so an unauthenticated read would succeed. The new test's redirect target is the same address on another port, so it pins the same-host case as well. What would change it: a private client repository that gets renamed while boxes run on it.
- Checks at `a8380c5` (host): `ruff check tools` clean; `pytest` 9607 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.9.6 and on 3.13.13. The new test `test_check_github_keeps_the_token_off_a_redirect` (two local servers, the first answers 302 to the second; `check_github`'s own request is pointed at the first) failed on the code before the change on both Pythons, with the redirect target receiving `Bearer secret-value`; that old line is the planted break.
- Probe record, outside the item's listed scope: this item changes `tools/box`, so the start gate (`probe_gate`) would refuse every box after the merge until `box_probe.py` ran again, as for #83 and #85. Run on the host for the image of `a8380c5` (CLI 2.1.323): 11 of 11 questions hold, $0.0036, written to a scratch file first and only then taken into the `box` key of `tools/orchestration/verified.json` (`82da8b6`); no other key changed.
- Review, first round: `bin/fresh-review --base origin/main` at `d65fec3` (Opus, $1.08, COMMENT, 2 MINOR), answered in the commit after it. (1) A renamed repository's 301 on the same host now arrives without the token: named in the second bullet, kept fail-closed. (2) The Merger line held a placeholder for the pull request: it names 102 now.
- Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18: Claude Code session `223fed05-7cdd-43c4-97a5-2ef579b8f0e9` (model Claude Opus 5.5) on the owner's Mac, with the owner's token, after CI is green on the head and a `bin/fresh-review` receipt for that head holds no open finding, by the command `python3 tools/orchestration/assert_run.py merge-ready 102` prints for that head. The forge records the merge under the owner's login, so this line and the pull request name the merging session (ALCOA+: attributable). No file under `.github/workflows/` is touched and no test is removed. #92 stays `status/review` until the owner's `owner done 92`.

## 2026-11-26 — tracker 0.3.0: the owner delegates `ready` and `done` to a session, and the record says a session did it (item #103)

- Asked and answered. On 2026-11-26 the owner ran `owner done 85 86 92` at his terminal, and the tracker refused it for unticked acceptance boxes: the line a session had given him lacked `--accept-all`. At 11:09:52 UTC he wrote to that session (Claude Code session `46be8305-148b-45c4-88ee-d81817781b77` on his machine, model Claude Fable 5.2): "there must be a way for you to run that owner done and ready commands". The session answered with what stops it (the tracker's terminal check `is_human()` and the guard rule `tracker-guard`), wrote that it would not get past them by faking a terminal, and proposed four things: two agent verbs beside the owner ones, recorded as delegated with the session id; a switch of one committed line in `.claude/guard.json` on `main`, refused where the boundary is `client`; for `done` the same checks, with evidence per criterion in place of his ticks; this block and a dated row in `docs/RULES.md`, ended when plan step 2.2 is in force. It named the cost in these words: "until Phase 2, a change in this repository would reach `main` and be closed with no act of yours in the record. The only human acts left would be your plan approval and what you say in chat." It offered "done only" as the narrower choice. He answered at 11:25:19 UTC, verbatim and in full: "go". The evidence of his words is that transcript and this block, which the session that gains the right wrote itself, the limit the block of 2026-11-18 already names for his standing word.
- Decision: `tracker delegate ready N [N ...] --word TEXT` and `tracker delegate done N [N ...] --word TEXT --evidence FILE` (tracker 0.3.0). They act only where `.claude/guard.json` on the default branch of the forge says `"boundary": "own"` and `"owner_verbs": "delegated"`, and this repository's file says so from commit `207fd11` of this pull request on. Why: his words; and the other way to give him what he asked for, a faked terminal, would make a done mark say that a person accepted work nobody looked at, which is the one thing that mark is for. What would change it: his word at any time (the line leaves by a one-line pull request or a revert of that commit); plan steps 2.1 and 2.2 in force, when his `/approve` becomes the human act on a change (row S-2 of `docs/RULES.md`, at the latest 2026-12-31). Cost: $0 a month. Compliance (ALCOA+, attributable): the forge shows the owner's login for his acts and for a session's, because a session works with his token; what tells them apart is the mark, which says `delegated: true`, names the session, quotes the words it acted on and names the blob of the file that delegated. A delegated act is never recorded as his own.
- What this supersedes, in this repository and while the line stands. The sentence of the block of 2026-11-18 "The tracker's owner verbs (`owner ready`, `owner done`) stay with him at his terminal: the tracker gives them to no session", and line 1 of section 6 of `docs/PLAN-2026-11-phase-1.md` ("A session does not close an item"), which named this alternative as his to take by striking the line: "in a `boundary: own` repository a session moves `review → done` and the done mark says so". That alternative covers `done`; `ready` goes beyond it, and he asked for both and took "go" over "done only". The owner verbs themselves are unchanged: they still refuse a session, and `tracker-guard` still denies a session's `tracker owner ...` (`tools/tests/test_tracker_guard_verdicts.py` pins both verdicts on the real hook).
- A verb family of its own, not a looser `owner`. Why: `owner` keeps one meaning, a human at a terminal, and the guard needs no change, since its rule matches the word `owner` after the script; no rule, fixture or baseline moves. What would change it: a wish to have the guard log or ask on delegated calls, which is a guard change with an item of its own.
- The switch is read on the default branch of the forge, never in a working tree (`committed_file`, on GitHub the contents API without a ref). Why: a session cannot delegate to itself in a branch; the line has to pass a pull request's gate first. The key sits in `.claude/guard.json` because `boundary` already lives there and an edit of that file asks a human wherever the owner has not silenced `guard-config`; the guard itself ignores the key. Read by this session against the real forge on 2026-11-26: `gh api repos/kacper38/agent-change-control/contents/.claude/guard.json` answers with the blob id and base64 content, and a path that is not there answers `HTTP 404`, the text `committed_file` reads as "not there"; `has_path` at commit `3aeba29` says yes to a file and to a folder and no to a path that is not there. What would change it: a forge without such a read (Azure DevOps today: the verbs refuse there, `tools/tracker/ado.py` is unchanged).
- Evidence in place of ticks. `delegate done` runs `done_checks` as `owner done` does (frozen body, claim, review record, a merged pull request with `Refs #N` that no bot merged, green checks on the merge commit) and wants, for every acceptance criterion, one line that points at something that exists: a file or folder in the tree of the merge commit (`has_path`, the contents API at that commit; up to six paths of a line are tried), or a URL under the repository's own address on the forge. It ticks no box and waives nothing, so a ticked box stays a person's sign. Marks keep their kinds (`ready`, `done`) and gain fields, so `label_lint.py`, `done_checks` and `evidence_export.py` read them as before and an evidence pack carries the fields. What it does not do: check that what a line points at proves its criterion. A URL is matched by its start and is not fetched.
- Refusals, each before any write: a box (the marker); `KZ_BOUNDARY` set to anything but `own`; no `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, or one that is not a plain token; a `--word` or an evidence line that is empty, over 500 characters, more than one line, or holds `<!--`, `-->` or `--!>` (part of the record would not show on the forge); the Azure DevOps backend. `install.sh` drops the line when it writes a new target's `.claude/guard.json`, so no repository inherits the kit owner's delegation; a new target's boundary stays `client` by default, where the verbs refuse anyway.
- Named limits, for a reader of the record. (1) The quoted words and the session id are the session's claim: the tracker checks their form, not their truth, and the transcript that holds his words is on his machine, not in the repository. (2) Until step 2.1 gives sessions an identity of their own, the forge cannot tell the owner from a session, and a session that ignored the protocol could run a changed copy of the tracker or call the forge directly with his token. That holds for every tracker rule today and is not new here; what is new is a sanctioned path with no human act in it. (3) The grant itself is the owner's act (the Merger line and the review bullet below), but the standing word (S-1) still lets a session merge a later pull request, whose gate is CI and a fresh-context review, not a person. The rule in the next bullet keeps a change to the line or to the delegation check out of that; nothing enforces the rule. Steps 2.1, 2.2 and 2.6 close this. (4) With the line on `main`, an item can go from backlog to closed with no act of the owner on record, and `delegate ready` lets a session freeze the scope of work it then does itself. His acts left: one plan approval per phase, what he writes to a session, and his read of the record. (5) The working tree's `guard.json` and `guard.local.json` are not read, so a local tightening of the boundary does not stop the verbs; `KZ_BOUNDARY` does. (6) Only Claude Code exports `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`; under another harness the verbs refuse.
- How a session uses them (this session's reading, his to strike): `delegate ready` only for an item whose scope he has agreed to in words of his own, quoted in `--word`; `delegate done` only after the merge, with each evidence line checked against `main` by the session that writes it; an item with a criterion the session could not check is handed to him. A pull request that adds, changes or removes the `owner_verbs` line, or changes the delegation check in `tools/tracker/tracker.py`, is merged by the owner and not under S-1, and its item is promoted and closed with his own verbs.
- Item #103 and the order of its record. The item was filed at 11:29 UTC and amended twice while still in backlog; two notes on the item say what changed and why (the installer, the id of row S-2, and the evidence rule, the second time after the first review). The build started on his "go" before the item was ready, because he had not typed `owner ready 103` yet. No delegated verb is used on #103: he promotes it with `owner ready 103` before the merge and closes it with `owner done 103` after it, at his terminal. Both verbs refuse a session, so these two marks are the acts of his that the record can tell from a session's. The claim and the review record follow his `owner ready`, and their times will say that the build came first.
- Checks at `26a4a4e` (host): `ruff check tools` clean; `pytest` 9710 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.9.6 and on 3.13.13 (9607 passed on `main` before). The engineer planted ten breaks, each caught by the test aimed at it. Before the review: the box check off, the `owner_verbs` check off, a missing evidence line accepted, the installer's `sed` deletion off, the installer's check before any copy off. After it: any path counting as existing, `--!>` allowed, a weaker look-behind for path candidates, a cap of seven paths, the filter on `..` segments off.
